        "markdown": "[Naples](https://naplespompeii.com/ \"Naples\") / _Transport_\n\n_All info about the Naples metro lines, ferries, trains, hop on hop off busses and public transport tickets in the center of Napoli._\n\n## Naples Public Transport\n\nMany [Naples sights](https://naplespompeii.com/things-to-do-in-naples.html \"Naples sights\") are located in the historic center of Naples (Napoli), but also outside the center are a number of important highlights. Naples' transport options at a glance:\n\n1. [Metro](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#1 \"Metro Naples\")\n2. [Funicolare (cable cars)](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#2 \"funicular\")\n3. [Trains](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#3 \"Naples trains\")\n4. [Hop on hop off bus](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#4 \"bus Naples\")\n5. [To and from airport](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#5 \"Naples airport\")\n6. [Ferries](https://naplespompeii.com/transport.html#6 \"Ferries Naples\")\n\n![Metro Naples](https://naplespompeii.com/images/metro-naples.jpg)_The comfortable metro in Naples_\n\n![Metro Napoli](https://naplespompeii.com/images/napoli-italy.jpg)_The beautiful Toledo metro station, a landmark of line 1_\n\n## 1\\. Naples Metro\n\nThe Naples metro network consists firstly of **two metro lines** of ANM (Azienda Napoletana Mobilità ), namely the most used and central yellow line 1, in addition there is also a purple line 6 which you probably won't use. In addition, the network has **four suburban trains** of different companies, namely lines 2 to 5. Line 2 in particular has a connection to metro line 1 and stops near several [places of interest](https://naplespompeii.com/things-to-do-in-naples.html \"Naples places to visit\").\n\n- Metro line 1 runs every 14 minutes between 6:00 am and 10:30 pm\n- You can buy tickets in the metro stations at a ticket machine or a manned ticket counter. You can choose the following **metro tickets** when you stay within the city (within the white part of the metro map below):\n  - **Biglietto corsa singola**: metro ticket valid for one journey on one ANM vehicle (not in line 2 to 5) Price: 1.30 euros.\n  - **Biglietto a tempo valido 90 minuti (biglietti intergrati TIC)**: metro ticket 90 minutes valid for one or more rides, also of different companies and the funiculars (funicolari). Price: 1.70 euros\n  - **Biglietto giornaliero (biglietti intergrati TIC)**: day ticket, unlimited number of rides until midnight, also with different companies and the funicolari. Price: 5.10 euros\n\nBelow you will find a link to the Moovit route planner:\n\n[Route planner Moovit](https://moovitapp.com/napoli_e_campania-882/poi/nl?utm_source=widget&o=1 \"Metro Naples\")\n\nBelow you will find a link to the detailed map of the Napoli metro and trains:\n\n[Naples metro map](https://www.anm.it/images/stories/anm_mappa_web_2021__5.pdf \"Naples metro map\")\n\n[![Metro Napoli](https://www.anm.it/sfsites/c/resource/MappaReteSuFerro)](https://www.anm.it/images/stories/anm_mappa_web_2021__5.pdf)\n\n## 2\\. Funicolare (cable cars)\n\nThe metro map above also shows four orange lines of the ' **Funicolare**', or the cable cars of Naples with a total of 14 stations that are ideal for getting on top of the hills. These funiculars are operated by the transport company ANM, which also manages metro line 1. In particular, the ' _Funicolare di Montesanto_' is widely used by tourists to visit the [Castel Sant' Elmo](https://naplespompeii.com/castelsantelmo.html \"Castel Sant Elmo\") and the [Monastery San Martino](https://naplespompeii.com/san-martino.html \"Monastery San Martino Naples\"). You can easily buy tickets for the funicolare from the machines at the station of the funicular. You can choose from the same tickets as described above for the metro.\n\n## 3\\. Trains outside the city\n\nSeveral trains depart from Piazza Garibaldi (Central Station). To book tickets to cities further away such as [Rome](https://romesite.com/ \"Rome\"), Salerno and Caserta it is better to use the [Trenitalia route planner](https://www.trenitalia.com/it.html). To visit the top attractions [Pompeii](https://naplespompeii.com/pompeii.html \"pompeii italy\"), [Herculaneum](https://naplespompeii.com/herculaneum.html \"Herculaneum\") and the [Vesuvius](https://naplespompeii.com/mount-vesuvius.html \"Vesuvius Naples\") you are dependent on the Circumvesuviana trains towards [Sorrento](https://naplespompeii.com/sorrento-italy.html \"What to do in Sorrento\"). In the articles below we tell you exactly how you can visit these attractions by train from Napoli. For other destinations, we recommend using the Moovit route planner below.\n\n- [How do I travel to Pompeii?](https://naplespompeii.com/pompeii.html \"Pompeii from Naples\")\n- [How do I travel to Herculaneum?](https://naplespompeii.com/herculaneum.html \"Herculaneum visit\")\n- [How do I travel to Mount Vesuvius?](https://naplespompeii.com/mount-vesuvius.html \"Vesuvius from Naples\")\n- [Train tickets Naples to Sorrento](https://www.headout.com/train-tickets/one-way-campania-express-tickets-naples-to-from-sorrento-e-24594/?affiliate_code=gSvW4d&utm_campaign=naples&utm_content=link&utm_medium=affiliate&utm_source=bwdtravelguides)\n\nBelow you will find a link to the Moovit route planner for other destinations:\n\n[Route planner Moovit](https://moovitapp.com/napoli_e_campania-882/poi/nl?utm_source=widget&o=1 \"metro lines Naples\")\n\n![public transport naples](https://naplespompeii.com/images/funicolare-di-montesanto-napoli.jpg)_The Castel Sant'Elmo is accessible by the 'Funicolare di Montesanto'_\n\n![Bus Naples](https://naplespompeii.com/images/hop-on-hop-off-bus-naples.jpg)_The Naples Hop on Hop off bus is an easy way to explore the city_\n\n## 4\\. You can show your online purchased tickets on your mobile on the bus.\n\n- Line A (Places of the Art): 45-minute route along the highlights of the historic center\n- Line B (Views of the Gulf): 70 minute route along the coast towards Posillipo\n- [Map routes](https://ops.isango.com/echo/Files/City%20Sightseeing%20Napoli%20-%20Map.pdf)\n\n## 5\\. To and from Naples airport\n\nDo you want to go from the airport to the center of Naples or vice versa? Because the airport is only 6 kilometers from the city center, you can reach the center of Naples within fifteen minutes by bus, taxi or private transfer. [Read all about the transport options from the airport in this article](https://naplespompeii.com/airport-transfer-naples.html \"Naples airport\"):\n\n[Naples Airport Transportation](https://naplespompeii.com/airport-transfer-naples.html \"Naples Airport Transportation\")\n\n## 6\\. Visiting islands by ferry\n\n![ferry Naples](https://naplespompeii.com/images/naples-ferry.jpg)_A ferry departing from Naples_\n\n![Procida ferry](https://naplespompeii.com/images/isola-di-procida.jpg)_Procida is one of the beautiful islands that you can visit by ferry_\n\nFrom the port of Naples, 39 ferries depart daily, mainly to the islands off the coast of Naples, but also to further destinations such as Sicily. In the article about the [Naples area](https://naplespompeii.com/excursions-from-naples.html \"Naples area\") you can read more about day trips in the Naples area, such as the beautiful islands **Capri**, **Procida** and **Ischia** which are all within one hour ride by ferry.         "title": "How to use Public transport in Naples while saving money",
        "description": "# How to use Public transport in Naples while saving money\n## How to go around Naples with public transport\nThe **public transportation system of Naples** is operated by two companies and includes **city buses**, **funicular lines**, **regional buses**, **Metro Lines 1**, **2** and **6**, **Circumvesuviana**, **Circumfegrea** and **Cumana train lines**.\n\nYou can travel with all of these within the city limit, using a Unico ticket or TIC ticket.\n\nThere are also ticket to reach specific zones of the province of Naples, they are valid in the city too.\n\nWhat kind of ticket i have to buy?\n\nHourly, daily, weekly and monthly tickets can be purchased at tobacco shop (tabaccheria) or newspaper stands. There are ticket machines at many of the railway, metro and cable railway stations as well as at selected bus stops throughout the city.\n\nIf you are on holiday in Naples, we recommend the purchase of **Naples Pass**, the official digital tourist card of Naples.\n\nWith Naples Pass you have the opportunity to upgrade your package by purchasing **unlimited access to transportation for 3 or 7 days**, possibility to purchase special routes in advance (Pompeii, Herculaneum, Caserta), possibility to purchase transfers to and from Capodichino Airport in advance, as well as discounts in museums and activities around the city and free pizza!\n\n[Find out more about Naples Pass❯](https://www.naplespass.eu/)\n\n## First thing: Taxi or bus in Naples?\nYou just landed in Naples, don't have a mountain of luggage or you are not in a big group with which you could split the taxi cost. **Take the special bus from the airport!** You don't have to book before and **you can buy the tickets directly on the board**. Just in front of the airport, there is a bus stop which is bringing you for only 4 euros **to the central train station, port or the main square**. The taxi costs from about 20 euros fixed tariff and unless you're not in a big group or having too heavy luggage I'd suggest that you take a bus instead.\n\nThere are two bus lines from Capodichino airport to the city center::\n\n- 3S line is an urban bus with several busstops around the city, including one in Capodichino Airport, one in the Central Station and one near Piazza Municipio.\n- Alibus is a shuttle bus that only goes from Capodichino Airport to the city center, without other busstops. Alibus line has departures every 20 minutes.\n\nIt takes about 15 minutes to reach Naples city center from Capodichino without traffic.\n\nIf you like to plan everything in advance and enjoy your trip in complete relaxation, we recommend you **secure your Alibus ticket in advance** by purchasing [**Naples Pass**](https://www.naplespass.eu/).\n\nHere you can download Alibus Schedules:\n\n- [ALIBUS from Capodichino Airport to Central Station (p. Garibaldi)](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/1.capodichino%202015.pdf)\n- [ALIBUS from Via Arenaccia to Central Station (p. Garibaldi)](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/2.arenaccia%20andata%202015.pdf)\n- [ALIBUS from Central Station (p. Garibaldi) to p.zza Municipio](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/3.piazza%20garibaldi%20porto%202015.pdf)\n- [ALIBUS from p.zza Municipio to Central Station (p. Garibaldi)](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/4.porto%20%20municipio%202015.pdf)\n- [ALIBUS from Central Station (p. Garibaldi) to Capodichino Airport](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/5.piazza%20garibaldi%20aeroporto%202015.pdf)\n- [ALIBUS from piazza Nazionale to Capodichino Airport](http://www.anm.it/images/stories/PDF/alibus/6.nazionale%202015.pdf)\n\n## Take the Naples metro\nNot only you can visit, according to BBC, **the most beautiful metro stop in Europe: \"Toledo\"**, but also it's a 1 euro cheap way to give your feet a break. If you think you will move a lot with the public transport inside the city I suggest that you **take a 3,50 euro daily** or **12 euro weekly ticket**. **All the public transport**, like bus, metro, and funicular, which move inside the Naples are included in the ticket.\n\n## Visit Hercolaneum, Pompeii and Sorrento with public transport\nOne of the coolest things about Naples is that you can travel to some other very cool places like **Herculaneum, Pompeii or Sorrento** super cheap and you're not obligated to look for the special buses or book anything, an easier and cheaper way exists. There is a train that leaves right from Garibaldi central train station. It's called \" **Circumvesuviana**\". You can get the tickets near the entry and the price is only from 2 to 3 euros, depending on where you want to go. And it takes only **about 20 minutes to go to Herculaneum**, **40 minutes to Pompeii** and **1 hour to Sorrento**.\n\nHowever, again, if you **prefer to plan** without having to worry about anything, **the only way to secure your tickets in advance** is to buy a **tourist pass**. This is definitely the best option if your trip is short and if you have never been to Naples before because it **saves you time**. With **[Naples Pass](https://www.naplespass.eu/)** you can **buy in advance** the round trip to **Pompeii**, **Herculaneum** and **Caserta**.\n\nHope you'll find these **tips useful for your journey in Naples**. Use public transport in the cheaper way and use the money you save for something more exciting.\n\nSo, use the public transport, save money and **visit Naples**.\n\n## The Naples Pass: one card in Naples for transport and museums\n![20181031173849Card trasporti musei Napoli.jpg](https://images.marketing-italia.eu/uploads/visitnaples/uploads_articoli/paragrafo/20181031173849Card%20trasporti%20musei%20Napoli.jpg)\n\nDuring your trip you will need to use public transport and you will definitely visit the main attractions of Naples. A smart way for tourists to do both is to buy the **Naples Pass**.\n\nThe Naples Pass is a **[card in Naples for transport and museums](http://www.naplespass.eu/en/)**: Naples Pass can offer you a **season ticket for Naples public transport** along with discounts and reductions in **the main museums and attractions of Naples**, but also in many restaurants, pizzerias and partner activities.\n\nThe Naples Pass can be purchased before departure and activated once you arrive in Naples.         "title": "Naples Transport Pass | Metro, Tram & Bus",
        "description": "# Naples\nItaly\n\nWhere ancient volcanic history, street food culture, and Mediterranean energy converge.\n\nGet easy access to:\n\nMetro, Bus, Tram & Funicular\n\nConetpass is a digital public transport pass offering, in Naples, two-trip and 1-day, 2-day and 3-day options, valid on all ANM services: metro (Line 1, Line 2), funiculars, tram, and bus. Combo options include access to the Archaeological site of Pompeii and a skip-the-line ticket for Naples Underground. <br />\n\nAll day passes include a one-way airport shuttle to/from Naples Capodichino Airport, bike sharing via Uber Lime, and a €5 taxi credit. 100% digital, no pickup required.\n\n## Choose Your Conet Pass Naples\nGet access to local transport\n\n## Transport Network\nNaples’ public transport network is operated by ANM. The system includes 2 metro lines, 3 funicular lines, multiple tram and bus routes, and an airport shuttle service covering the entire city and key tourist areas. The ConetPass Naples gives you unlimited access to all ANM operated services on a single digital pass. The network runs from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM daily.\n\n### Bus Network\nNaples' bus network is operated by ANM and covers the entire city, including areas not served by metro, funicular, or tram. Bus routes run from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM. Night services operate on key routes after 11:00 PM. The same ticket valid on metro and tram is also valid on bus. Validate your ticket immediately upon boarding using the onboard validation machine. For route planning and real-time arrivals, use the Moovit app or the ANM official website.\n\n### Tram Network\nNaples operates tram services connecting the city center to coastal and residential neighborhoods along the seafront and eastern districts. Trams run from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM. The same ticket valid on metro and bus is also valid on tram. For real-time routes and schedules, use the Moovit app or the ANM official website.\n\n![Naples transport network map](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Naples-Public-Transport-Map-ANM-1.jpg)\n\nNaples Transport Network Map\n\n## Exclusive Benefits in Naples\n### 10% Discount on FlixBus Tickets in Italy\nFlixBus is a European intercity bus operator offering low-cost travel across an extensive network of routes in Italy and abroad.\n\nConetPass holders receive a 10% discount code redeemable on FlixBus intercity routes across the national network in Italy.\n\nREWARDS PARTNER\n\n[![Miles & More logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/mam_logo_partner_en_rgb_black.jpg)](https://miles-and-more.qiibee.com/partner/conetpass/)\n\nMiles & More\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions\n### Which transports are included in my travel card?\n- All tram lines (comprehensive city coverage)\n- All local buses (extensive network)\n- All funicular lines (Centrale, Montesanto, Chiaia to Vomero district)\n- Airport shuttle to/from Naples Capodichino Airport (one-way)\n\n### Which transport services are NOT included in Conetpass Naples?\n- Circumvesuviana (EAV railway to Pompeii, Herculaneum, and Sorrento) — not operated by ANM\n- Ferries and hydrofoils to Capri, Ischia, and Procida — not included, regardless of departure point (Molo Beverello or Mergellina)\n- Trenitalia regional trains (e.g. Naples–Salerno, Naples–Caserta)\n- Italo and Frecciarossa high-speed trains\n- Private taxi and ride-hailing services ( **a separate €5 Wetaxi credit is included but is not unlimited transport**)\n\n## Ready to Explore Naples?\nBuy your Naples transport pass valid on all ANM metro, tram, bus, and funicular lines. Includes airport shuttle, bike sharing, and a €5 taxi credit. 100% digital — activate on your phone, no queues, no pickup.\n\n[Choose Your Pass](https://conetpass.com/cities/naples/#products)",

Transport Network\n\nNaples’ public transport network is operated by ANM. The system includes 2 metro lines, 3 funicular lines, multiple tram and bus routes, and an airport shuttle service covering the entire city and key tourist areas. The ConetPass Naples gives you unlimited access to all ANM operated services on a single digital pass. The network runs from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM daily.\n\n### Metro Lines\n\nLine 1 (Yellow) — Piscinola → Garibaldi. Key stops: Piscinola, Piscinola, Municipio, Università, Garibaldi (Central Station). Connects the northern suburbs to the city center and Piazza Garibaldi.\nLine 2 (Blue) — Pozzuoli → Gianturco. Key stops: Pozzuoli, Bagnoli, Campi Flegrei, Mergellina, Montesanto, Piazza Garibaldi, Gianturco. Connects the Phlegrean Fields area to the city center and eastern districts. Also used to reach Ercolano (20 minutes) and as a connection point for Pompeii via Circumvesuviana.\n\n### Bus Network\n\nNaples' bus network is operated by ANM and covers the entire city, including areas not served by metro, funicular, or tram. Bus routes run from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM. Night services operate on key routes after 11:00 PM. The same ticket valid on metro and tram is also valid on bus. Validate your ticket immediately upon boarding using the onboard validation machine. For route planning and real-time arrivals, use the Moovit app or the ANM official website.\n\n### Tram Network\n\nNaples operates tram services connecting the city center to coastal and residential neighborhoods along the seafront and eastern districts. Trams run from approximately 06:00 AM to 11:00 PM. The same ticket valid on metro and bus is also valid on tram. For real-time routes and schedules, use the Moovit app or the ANM official website.\n\n![Naples transport network map](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Naples-Public-Transport-Map-ANM-1.jpg)\n\nNaples Transport Network Map\n\n## Exclusive Benefits in Naples\n\nIncluded with your ConetPass — automatically.\n\nFREE\n\n[![Ubigi logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Ubigi-logo-1.png)](https://www.ubigi.com/)\n\nUbigi\n\n### Free Ubigi eSIM with 500 MB preloaded\n\nUbigi is a global provider of cellular data connectivity via eSIM for mobile devices and connected cars.\n\nFree Ubigi eSIM with 500 MB preloaded. You will receive the eSIM directly in the confirmation email, together with your ConetPass. You will only need to activate it on Ubigi’s website, our partner.\n\nExclusive discount\n\n[![Tiqets logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/tiqets.png)](https://www.tiqets.com/)\n\nTiqets\n\n### 10% OFF\n\nTiqets is an online platform that sells digital tickets for museums, attractions, and cultural experiences worldwide.\n\nThanks to our partnership with Tiqets, your Card unlocks 10% off tickets to the city’s most famous attractions. Make the most of your visit while enjoying exclusive savings.\n\nExclusive discount\n\n[![Stow Your Bags logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Stow-Your-Bags.png)](https://www.stowyourbags.com/)\n\nStow Your Bags\n\n### 10% OFF\n\nStow Your Bags offers self-service lockers for storing your luggage securely in various cities across Europe.\n\nWith Conet Pass, you get 10% off luggage storage services, so you can explore the city hands-free and enjoy your visit without carrying your bags.\n\n€5 VOUCHER\n\n[![Wetaxi logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/WeTaxi-logo.png)](https://www.site.wetaxi.it/)\n\nWetaxi\n\n### €5 Off a Wetaxi Ride\n\nWetaxi is an application providing mobility services across various Italian cities. It allows users to book a taxi with a guaranteed upfront price and pay for parking in over 300 locations.\n\nConetPass users receive a €5 discount on a single Wetaxi taxi ride booked through the Wetaxi app.\n\nExclusive discount\n\n[![Flixbus logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Flixbus-logo.png)](https://www.flixbus.it/)\n\nFlixbus\n\n### 10% Discount on FlixBus Tickets in Italy\n\nFlixBus is a European intercity bus operator offering low-cost travel across an extensive network of routes in Italy and abroad.\n\nConetPass holders receive a 10% discount code redeemable on FlixBus intercity routes across the national network in Italy.\n\nREWARDS PARTNER\n\n[![Miles & More logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/mam_logo_partner_en_rgb_black.jpg)](https://miles-and-more.qiibee.com/partner/conetpass/)\n\nMiles & More\n\n### Earn Miles & More Miles\n\nMiles & More is the loyalty programme of the Lufthansa Group\n\nThanks to this partnership, ConetPass customers earn 10 Miles & More miles for each day of their Conet Pass.\n\n[View all exclusive benefits →](https://conetpass.com/membership/)\n\n## Integrated Mobility Partners\n\n[![Moovit Logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Moovit-Logo.png)\\\\\n\\\\\nMoovit is a leading Mobility-as-a-Service (MaaS) app and journey planner, owned by Mobileye (Intel), providing real-time public transit info worldwide.](https://moovitapp.com/) [![Uber Logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Uber-Logo.png)\\\\\n\\\\\nUber is a global ride-hailing platform operating in 70+ countries, connecting passengers with drivers on demand via a mobile app.](https://www.uber.com/) [![Ubigi logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Ubigi-logo-1.png)\\\\\n\\\\\nUbigi is an international mobile connectivity provider that offers eSIM-based data plans for travelers across multiple countries worldwide.](https://www.ubigi.com/) [![WeTaxi Logo](https://conetpass.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/WeTaxi-logo.png)\\\\\n\\\\\nWetaxi is an application providing mobility services across various Italian cities. It allows users to book a taxi with a guaranteed upfront price and pay for parking in over 300 locations.](https://www.site.wetaxi.it/)\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions\n\n### What is Conetpass Naples?\n\nConetpass, the digital travel pass managed by Conet, operates also in Naples where its included access to every local transport with the technical support of Wetaxi app. The pass gives unlimited access to all ANM urban services:\n\n- Metro (Line 1, Line 2)\n- Tram\n- Bus\n- Funiculars\n- One-way airport shuttle to/from Naples Capodichino Airport\n\nAvailable for 1, 2, or 3 consecutive calendar days. 100% digital, no physical pickup required.\n\nConetPass is not operated by ANM directly.\n\n### Which transports are included in my travel card?\n\nYour Conetpass Naples travel card provides unlimited access to the entire public transport network in Naples, which includes:\n\n- All metro lines (2 lines: Line 1 and Line 2)\n- All tram lines (comprehensive city coverage)\n- All local buses (extensive network)\n- All funicular lines (Centrale, Montesanto, Chiaia to Vomero district)\n- Airport shuttle to/from Naples Capodichino Airport (one-way)\n\n### How do I activate my Conetpass Naples?\n\nYour Conetpass will be a 100% digital ticket sent by email after purchase. Please follow the instructions you will receive by email for activation.\n\n### Which transport services are NOT included in Conetpass Naples?\n\nConetpass Naples covers all ANM urban services only. The following are not included:\n\n- Circumvesuviana (EAV railway to Pompeii, Herculaneum, and Sorrento) — not operated by ANM\n- Ferries and hydrofoils to Capri, Ischia, and Procida — not included, regardless of departure point (Molo Beverello or Mergellina)\n- Trenitalia regional trains (e.g. Naples–Salerno, Naples–Caserta)\n- Italo and Frecciarossa high-speed trains\n- Private taxi and ride-hailing services ( **a separate €5 Wetaxi credit is included but is not unlimited transport**)\n\nIf a service is not listed under “What’s Included” in your booking confirmation, assume it is not covered by the pass.\n\n### When does my Conetpass Naples ticket validity start?\n\nYour Conetpass Naples ticket validity starts as soon as you activate the ticket in the WeTaxi app. No physical validation on board is required. Activate it only when you are ready to travel, as validity begins immediately. Naples passes are available as one way, 1-day, 2-day, or 3- day options, and multi-day passes are calculated on a calendar-day basis.\n\n- **The two-trip ticket (90 minutes)** is valid for exactly 90 minutes from the moment of first validation and allows two separate journeys within that time window.\n- **The 1-day ticket** is valid for one calendar day, until midnight on the day of activation.\n- **The 2-day ticket** is valid for two consecutive calendar days. For example, if you first use it on Monday at 5:00 PM, it remains valid until Tuesday at midnight.\n- **The 3-day ticket** is valid for three consecutive calendar days. For example, if you first use it on Monday at 5:00 PM, it remains valid until Wednesday at midnight.\n\n### Can I use my airport shuttle ticket outside the validity period of my pass?\n\nYes. Your airport shuttle ticket is an open-date ticket and can be used whenever you want, independent of your public transport card’s validity period. The airport shuttle is separate from your main transport pass, so you can use it before, during, or after your card’s active days.\n\n### Can I use the airport shuttle ticket either direction (airport to Naples or Naples to airport)?\n\nYes. Although your ticket may show a specific route, you can use it in either direction:\n\n- From the airport to Naples, OR\n- From Naples to the airport\n\nYou decide which direction when you use it.\n\n### Where can I find information about the airport shuttle stops and timetable?\n\nNaples Airport is served by the Alibus shuttle service operated by ANM.\n\n- **Service details**:\n\nBus name/operator: Alibus (operated by ANM)\n- **Stop locations**:\n\nAt Naples Capodichino Airport:\n\nMain bus stop located outside the Arrivals area\n\nIn Naples (three main stops):\n\n- **Piazza Garibaldi (Central Station) — main transport hub**\n- **Varco Immacolatella / Porta di Massa (port area entrance)**\n- **Molo Angioino / Beverello (Maritime Station)**\n\n**Schedule:**\n\n- Operates daily from approximately 5:30 AM to midnight\n- Frequency: approximately every 20–30 minutes\n- Journey time: approximately 20–30 minutes (depending on traffic)\n\n**Route (from airport to city)**:\n\nCapodichino Airport → Piazza Garibaldi (Central Station) → Varco Immacolatella / Porta di Massa → Molo Angioino / Beverello (Maritime Station)\n\n**Return route (from city to airport)**:\n\nMolo Angioino / Beverello → Varco Immacolatella / Porta di Massa → Varco Pisacane (opposite Port Authority) → Piazza Garibaldi → Capodichino Airport\n\nFor detailed information: [Alibus shuttle](https://www.anm.it/s/alibus?language=en_US)\n\n### Which Naples Metro stations are most useful for tourists?\n\nThe most tourist-relevant metro stations in Naples are served by Line 1 (Yellow) and Line 2 (Blue).\n\n**Line 1 (Yellow) — key stops for tourists**:\n\n- **Garibaldi**: Naples Central Station (Napoli Centrale); main arrival and departure hub for trains and the Alibus airport shuttle.\n- **Università**: historic city center, close to Spaccanapoli and the main pedestrian areas.\n- **Toledo**: considered one of the most architecturally striking metro stations in Europe; close to Via Toledo shopping street and the Royal Palace.\n- **Municipio**: Piazza Municipio, Castel Nuovo, and the port area (Molo Beverello) for ferry connections to Capri, Ischia, and Procida.\n- **Dante**: Piazza Dante and the historic center (Centro Storico).\n\n**Line 2 (Blue) — key stops for tourists**:\n\n- **Mergellina**: seafront promenade, harbor, and ferry connections.\n- **Montesanto**: interchange with the Montesanto funicular to Vomero.\n- **Campi Flegrei**: access point for the Phlegrean Fields archaeological area.\n\n**Funicular interchange**: to reach the Vomero hill district (Castel Sant’Elmo, Certosa di San Martino), use the Centrale, Chiaia, or Montesanto funiculars — all included in the ConetPass Naples.\n\n### What are the tram and bus operating hours in Naples?\n\n**Trams**:\n\n- Operate daily from 6:00 AM to 11:00 PM\n- Frequent service connecting city center to outlying neighborhoods\n- Connect historic and tourist areas\n\n**Buses**:\n\n- Operate daily from 6:00 AM to 11:00 PM\n- Extensive network covering city neighborhoods and suburban areas\n- Connect residential districts with major transport hubs and attractions\n\nBoth services offer reliable connections to main points of interest, residential neighborhoods, and transport interchanges.\n\n### What are the funicular operating hours in Naples?\n\nNaples has three funicular lines serving the Vomero district. Operating hours vary by line:\n\n**Funicolare Centrale (Central Funicular)**:\n\n- Monday–Tuesday: 7:00 AM to 10:30 PM\n- Wednesday, Thursday, Sunday: 7:00 AM to 12:30 AM (following day)\n- Friday–Saturday: 7:00 AM to 2:00 AM (following day)\n\n**Funicolare Montesanto**:\n\n- Daily: 7:00 AM to 10:00 PM\n\n**Funicolare Chiaia**:\n\n- Daily: 7:00 AM to 10:00 PM\n\nAll three funiculars climb the steep Vomero hills, offering scenic panoramic routes to the Vomero district and surrounding neighborhoods.\n\n### How can I book tours at a reduced rate?\n\nEach Conetpass includes a **“Special Promotions” section** with a unique promocode.\n\nUse this code when booking directly on tour operators’ websites to receive discounts up to 25% — the exact percentage varies by tour operator.\n\nFind your promocode in the email titled “Your Pass is Ready” sent after your booking confirmation.\n\n### Are there discounts for children, seniors, groups, or families?\n\nAt this time, Conetpass does not offer promotional discounts for children, seniors, groups, or families. All customers pay the same price.\n\nHowever, young children can travel free on Naples public transport under certain age conditions (see next FAQ).\n\n### What is the age limit for children to travel free on Naples public transport?\n\nChildren aged 5 and under travel free of charge on all Naples public transport (metro, trams, buses, and funiculars) when accompanied by a paying adult.\n\nFor individuals aged 6 and over: a separate ticket must be purchased.\n\nNote: free travel policies for children are standard for all tickets, including those purchased through Conetpass.\n\n### How do I get and use the Uber-Lime bike credit included with Conetpass Naples?\n\nYour Conetpass Naples includes an Uber-Lime promo code. To use it, follow the instructions in your confirmation email.\n\n## Ready to Explore Naples?\n\nBuy your Naples transport pass valid on all ANM metro, tram, bus, and funicular lines. Includes airport shuttle, bike sharing, and a €5 taxi credit. 100% digital — activate on your phone, no queues, no pickup.\n\n[Choose Your Pass](https://conetpass.com/cities/naples/#products)",

        "url": "https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/",
        "title": "Bus Travel in Italy: What You Need to Know",
        "description": "# Bus Travel in Italy: What You Need to Know\n## Bus Travel from One Italian Region to Another\n- **[SENA Autolinee](http://www.sena.it/Home/78-1-en.html)** (English option) – SENA is a Tuscan bus comapny that has partnered with Baltour, another Italian bus company. Together, they’ve also partnered with Eurolines (a European coach network – more about them in the next section). They’re the closest thing to a national bus network Italy has, and you can actually search bus schedules on their site for 17 of Italy’s 20 regions. They also offer a couple of bus discount passes – the ItalyBus Card, a €30 card that grants you 15% off tickets in the SENA/Baltour network; and the Italy Pass, which is like a Eurail Pass for SENA/Baltour buses. Plus, they sometimes offer spectacular €1 ticket prices if you book online in advance. All that said, their network doesn’t cover the country, and sometimes there’s only one city served within a region. So this is definitely an option worth considering if you’re on a budget and you can book tickets ahead of time for a whopping €1, but I’d suggest checking all your potential routes on their site beforehand to make sure SENA/Baltour go where you need them to go.\n- **[SAIS Autolinee](http://www.saisautolinee.it/default.htm)** (site only in Italian) – In addition to operating the city buses in one Sicilian town and regional buses throughout Sicily, this company also has [some national routes](http://www.saisautolinee.it/nazionali.htm). Cities connected via SAIS Autolinee include Palermo, Agrigento, Pescara, Ancona, Urbino, Perugia, Florence, Pisa, La Spezia, Genoa, Siena, Bologna, Parma, Piacenza, Milan, Turin, Padua, Venice, and Verona. The schedule is limited, so if this is your transport of choice be absolutely sure you know when and where to catch the bus.\n- **[SAIS Trasporti](http://www.saistrasporti.it/)** (English option) – Not to be confused with SAIS Autolinee, SAIS Trasporti has its own set of national bus routes in Italy. (The logos are similar. There’s probably a connection. But this is Italy – one never knows.) This company also has a Sicilian concentration of destinations, and many of their routes are a combination of train travel and bus travel (although you buy both in one transaction). Still, some are all buses and reasonably priced.\n\n## Bus Travel Between Italy and Another Country\n[![Eurolines bus | creative commons photo by Sludge G](http://italyexplained.com/wp-content/uploads/2014/10/bustravel_eurolines-e1413152083577.jpg)](https://www.flickr.com/photos/sludgeulper/4579710192)\n\nEurolines bus || creative commons photo by Sludge G\n\nYou’ve probably heard of Eurail passes, right? Those train ticket substitutes that backpack-wielding teenagers and twenty-somethings have been toting around Europe for decades? Well, there are bus companies that act sort of like European trains in that regard, ferrying backpackers around the continent in an oversized hop-on/hop-off tour. Companies like [Eurolines](http://eurolines.com/en/) and [Busabout](http://www.busabout.com/) offer organized tours or simply long-haul coach (bus) transit from one major European city to another. These options may include multiple stops in Italy, so if you’re on a trip through several countries in Europe this is one option that could get you from one Italian region to another by bus. It isn’t, however, an Italian bus network.\n\n## Bus Travel Within Italian Cities\n[![City bus in Florence | creative commons photo by Chris Sampson](http://italyexplained.com/wp-content/uploads/2014/10/bustravel_city-e1413152138742.jpg)](https://www.flickr.com/photos/lodekka/8279187192)\n\nCity bus in Florence || creative commons photo by Chris Sampson\n\nMany Italian cities have city buses that are excellent options for getting around. Some of the city bus networks are listed on [the Bus Station website](http://www.busstation.net/index.phpeurope/westerneurope/italy/) (discussed earlier), but you may also need to stop in at the tourist information office when you arrive to find out the best way to get around. Tickets for city buses are often sold at tabacco shops (they’re indicated by a big T hanging outside the door) and newsstands, but not usually on the buses themselves. And don’t forget to validate your ticket when you first get on the bus – in some places, not doing so can result in hefty fines.\n\nPost Button\n\n### 76 responses to “Bus Travel in Italy: What You Need to Know”\n1. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/bd9b4e17eb41f6ba813cc0d22498a742b305034813a56b14e06a77d41dd73ba8?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Ricksays: [25 November 2015 at 03:54](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-10950) Hi — great info on your site. I’m in an out of the way place, and am going to try to pick up a bus on a road (not close to any station). Can you buy a ticket on the bus? And, it looks like from the Italian bus website that you have to pay 7 Euros each time you transfer…in your experience, is that correct? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=10950#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [25 November 2015 at 08:26](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-10957) Hi, Rick: Yes, you can usually buy a ticket on the bus. It’s best if you have small bills so you can make exact change in that case. And I haven’t heard of paying for a transfer – what you might be paying for is a new ticket with a different company, since they wouldn’t have the same tickets or share revenue. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=10957#respond)\n2. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/3a726baaf1cf29422d2bb9a17489ecf3e14b54ae079e0a2cb76dfcc1fac0db58?s=32&d=blank&r=g)TOYsays: [27 February 2016 at 13:02](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-14722) Hi!! So glad that I came across your site! Have been researching about the Italy Pass but there isn’t much info about their routes and how it works etc etc.. So if you’d be so kind to help me solve my problems here: [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=14722#respond)\n   1. How does the Italy Pass work? Can I use it on any SENA/Baltour bus, anytime without limit?\n   2. Does the pass allow me to go from regions to regions any easier, by any chance?\n   3. would you recommend buying the Italy Pass, Interrail Pass or simply reserving tickets from point to point? FYI, we’ll be travelling to Italy this summer for about 2 weeks, and we’re hoping to cover every corner of Italy, at least most of it.. 😀\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [29 February 2016 at 21:05](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-14750) Do you know, I’d never even heard of a pass for bus travel in Italy! Most bus tickets are inexpensive enough, it didn’t even seem like something to look up. I see one that covers a number of bus lines within Italy, including SENA and Baltour, which it sounds like is the one you were looking at. As with a rail pass, however, I’d suggest looking up actual bus ticket prices on the routes you’d be traveling, add them up, & compare that to the price of the bus pass. It may offer some savings, but it may not – sometimes buying individual train tickets is much cheaper than a rail pass. As for the ease of movement from region to region, I wouldn’t think so – it’s the same bus network you’d be using, just with a pass instead of tickets. The pass doesn’t change inter-regional bus travel. Now, when you ask about buying this bus pas or an Interrail Pass, I’m confused – Interrail is train travel. If you’re asking whether you should travel by train or bus, my suggestion is train nearly all of the time. Some trips are easier/better with a bus, but most of the time the train wins in my book (no pun intended, although I did literally write a book on Italian train travel). I hope that helps! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=14750#respond)\n3. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/2927dec2da3b1b186d53fab5dda80acd8730b77722df69e84ab1e16c5bf0fa2f?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Mick Shorthosesays: [10 May 2016 at 05:36](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-16963) Thank for the information on your site,wish I had found it earlier,However you might be able to help with one question.We have found the bus service we want(autolinee marino)with a return trip from Naples to Matera.We have emailed the service with a couple of questions and have had a response but we cannot seem to get a response to this one.Do they have toilets on the coach or stop at a predesignated place for the toilet.The journey we want to book is about 4.5 hours so you can see the reason for the question,I am sure we are not the only ones asking.Thanks. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=16963#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [10 May 2016 at 13:13](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-16973) Thanks for the note, Mick! The Marino website has a [page about their bus fleet](http://www.marinobus.it/en/bus_fleet.htm), which lists all of the amenities on board – including toilets. 🙂 [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=16973#respond)\n     - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/2927dec2da3b1b186d53fab5dda80acd8730b77722df69e84ab1e16c5bf0fa2f?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Mick Shorthosesays: [10 May 2016 at 15:19](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-16976) Thanks Jessica,That was quick.It`s knowing where to look for the information you need,That`s the problem.Anyway thanks again,there will be no damaged bladders on our trip!! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=16976#respond)\n       - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [11 May 2016 at 14:11](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-17016) Haha! Sometimes it takes a little creative searching… Finding not where _you_ might put information, but where someone else might store it. I’m glad I could help! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=17016#respond)\n4. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/3d26de34175f599a01264ed5b6ef949a216b57c5e986050cb8966744d7bc5e61?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Anne Geoghegan](http://eircom.net/)says: [1 August 2016 at 09:55](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-19739) Hi I am just wondering the best way to get to Pescheria Del Garda from Bergamo Airport. As I will not be driving can anybody advise please. Thanks Aine [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=19739#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [1 August 2016 at 13:32](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-19742) It looks like there’s a [bus from the Bergamo Airport (BGY) to Peschiera del Garda](https://www.rome2rio.com/s/Milan-Bergamo-Airport-BGY/Peschiera-del-Garda), but you should probably also check how long it would take to get a bus from the airport into the city of Bergamo and then, from there, the train to Peschiera del Garda. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=19742#respond)\n5. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/841e802abba85b9586532f6fa9eafe621af64629f0840fc64e5308a7b320506a?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Adamsays: [14 August 2016 at 02:09](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20029) Love your site, as this is my first time going to Italy. Is there a kind of bus and train card that is valid for 6 days?(Im staying there for 1 week) I need a card that works on both busses and trains. Where do I buy that and how much does it cost me? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20029#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [14 August 2016 at 17:49](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20052) Thanks for the question, Adam. As far as I know, there’s no combination card for trains and buses. You can get a rail pass for however many train trips you’ll be taking, and I think there are similar passes for some of the international coach touring companies, but I don’t think there’s something that covers both. This article might help you decide [whether it’s better to buy a rail pass or point-to-point tickets](http://italyexplained.com/how-to-decide-whether-to-buy-a-rail-pass-or-point-to-point-train-tickets-in-italy/). [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20052#respond)\n6. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/15292a698d544a405a2b9a36e3570b9a95b9fbb959c6d1df9253f5eb549953be?s=32&d=blank&r=g)zacksays: [23 August 2016 at 13:01](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20303) hi. i want to book bus in italy (online). suddenly, i found buscentre.it website. when i try to book online, webssite jump to onbus.it website. this is scammer or trusted? because ticket price very cheap. if trusted, this is agent or bus company? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20303#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [24 August 2016 at 16:05](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20335) Thanks for the question, Zack. I’m not familiar with either of those sites, but they look similar. I don’t know where you were trying to book, but it’s possible that you were transferred to another site because the latter is the official ticket site for that particular city or region. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20335#respond)\n7. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/bea3dec5f6e86f5ae176a169642b1350a97e723f171b6b1734438e13fea3089f?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Maxsays: [2 September 2016 at 06:20](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20603) Sena is the worst bus company you can take, overpriced tickets, lack of support, credit cards or any payment online often having issues on their website, delays delays delays, unapologetic staff, though it’s the only one that serves some routes which otherwise would take 2-3 times the time by train, guess why it’s only them? Yes you can say it’s fishy. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20603#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [2 September 2016 at 11:28](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-20613) My experience with Sena is limited, but it wasn’t bad… Lucky for me, perhaps? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=20613#respond)\n8. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/0e842c79c2dc94d55d7faa13d0bfb4cc5a155a5d5b34d75523f5e050be5bd16c?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Leeanesays: [2 January 2017 at 16:06](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-23970) Does anyone know if the Eurail goes to Cinque Terre? we couldn’t find it on their routing but I know you can get there by train?? Or Lake Garda? We tried looking at the bus pass but it also does not post the routes. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=23970#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [2 January 2017 at 18:00](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-23971) Thanks for the note, Leeane! There are a few things to unpack in your question… “Eurail” is a special ticket some foreign travelers can buy to travel in Europe (not just Italy), so what you really need to know is whether Italian trains serve the Cinque Terre (learn more about [Eurail passes](http://italyexplained.com/rail-pass-options-for-italy-eurail-interrail-the-eurail-italy-pass/)). The second thing is that “Cinque Terre” is the name of a collection of towns, so when you’re looking on a route map you won’t see “Cinque Terre” as a destination – you need to look up specific towns to find out whether buses or trains go there. You can read more in my [Cinque Terre guide](http://italyexplained.com/italy-cities-regions/cinque-terre/), and learn more about each of the [five towns in the Cinque Terre](http://italyexplained.com/the-five-towns-of-the-cinque-terre/). You’ll need to do the same regarding traveling to Lake Garda – find out the names of towns you might use as your destination, and check routes for those specific towns. Finally, I find [getting around by train](http://italyexplained.com/how-to-figure-out-if-trains-are-your-best-option-for-getting-around-in-italy/) much easier than by bus in Italy, for many of the reasons outlined in the article above. Bus travel can be useful within one region, but harder to manage to get around the whole country. I hope that helps! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=23971#respond)\n9. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/757b1c22c285172c07672c40603ba519771b897a00c67f6870aab1986bc49aad?s=32&d=blank&r=g)haidersays: [29 January 2017 at 14:48](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-24920) Hi how to run bus ticket center in italy? is that possible to open bus ticket center in any bus station like milan,rome etc [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=24920#respond)\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [29 January 2017 at 16:45](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-24925) I’m not sure I understand your question, Haider. Are you asking about where to buy a bus ticket, or about starting a business that sells bus tickets? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=24925#respond)\n10. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/8eba10c1ad2065d194a89d62461bcf960a4421e940e021c5c1d2a3ecc9afcfca?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Danielesays: [31 January 2017 at 14:40](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25027) I’m taking a train from milan to colico in como area .. i need to take a bus the c10 i believe to gravedone ..where can i purchase the bus ticket? is there a site online where i can purchase my bus ticket or do i have to find a store that sells them ? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25027#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [7 February 2017 at 10:46](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25283) [This page](http://www.sptlinea.it/content/en/interurban-network-times) has a link to the timetable for the bus you’re talking about, and [this page](http://www.sptlinea.it/content/en/tickets-offices?link=oln266n.redirect&nav=page36.50) shows you where in Colico you’d need to buy a ticket. There’s also a [ferry](http://www.navlaghi.it/c_download/E2016_NLC_Como_Colico.pdf) that takes less than 15 minutes to get across the lake (it leaves Colico twice a day). [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25283#respond)\n11. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/6c6bf07384915e558c557d5391d5b510a5e1a5e7341abc9af050985b4c99064a?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Nadeemsays: [12 February 2017 at 06:41](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25445) Hi! Thanks for the information on your site. I have a question, though. I’m planning a trip from Milan to Rome by bus. Found some cheap options like buscenter.it and baltour, since i’m on a tight budget. Buying online however requires either a paypal account or a credit card, both of which I don’t possess. I’d like to know if it is recommendable to buy tickets directly from the bus station or anything like that compared to buying online. If so, is it easy to find counters selling the bus tickets? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25445#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [5 March 2017 at 16:48](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26153) The only way I’ve ever purchased bus tickets is at the stations in Italy or from ticket sellers that are right there (news agents, cafes near a bus stop, etc.), so if you’re asking whether it’s safe to do so, I’d say yes. 🙂 The bus station hubs almost always have a ticket window (it’s just like buying train tickets), and for some buses you can buy tickets from newsstands and cafes like I mentioned above. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26153#respond)\n      - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/6c6bf07384915e558c557d5391d5b510a5e1a5e7341abc9af050985b4c99064a?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Nadeemsays: [6 March 2017 at 08:38](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26198) Thanks! Really appreciate the help 😀 [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26198#respond)\n12. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/16dd8ca870369afdd658a735713fefb73e5e493a436566adc224fc6e3b71be9d?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Maggiesays: [13 February 2017 at 01:56](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25465) Hi, do you have any idea how to travel from the city Bassano del Grappa to Asiago? (both in the Veneto, Vicenza region, approx.40km away from each other)I think I´ve had searched the whole internet already;) but have not found anything…(I actually need to get from Venezia Santa Lucia to Asiago -the closest I can get by train is Bassano but then…is taxi in Italy expensive;)?) Thanks in advance for any suggestions. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25465#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [5 March 2017 at 16:53](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26154) It looks like there’s a bus that will get you the last leg of the trip, from Bassano del Grappa to Asiago. On [this site](http://www.ftv.vi.it/), click on “EXTRAURBANO” under “ricerca orari” in the middle. A new window pops up. Choose Bassano del Grappa in the “Partenza” box and Asiago in the “Arrivo” box. When you click OK, you’ll get some more things to enter – date and window of time. You can choose “corse di andata” if you’re only going one way, or “corse di ritorno” if you’re needing a round-trip ticket. I hope that helps! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26154#respond)\n13. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/001861d001a95d6200c91f23458195791ebe07c925277a7b32ef53d417a20c63?s=32&d=blank&r=g)angassays: [16 February 2017 at 11:08](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25608) Hello Jessica, My husband and I are going to Italy in September and we plan to take the Marino bus from Naples to Gallipoli. We are both over 60 and our daughter says it would better advised to take the train. The problem is that there are no direct trains and we would have to change several times which is a bit of a worry as we do not speak Italian and only I can speak English ( our native language is French) and we come from a small island where trains do not exist so we have no experience in the matter. It is a five hour bus ride and I wonder if it won’t be too long. What is your experience of such a long ride? Thank you for your site and your help. Best regards. Angas [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25608#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [5 March 2017 at 16:58](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26155) Thanks for the question, Angas. It looks like the train trip would require 2 changes – one relatively soon after leaving Naples (in Caserta) and one near Gallipoli (in Lecce), but it also looks like the train trip would take longer than the bus trip! [According to the Rome2Rio site](https://www.rome2rio.com/s/Naples/Gallipoli-Italy), the train trip is nearly 8 hours while the bus trip is about 6.5. So the bus might really be the better option all the way around. I haven’t taken a bus trip that long in Italy, but I suspect for that kind of long-distance bus trip it’s a coach-style bus – so something more comfortable, where you put your luggage in the hold below and there’s a toilet on board, etc. You may be able to tell whether it would be that type of bus by looking at the website of the bus company that serves the route. I hope that helps! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26155#respond)\n      - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/001861d001a95d6200c91f23458195791ebe07c925277a7b32ef53d417a20c63?s=32&d=blank&r=g)angassays: [5 March 2017 at 21:15](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26176) Thank you very much Jessica. I checked, it is indeed a coach with toilet etc. on board. I hope everything will be fine. Best regards, Angas [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26176#respond)\n14. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/18ca1207141149fe7a271eb8928ce7dce8e4c70278ec9842649a4ae88fd9ec2b?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Tejashsays: [18 February 2017 at 13:25](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-25692) Hey friends.. im planning to come down to Italy in May. Few of the Places which we would be visiting is Milan, Venice, Florence, Rome , and Salerno. However i am bit confused between Salerno and Bari. What would be better option. We are 3 couples with 2 Kids. And also is it safe and fine to travel in buses between this location or should prefer Train. Could any one guide me with this. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=25692#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [5 March 2017 at 16:59](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-26156) All of the cities you mention – whether you visit Salerno or Bari – is served by high-speed trains, so I’d suggest that’s the better option for getting around. As for which is “the better option” between Bari or Salerno, it really depends on what you want to see/do in each one, and how long you’re willing to travel to get there (Bari is significantly further away than Salerno). [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=26156#respond)\n15. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/d85194f140516b4622eca6514ed49dc8b80189c6dbc2fee046e2158972bccb30?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Anitasays: [12 April 2017 at 07:49](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-27167) Hi, So glad to have found this site. we have a month to spare and are planning to visit: Rome, Florence, Milan and Venice then cross boarder to Slovenia, Austria and Hungary. Would you recommend Eurail Global Pass? Could we hop-on hop-off on trains with this pass in Italy and other central European countries? Thank you. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=27167#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [13 April 2017 at 18:04](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-27198) Yes, Anita, to answer your last question, a Eurail Global Pass gives you unlimited train trips in the 28 countries covered by Eurail – including all the countries you’ve listed – for as many days/months as the pass you buy is valid for. Whether that’s the best option for you is something you’ll need to figure out based on comparing the cost of the pass against an estimate of what train tickets would cost. Here are my [tips for deciding whether to buy a rail pass or point-to-point tickets in Italy](http://italyexplained.com/how-to-decide-whether-to-buy-a-rail-pass-or-point-to-point-train-tickets-in-italy/), which may help you choose. I hope that helps, and I’m glad you’re enjoying the site so far! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=27198#respond)\n16. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/bfa6556f8527ebb533710ec55bf38b70d5aaeeab9ba9f4ccb5a264146f348c92?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Nicolesays: [6 May 2017 at 04:45](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-27784) Hi Jessica, I was wondering if the bus timetables you linked is the same throughout the summer? Also, is there a way to prepay for bus tickets? Finally, is there an option to purchase tickets in english while in Italy? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=27784#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [11 May 2017 at 19:36](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-27969) Most of the time, bus schedules will differ by day of the week (fewer buses on Sundays) and holidays rather than summer/winter. If there are summer-specific schedules, you’ll probably the word “estate,” which means “summer” in Italian. I’m not sure what you mean by “prepay for bus tickets” – like a Metro card? That’s available for public transit in some cities, but not for longer-distance bus trips as far as I know. As for purchasing in English, that depends on how fluent the person at the ticket counter is! It’s always a good idea to learn a few Italian words for a transaction like that, especially if you’ll be using them repeatedly. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=27969#respond)\n17. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/2a4f3b6c16cd4bf205914fd1ea04f59993e956424f3d71da4b96f9c6251be177?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Anjali Msays: [17 May 2017 at 09:28](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-28126) Hi Jessica Is it better to take a bus from Genoa to Florence? looks like it takes almost the same time by train, given that a change is required in Pisa. What do you think? is there a reliable website that I can buy bus tickets from? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=28126#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [17 May 2017 at 16:48](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-28135) I would generally say the train is the best option for cross-regional travel in Italy (there’s no national bus network), but I looked at the Rome2Rio options for Genoa-Florence and the bus and train routes are pretty comparable. I found [a new-to-me bus service](https://www.flixbus.com/) (it appears to be German) that serves several Italian cities, including Genoa and Florence. Change the language and currency and you can buy tickets on that site. (I’ve never used it, so I can’t vouch for it personally.) [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=28135#respond)\n      - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/2a4f3b6c16cd4bf205914fd1ea04f59993e956424f3d71da4b96f9c6251be177?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Anjali Msays: [18 May 2017 at 08:48](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-28153) Thanks Jessica, I checked Flixbus before I emailed you, however they have pretty bad reviews. I would prefer to take the train, however I noticed that the schedule for Sunday, Sept 10th is not showing the nonstop train that leaves early morning. Is it too early to book for September? If so, when is it a good time to book for Sept 10th? We have to check in at the B&B in Florence before 1:00 pm as they close the reception after that. Since we also have luggage, it will be quite impossible for us to change trains at Pisa given that the switch-over time is around 8 minutes! What do you recommend we do? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=28153#respond)\n        - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [18 May 2017 at 14:05](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-28161) Eight minutes isn’t long, but it’s actually possible to make the switch – provided your incoming train is on time. You only need to get on the right train – in any car – before it leaves the station, since you can walk through the whole train to find your seat later. Pisa’s station only has 16 platforms, and if you’re anticipating arriving in Pisa (i.e. you’ve already retrieved your bags and are standing in the vestibule by the doors) you can hop right off when the train stops. That gives you a minute or two to find the track number for your Florence-bound train and then another few minutes to walk to the right platform. Of course, if that timeframe makes you uneasy, then you don’t want to do that! I like it when travel pushes my boundaries, but I’d rather not have it stress me out. 😉 As I outline in my article about [using the Trenitalia website](http://italyexplained.com/tips-for-using-the-trenitalia-website/), the official train schedule seems most reliable for a few months in advance, which may be why you’re not seeing a direct Genova-Firenze train on the 10th of September. Having said that, I tried a couple other dates (including weekdays) and I didn’t see a direct train at all. You might try using the [ItaliaRail](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=ITEX) site, which has a partnership with Trenitalia but is an American company with an infinitely more navigable site. (Italy Explained is an affiliate partner of ItaliaRail.) They’re showing earlier trains leaving Genoa, if you want to hedge your bets. You could leave at 5:15am, and if you miss the 8-minute connection time you know there’s a later train from Pisa that will get you to Florence in time. There’s also a 5:44am train that goes through Milan, though the ticket is more expensive and the trip is longer. Have you asked the B&B if there’s another way to check in if you miss your train and arrive after 1pm? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=28161#respond)\n          - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/2a4f3b6c16cd4bf205914fd1ea04f59993e956424f3d71da4b96f9c6251be177?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Anjali Msays: [19 May 2017 at 11:15](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-28188) Hi Jessica Thanks very much for the information! Cheers! I would be trying to get to Amalfi from Castrovillari in mid August. I’d prefer a train but don’t think there’s one that connects the two. Is bus a better option and how do I plan this in advance? Or can I purchase tickets day of travel? Which bus station is near Castrovillari? Thanks! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=29340#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [23 June 2017 at 11:51](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-29372) There’s no train service on the Amalfi Coast at all, and I just looked up Castrovillari to find that its train station closed in the late 1970s. So you’re going to need to drive or take a bus, yes. I like the Rome2Rio site for checking out transportation options ( [here’s the Castrovillari-Amalfi result](https://www.rome2rio.com/s/Castrovillari/Amalfi-Italy)), but I’ve never bought anything through the site so I can’t vouch for their ticket sales. You might need to do more investigation when you’re in Castrovillari, to find out the best route (or what’s available when you want to travel). [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=29372#respond)\n19. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/8a7d34e21afe4e7330c4c7c4500c34843b1423eaed56675933f61c056160c675?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Elle Smithsays: [31 July 2017 at 18:19](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30434) What is the best way (fastest, but comfortable) to get from Amantea to Montedoro on a Saturday I checked Rail Europe online but had no luck. I also checked Oraribus online and again, no luck and it was only in Italian! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=30434#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [1 August 2017 at 15:07](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30469) It looks like the fastest way would be to drive – even a bus will only get you as far as a town called Canicatti, from which point you’d have to have a car anyway. It appears Montedoro doesn’t have a train station or bus service. A train/bus/car combo would take 9+ hours (train to Catania, bus to Canicatti, drive from there). [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=30469#respond)\n      - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/8a7d34e21afe4e7330c4c7c4500c34843b1423eaed56675933f61c056160c675?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Elle Smithsays: [1 August 2017 at 17:18](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30477) Thanks, Jessica. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=30477#respond)\n        - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [2 August 2017 at 10:33](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30503) Yeah, train travel in the south – and especially to the islands – is much more challenging than in the north. Is that route from amantea to Montedoro/ Serradifalco dangerous? along the coast?\n          - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)[Jessica](http://italyexplained.com/)says: [5 August 2017 at 16:57](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30602) I don’t know that route, but here’s some general information about [driving in Italy](http://italyexplained.com/driving-in-italy/).\n          - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/8a7d34e21afe4e7330c4c7c4500c34843b1423eaed56675933f61c056160c675?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Elle Smithsays: [5 August 2017 at 20:02](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-30607) Thanks for that enlightening article !\n20. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/5212dd9b324095e4998b81ae16f13803a04af8b6dfc73844131909968f384a1b?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Evesays: [20 September 2017 at 10:07](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-31927) Hey Jessica! [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=35936#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Jessicasays: [18 May 2018 at 13:48](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-36043) My articles about [Italian trains](http://italyexplained.com/transportation-in-italy/italian-trains/) should help answer many of your questions. As you read above, buses from one region to another – which is the route you’re talking about – are few and far between, so even though it’s a many-stop process I’d still recommend the train. ( [Or a car, if you want to drive](http://italyexplained.com/driving-in-italy/).) Buses don’t typically take cash, so you’d need to buy a ticket beforehand – often at a tobacco shop or newsstand. [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=36043#respond)\n29. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/34fe6184ee2eebb93cfb78a0ca7669b5346ef0e3910c5c3ebd90352b85918a5e?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Shaimasays: [1 June 2018 at 19:24](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-36208) Hi Jess , i may go and spend some says in italy , and i wanted to know if the busses are working 24/24 or not ? there are some festivals that i want to go to and i need to move from one city to another , and im afraid that i cant find busses in late hours , can you help me ? [Reply](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/?replytocom=36208#respond)\n    - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/00270ed263160ba01da14b226daadd0b9fc6482b706bbbbb2d1e4fb59c7d00dc?s=32&d=blank&r=g)Jessicasays: [24 July 2018 at 12:39](https://italyexplained.com/bus-travel-in-italy/#comment-36628) It depends a great deal on where you are, but in my experience the only buses that run late or overnight are within a city like Rome. Some long-haul buses may have overnight routes, but – again – that really varies. Your best bet is to find the bus companies that serve the routes you’re looking at and check their websites (if they exist) or ask at the bus stations.
