        "url": "https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport",
        "title": "Transportation in Naples",
        "description": "# Transportation in Naples\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_v51EoQGjw57A)    Public Transit\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_EDNg_WDvw57A)    Networks\n- **Airport: [Alibus](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314%26Itemid%3D373&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1gqfI3HQzgcQVy7X93CzqV)** - €5 one-way (tickets also good for a connecting public transit ride up to 90 min total), circulates between Capodichino Airport, Piazza Garibaldi Central Train Station, and 2 stops at the Port (Immacolatella/Porta di Massa and Molo Angioino/Beverello). Runs every 20-30 minutes from 0745 (from the airport) or 0700 (from the port) to 2140 (from the airport) or 2100 (from Piazza Municipo)...but [check the schedule](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3eDUmJ1LJn8661we1UncDO) to be sure.\n  - Cheaper but Slower/Less Convenient Alternatives:\n    - [ANM Bus C68](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D2521%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3ZYIIeo6CIYhrnf61kkEFO) runs between Viale Fulco Ruffo di Calabria (just outside the airport) and Piazza Carlo III (which is still pretty far from downtown Naples)\n    - [ANM Bus 580](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D3663%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw04nVNggdfyU-esLANMTnvx) runs between Via Salomone Oreste (not far from the airport/Capodichino base) to Piazza Tecchio in Fuorigrotta (in one direction) and Stazione Scampia (in the other direction, aka Piscinola Metro Line 1).\n    - [ANM Bus 182](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D2434%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0awKUoZfZpnuaww0n9QfCZ) runs between Viale Comandante Umberto Maddalena (a bit farther away) to Piazza Cavour (a stop on Metro Lines 1 & 2) and then to Piazza Dante (also Metro Line 1)...but make sure you get on the right direction (the other way goes north of the airport)\n  - If you're going to [Sorrento](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/sorrento) or the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi), [Curreri Viaggi runs a bus between the airport and Sorrento](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.curreriviaggi.it%2Fit%2Fnavetta-aeroporto-di-napoli&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2S71FTvIl6J0MYEJxclZRc).\n  - If you're going to Salerno (at the end of the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi)), [CSTP](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.cstp.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw30CXT9Y8zTF33syNa65K7w) runs [line 119](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.cstp.it%2Flinee%2F119fe.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2zIsewd7i_nSi5ZKfX9GJa) but only 3 times a day, and not on Sundays\n- **Underground: Metropolitana**- Naples' main \"metro\" underground, which is technically two services:\n  - the older Line 2 is run by [Trenitalia](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aQR4S7llrhRoSa0ET3bME)\n  - the rest ( [Line 1](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D71%26Itemid%3D98&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3PGyrdMWqw9PAyEk8RI9pz) and [**Funiculars**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D73%26Itemid%3D100&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2S5mP5Feh65spDMrctmg93)) are run by [ANM](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3IzrLpmVXZs6DNzLrUF-HC) (Azienda Napoletana Mobilità, formerly Metronapoli).\n  - The [Naples Metro](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FNaples_Metro&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1W7NvwydDH87MVEZXfHZAm) and [Naples Line 2](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FLine_2_%28Naples%29&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1xxhf34FBPUdjYAKyQsG5H) Wikipedia pages also have good information, as does [Vivere Napoli for Line 2](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.vivere.napoli.it%2Fmobilita%2Fmetropolitana-di-napoli-linea-2%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw00AmWZERyGS28-w4U2pxbR) (in Italian)\n- **West: [Cumana](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9)** [and **Circumflegrea**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9) run between Naples and the [Campi Flegrei](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/campi-flegrei), west of Naples. While their trains are a bit more run down than the Metropolitana, they can be very convenient for people who live west of Naples. [The EAV website](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9) has more.\n- **South: [Circumvesuviana](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9)** runs around Vesuvius (hence the name, \"around Vesuvius\"!) to various suburbs; it is most useful for getting to [Herculaneum, Pompeii](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/vesuvius-area), and [Sorrento](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/sorrento). Catch it from the Centrale Train Station; you have to walk to the end of the train station and down some escalators to get there; in most cases you want to catch the trains leaving for Sorrento. Note the tips under \"Avoiding Pickpockets,\" below. Herculaneum, Pompeii, and Sorrento require NA-2, NA-3, and NA-5, respectively (see Tickets, below).\n  - The [**Campania Express**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fcampania-express-2020-en&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1paA1b9ZkhWaJ1GrPG3xBL) is a special version of the Circumvesuviana that only stops in Naples, Ercolano, Pompei, and Sorrento (basically the 4 places tourists want to go), and thus goes a lot faster. It also costs a lot more (€15 roundtrip, or €10 if you have a Campania Artecard, compared to €2.50, €3.20, or €4.50 for the normal NA-2/3/5 tickets)...it also only runs a few times a day and only between late May and mid-October.\n- **Buses: [ANM (Azienda Napoli Mobilità)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3IzrLpmVXZs6DNzLrUF-HC)** is the major Naples bus service; they mostly service the city itself, but also have \"extraurbane\" lines that run to nearby suburbs like Pozzuoli. They also run the Alibus airport shuttle and half of the Metro system (see above).\n- **Buses Outside Naples: [CTP (Consorzio Trasporti Pubblici di Napoli)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.ctp.na.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3Hd7b1E9gImnM2qevQEP-3)** runs buses in various Neapolitan suburbs, mostly north of the city, including the [T63R](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.ctp.na.it%2Fpercorsi2.asp%3FLINEA%3DT63R&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2SmQWRUyM3Lzjz_GmU8Jxe), which runs roughly once an hour between just outside the gate of the **Gricignano Support Site** (the schedule calls it \"Cittadella NATO\") and the **Aversa train/metro station** from 0400 to 2200. From the Aversa train station you can take trains (including the EAV Arcobaleno \"Rainbow\" line) to Naples or other destinations (Rome, etc.). CTP also runs buses between towns north and west of Pozzuoli (Quarto, Monterusciello, Licola, Arco Felice...).\n- **Amalfi Coast: [SITA Sud](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.sitasudtrasporti.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3i823yZM2CD3OlHVRJW7Q2)** run buses mostly to/from and within the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi). SITA stands for _Società Italiana Trasporti Automobilistici_ (\"Italian Auto Transport Company\")\n- **Everything Else: [EAV (Ente Autonomo Volturno)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0XYFAbYV279svLnLN6T0qf)** runs a bunch of different services including the Circumvesuviana (see above), former MetroCampania NordEst (the _Arcobaleno_ or \"Rainbow\" line, going from Piscinola, the end of Line 1, to Aversa, near the Support Site), and the former \"Sepsa\" lines (Cumana and Circumflegrea)\n  - they have a [really good map showing all of the rail lines in the Naples region](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520treni_mappa%2520web.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3mHqVjDIQ-k3R928EfTLoJ)\n- **Maps:**\n  - Trains/Metros:\n    - [**Google Maps with \"Transit\" turned on**](https://www.google.com/maps/@40.8493663,14.235288,13.45z/data=!5m1!1e2)\n    - Naples: ANM has a [pretty good PDF map showing all of the various trains, funiculars, and metros in Naples](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2FPDF%2Fmapparetemetronuova.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3z6M7B49VHjwX6w5f-wDi0) (copy [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.sisec.it%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2018%2F05%2Fmappa-trasporto-pubblico.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2gT7KLGrTZefEMzdqVijPR))\n    - Region: [ANM has a nice PDF map showing all trains/metros/funiculars in the greater region](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2Fanm_napoli_mappametro_191015.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw13AkkUg-erqEey1r3HfOPT) (copy [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2Fanm_mappa_metro_2019.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3MED_tM4LugT733Ys10hFI))\n      - [EAV has an even more comprehensive PDF map](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520treni_mappa%2520web.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3mHqVjDIQ-k3R928EfTLoJ) showing rail connections as far as Salerno, Benevento, Formia\n    - somebody made a [cool Google Map of the major metro systems](https://www.google.com/maps/d/viewer?oe=UTF8&msa=0&mid=1JP8Kse7GrnsIqJ5ZQvLuQwBlUpY&ll=40.85259549442355%2C14.166516000000007&z=12)\n  - Buses:\n    - ANM uses [Moovit to map its bus lines](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fmoovitapp.com%2Findex%2Fit%2Fmezzi_pubblici-lines-Napoli_e_Campania-882-9248&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1t-bGfZjcR7Cl2LjiMs02i); you can also download the Moovit app ( [iOS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fapps.apple.com%2Fus%2Fapp%2Fmoovit-train-bus-times%2Fid498477945&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2NHualTKjUnOZqBz4u41Xz), [Android](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.tranzmate))\n    - EAV has a [basic bus map in PDF](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520bus_mappa%2520web%2520pubblicata.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3rIUeW7yT_7OV88oWrN2p7)\n  - More Info:\n    - [Napoli Unplugged's outstanding Transportation page](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fnaples-transportation&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0-luKXNOVz1nIjAlk2q1jf) explains most transit in the Naples area (including a list of bus lines that are of most interest to tourists)\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_XC7OejAEw57h)    Tickets\n- **\"Integrato\" TIC Napoli**- This is good for any public transit in and near the city of Naples, including the entire Metropolitana system. You can either buy an hourly ticket (good for 90 minutes, €1.50) or an all-day pass ( _giornaliero_, €4.50).\n- **UnicoCampania TIC NA-x**- This is for travel to/from Naples and somewhere outside the city. For example, to travel between urban Naples and...\n  - Pozzuoli, Herculaneum, Gricignano, Quarto = NA-2 (€2.70 for 120 min)\n  - Pompeii, Monte di Procida, Villa Literno = NA-3 (€3.50 for 140 min)\n  - Caserta, Capua = NA-4 (€4.30 for 160 min)\n  - Sorrento = NA-5 (€4.90 for 180 min)\n- **UnicoCampania TIC AC-x** - This is for travel between two points that doesn't include Naples. For example, TIC AC-4 gets you between Salerno and Positano. Find the exact fare [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.tic-campania.net%2FTIC%2Fcomponent%2Fcontent%2Farticle%3Fid%3D7&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw11wQ68QmM-etJwVn02toAP).\n- **\"Aziendale\" (Transport Operator) Tickets** - these are issued by one transport company and are only good on that company's services. Usually cheaper than the TIC tickets for a specific ride/itinerary (as long as you aren't changing operators), but more of a hassle. [This page](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1344%26Itemid%3D320&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1A_NjD94buzEh1i_XthmF-) shows what they look like for ANM; [this PDF](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2Fallegati%2FEAV%2520Brochure%2520Ferro%25202015.pdf%23overlay-context%3Dit%2Fcontent%2Fnuovi-biglietti-e-abbonamenti-aziendali-2015&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1gl3ONH-oKQ5HrTx0uU35P) shows the ones for EAV. (Prices below are as of 2021:)\n  - for example, if you want to take a single Naples Metropolitana Line 1 ride within the city of Naples, you can pay €1.10 for a single-way ( _corsa singola_) ticket from ANM (confusingly called \"ANM-U-NA-1\") or \"tipo A\", or €3.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - ...but if you want to ride the Metropolitana Line 2 (which is run by Trenitalia/RFI) or the Cumana (which is run by EAV) within the city of Naples, you pay €1.30 for a single-way ticket ( _corsa singola_\"ANM-U-NA-2\") or \"tipo B\", or €3.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - ...compared to the €1.60 UnicoCampania TIC Napoli which lets you ride any form of transport within the city of Naples for 90 minutes (including transfers), or €4.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - Similarly, an \"Aziendale\" ticket from Trenitalia/RFI on the metropolitana line 2 between Pozzuoli and Napoli costs €2.20 (good for a single trip) compared to the €2.70 TIC NA-2 (which good for 120 minutes, including transers)...unfortunately there does not seem to be a daily pass for NA-x and AC-x passes.\n- [**UnicoAlibus**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3eDUmJ1LJn8661we1UncDO) - €5 one-way ticket to/from the Capodichino airport, Piazza Garibaldi (train station), and Piazza Municipio (ferry terminal near Piazza Plebiscito), that is also valid for other connections within the Naples urban area (for up to 90 minutes)\n- Ticket Prices\n  - You can [look up which zone (NA-x or AC-x) cities/towns are from each other on the UnicoCampania site](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.unicocampania.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2fwgW0CRvLuzFEmyNYsXNJ)\n  - [This sheet](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FSchema%2520Tariffario%2520Integrato%2528Allegato%25202%2529_0.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3s_yU6VLx6fAtSb2TGWrop) shows all the prices.\n  - You can also download the UnicoCampania app ( [iOS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fapps.apple.com%2Fit%2Fapp%2Funico-campania-app%2Fid1504055273&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw06uUwDC8AlK1fYA85oJQy-), [Android](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=net.pluservice.unicoc))...but be forewarned that (as of 2021) they have really bad reviews (they are sloooow...but they do let you look up costs/ticket amounts)\n- [**ArteCard**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1h1sXs-lTTgxCDPTCtQEOv) - While the [ArteCard 365](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see) is great for residents, there are a few tourist versions that offer free transport as well as entry into museums/archaeological sites:\n  - [\"Campania 3 day\"](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-3-days%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0OO0hb1EJ5IxkvKafiLsH9), costs €32 (as of 2021) which includes free transit anywhere in Campania for 3 days, plus free entry into two sites with 50% discounts on most other sites\n    - [\"Young\" (18-25) version](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-3-days-young%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3nO9gJhrvzaO35kp2V75ea) is €25 and has the same benefits\n  - [\"Campania 7 day\"](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-7-days%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2ATeKtMAqpm-S_DwQROWYb) costs €34 (as of 2021) and includes 5 sites for free, but does _not_ include free transit (anymore)\n  - ArteCard passes _don't_ cover the Campania Express, Alibus, Sita Sud (Sorrento/Positano/Amalfi buses), or Trenitalia trains (except Metropolitana Line 2)\n- [confusingly the Campania Region started with \"UnicoCampania\", rebranded as \"TIC - Ticket Integrato Campania\" in 2015, and then in 2017 re-rebranded to \"UnicoCampania - TIC\" (!)]\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_1osui1txw58P)    Taxis/Uber\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_OfMk1ociw58T)    Prices\n- Official taxis have [**pre-determined fixed rates**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2018%2F03%2FTariffe_taxi_2017.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3Y2-WZAByDEsCKJQgh3M9p) (copy [here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fvisitnaples.com%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2019%2F02%2FTariffe_taxi.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0NzYTNmp0y5gs57PtTyzQc)) set by the [city government](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.comune.napoli.it%2Fflex%2Fcm%2Fpages%2FServeBLOB.php%2FL%2FIT%2FIDPagina%2F1193&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2-rzl9UodF8-1i8AIFdy9w) (more on [Napoli Unplugged](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fnaples-taxi-services&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2pRIjXDU47A7XPQvvGAJyg))\n  - Consult the fare card (which should be displayed) and discuss the fare with the driver before you leave to make sure you don't get ripped off.\n  - [The airport also has a useful page](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.aeroportodinapoli.it%2Fen_GB%2Fon-arrival%2Fby-taxi&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3vAmtfm-GnGKmWg6Jgqxbv) describing how to register a complaint\n- For U.S. Military, the Naples Chief Petty Officers' Association runs a **\"Tipsy Taxi\"** service to prevent drunk driving (basically the chief's mess will spot you up to €150 for a taxi, which you need to repay the next day); call the NSA CDO at 335-640-6597 or NSA Quarterdeck at 081-568-5547\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_1T0g1EMyw58U)    Strikes\nGoogle Sites\n\nReport abuse",
        "position": 7,
        "markdown": "Search this site\n\nEmbedded Files\n\nSkip to main content\n\nSkip to navigation\n\n# Transportation in Naples\n\nA comprehensive guide to transit, ferries, trains, and other ways Companies](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_c7P1y6mew571)\n\n[Trains](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_855xO8PHw579)\n\n[Stations](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_k7Ha06Gqw57_)\n\n[Types of Trains](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_RMV1DFbbw58G)\n\n[Airports/Airlines](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_Z4psilLlw58M)\n\n[Taxis/Uber](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_1osui1txw58P)\n\n[Apps](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_A8UO157r-qfV)\n\n[Phone Numbers/Companies](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_vK5eIc6u-bxa)\n\n[Prices](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_OfMk1ociw58T)\n\n[Strikes](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport#h.p_1T0g1EMyw58U)\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_OyA_-QZjw4tG)    [Driving](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport/driving-in-italy)\n\nSee comprehensive pages about [driving in Italy](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport/driving-in-italy) and [driving directions in Naples](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport/driving-in-italy/driving-in-naples)\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_v51EoQGjw57A)    Public Transit\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_EDNg_WDvw57A)    Networks\n\nMain public transit systems in and around Naples:\n\n  - **Airport: [Alibus](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314%26Itemid%3D373&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1gqfI3HQzgcQVy7X93CzqV)** \\- €5 one-way (tickets also good for a connecting public transit ride up to 90 min total), circulates between Capodichino Airport, Piazza Garibaldi Central Train Station, and 2 stops at the Port (Immacolatella/Porta di Massa and Molo Angioino/Beverello). Runs every 20-30 minutes from 0745 (from the airport) or 0700 (from the port) to 2140 (from the airport) or 2100 (from Piazza Municipo)...but [check the schedule](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3eDUmJ1LJn8661we1UncDO) to be sure.\n    - Cheaper but Slower/Less Convenient Alternatives:\n      - [ANM Bus C68](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D2521%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3ZYIIeo6CIYhrnf61kkEFO) runs between Viale Fulco Ruffo di Calabria (just outside the airport) and Piazza Carlo III (which is still pretty far from downtown Naples)\n      - [ANM Bus 580](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D3663%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw04nVNggdfyU-esLANMTnvx) runs between Via Salomone Oreste (not far from the airport/Capodichino base) to Piazza Tecchio in Fuorigrotta (in one direction) and Stazione Scampia (in the other direction, aka Piscinola Metro Line 1).\n      - [ANM Bus 182](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D2434%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0awKUoZfZpnuaww0n9QfCZ) runs between Viale Comandante Umberto Maddalena (a bit farther away) to Piazza Cavour (a stop on Metro Lines 1 & 2) and then to Piazza Dante (also Metro Line 1)...but make sure you get on the right direction (the other way goes north of the airport)\n    - If you're going to [Sorrento](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/sorrento) or the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi), [Curreri Viaggi runs a bus between the airport and Sorrento](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.curreriviaggi.it%2Fit%2Fnavetta-aeroporto-di-napoli&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2S71FTvIl6J0MYEJxclZRc).\n    - If you're going to Salerno (at the end of the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi)), [CSTP](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.cstp.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw30CXT9Y8zTF33syNa65K7w) runs [line 119](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.cstp.it%2Flinee%2F119fe.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2zIsewd7i_nSi5ZKfX9GJa) but only 3 times a day, and not on Sundays\n  - **Underground: Metropolitana**\\- Naples' main \"metro\" underground, which is technically two services:\n    - the older Line 2 is run by [Trenitalia](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aQR4S7llrhRoSa0ET3bME)\n    - the rest ( [Line 1](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D71%26Itemid%3D98&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3PGyrdMWqw9PAyEk8RI9pz) and [**Funiculars**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D73%26Itemid%3D100&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2S5mP5Feh65spDMrctmg93)) are run by [ANM](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3IzrLpmVXZs6DNzLrUF-HC) (Azienda Napoletana Mobilità, formerly Metronapoli).\n    - The [Naples Metro](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FNaples_Metro&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1W7NvwydDH87MVEZXfHZAm) and [Naples Line 2](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FLine_2_%28Naples%29&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1xxhf34FBPUdjYAKyQsG5H) Wikipedia pages also have good information, as does [Vivere Napoli for Line 2](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.vivere.napoli.it%2Fmobilita%2Fmetropolitana-di-napoli-linea-2%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw00AmWZERyGS28-w4U2pxbR) (in Italian)\n  - **West: [Cumana](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9)** [and **Circumflegrea**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9) run between Naples and the [Campi Flegrei](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/campi-flegrei), west of Naples. While their trains are a bit more run down than the Metropolitana, they can be very convenient for people who live west of Naples. [The EAV website](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9) has more.\n  - **South: [Circumvesuviana](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9)** runs around Vesuvius (hence the name, \"around Vesuvius\"!) to various suburbs; it is most useful for getting to [Herculaneum, Pompeii](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/vesuvius-area), and [Sorrento](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/sorrento). Catch it from the Centrale Train Station; you have to walk to the end of the train station and down some escalators to get there; in most cases you want to catch the trains leaving for Sorrento. Note the tips under \"Avoiding Pickpockets,\" below. Herculaneum, Pompeii, and Sorrento require NA-2, NA-3, and NA-5, respectively (see Tickets, below).\n    - The [**Campania Express**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fcampania-express-2020-en&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1paA1b9ZkhWaJ1GrPG3xBL) is a special version of the Circumvesuviana that only stops in Naples, Ercolano, Pompei, and Sorrento (basically the 4 places tourists want to go), and thus goes a lot faster. It also costs a lot more (€15 roundtrip, or €10 if you have a Campania Artecard, compared to €2.50, €3.20, or €4.50 for the normal NA-2/3/5 tickets)...it also only runs a few times a day and only between late May and mid-October.\n  - **Buses: [ANM (Azienda Napoli Mobilità)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3IzrLpmVXZs6DNzLrUF-HC)** is the major Naples bus service; they mostly service the city itself, but also have \"extraurbane\" lines that run to nearby suburbs like Pozzuoli. They also run the Alibus airport shuttle and half of the Metro system (see above).\n  - **Buses Outside Naples: [CTP (Consorzio Trasporti Pubblici di Napoli)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.ctp.na.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3Hd7b1E9gImnM2qevQEP-3)** runs buses in various Neapolitan suburbs, mostly north of the city, including the [T63R](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.ctp.na.it%2Fpercorsi2.asp%3FLINEA%3DT63R&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2SmQWRUyM3Lzjz_GmU8Jxe), which runs roughly once an hour between just outside the gate of the **Gricignano Support Site** (the schedule calls it \"Cittadella NATO\") and the **Aversa train/metro station** from 0400 to 2200. From the Aversa train station you can take trains (including the EAV Arcobaleno \"Rainbow\" line) to Naples or other destinations (Rome, etc.). CTP also runs buses between towns north and west of Pozzuoli (Quarto, Monterusciello, Licola, Arco Felice...).\n  - **Amalfi Coast: [SITA Sud](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.sitasudtrasporti.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3i823yZM2CD3OlHVRJW7Q2)** run buses mostly to/from and within the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi). SITA stands for _Società Italiana Trasporti Automobilistici_ (\"Italian Auto Transport Company\")\n  - **Everything Else: [EAV (Ente Autonomo Volturno)](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0XYFAbYV279svLnLN6T0qf)** runs a bunch of different services including the Circumvesuviana (see above), former MetroCampania NordEst (the _Arcobaleno_ or \"Rainbow\" line, going from Piscinola, the end of Line 1, to Aversa, near the Support Site), and the former \"Sepsa\" lines (Cumana and Circumflegrea)\n    - (If you're curious, SEPSA used to stand for Società per l'Esercizio di Pubblici Servizi Anonima which means \"Joint-Stock Company for the Operation of Public Services\").\n    - they have a [really good map showing all of the rail lines in the Naples region](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520treni_mappa%2520web.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3mHqVjDIQ-k3R928EfTLoJ)\n\n- **Maps:**\n\n  - Trains/Metros:\n    - [**Google Maps with \"Transit\" turned on**](https://www.google.com/maps/@40.8493663,14.235288,13.45z/data=!5m1!1e2)\n    - Naples: ANM has a [pretty good PDF map showing all of the various trains, funiculars, and metros in Naples](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2FPDF%2Fmapparetemetronuova.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3z6M7B49VHjwX6w5f-wDi0) (copy [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.sisec.it%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2018%2F05%2Fmappa-trasporto-pubblico.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2gT7KLGrTZefEMzdqVijPR))\n    - Region: [ANM has a nice PDF map showing all trains/metros/funiculars in the greater region](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2Fanm_napoli_mappametro_191015.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw13AkkUg-erqEey1r3HfOPT) (copy [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Fimages%2Fstories%2Fanm_mappa_metro_2019.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3MED_tM4LugT733Ys10hFI))\n      - [EAV has an even more comprehensive PDF map](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520treni_mappa%2520web.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3mHqVjDIQ-k3R928EfTLoJ) showing rail connections as far as Salerno, Benevento, Formia\n    - somebody made a [cool Google Map of the major metro systems](https://www.google.com/maps/d/viewer?oe=UTF8&msa=0&mid=1JP8Kse7GrnsIqJ5ZQvLuQwBlUpY&ll=40.85259549442355%2C14.166516000000007&z=12)\n  - Buses:\n    - ANM uses [Moovit to map its bus lines](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fmoovitapp.com%2Findex%2Fit%2Fmezzi_pubblici-lines-Napoli_e_Campania-882-9248&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1t-bGfZjcR7Cl2LjiMs02i); you can also download the Moovit app ( [iOS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fapps.apple.com%2Fus%2Fapp%2Fmoovit-train-bus-times%2Fid498477945&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2NHualTKjUnOZqBz4u41Xz), [Android](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.tranzmate))\n    - EAV has a [basic bus map in PDF](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FEAV%2520bus_mappa%2520web%2520pubblicata.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3rIUeW7yT_7OV88oWrN2p7)\n  - More Info:\n    - [Napoli Unplugged's outstanding Transportation page](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fnaples-transportation&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0-luKXNOVz1nIjAlk2q1jf) explains most transit in the Naples area (including a list of bus lines that are of most interest to tourists)\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_cIU-2X1Sw57R)    Late-Night Options\n\nUnfortunately, most of the public transit listed above doesn't run very late, especially when considering [how late Neapolitans eat dinner](https://www.wikinapoli.com/benvenuti/italian-culture). Some options:\n\n- **Metro Line 2:** As of 2021, the last Line 2 for Pozzuoli leaves Piazza Garibaldi at 2156\n  - According to the [Trenitalia website](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aQR4S7llrhRoSa0ET3bME), the _first_ Line 2 trains leave from Pozzuoli towards Naples at 0554. The first direct trains from Naples (Piazza Garibaldi) to Pozzuoli are at 0623 (but direct trains from Napoli Campi Flegrei to Pozzuoli start at 0500).\n- **Metro Line 1:** As of 2021, the last train leaves Piscinola at 1:04 AM and leaves Piazza Garibaldi at 1:32 AM...this can get you to various parts of downtown or Vomero but not the [Campi Flegrei](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/campi-flegrei). See the [ANM Website](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D71%26Itemid%3D98&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3PGyrdMWqw9PAyEk8RI9pz) for more.\n- **Cumana/Circumflegrea:** As of 2021, the [final Cumana](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FCUMANA%2520dal%252030%2520Novembre_Aggiornato%2520il%252015.12.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1TagOSjAGN9iH9_t5IKmkD) headed to Torregaveta leaves Montesanto at 2141; the [final Circumflegrea](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FCIRCUMFLEGREA%2520dal%252030%2520novembre_0.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2dGkrddjBABCJfTRIpYgtB) headed for Licola leaves Montesanto at 2112 (but there's another one that goes to Quarto leaving at 2136). See the [EAV Website](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Forari-linee-ferroviarie-emergenza-covid-19-rimodulazione-servizio-vigore&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3U0YpMm72gGHx8wFRhmHP9) for more.\n- **Campi Flegrei:** An [EAV](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0XYFAbYV279svLnLN6T0qf) bus ( [\"Napoli - Monte di Procida\"](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2Feavbus%2FNapoli%2520-%2520Monte%2520di%2520Procida_Rev_25-07-19.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2Ar2GPa-4ZAa_207rstD8D)) leaves [various spots in downtown Naples](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2Feavbus%2FFermate%2520Napoli%2520-%2520Monte%2520di%2520Procida.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2oOFbP0Wjzca-cpLDFkDif) including Piazza Vittoria (on the Naples waterfront, not far from Castel dell'Ovo) at 12:15 AM bound for Pozzuoli, Cuma, Bacoli, and finally Monte di Procida.\n  - **this bus is probably the best option** for folks living in Pozzuoli and beyond who want to stay downtown beyond 10 PM\n- **Airport:** Midnight to 4:30 AM you can take [night bus N5](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D2540%26Itemid%3D260&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3Da0meiFRpNXgNb9jpYFWx) (but the pickup/drop-off near the airport is at Piazza Vittorio, at the intersection of Calata Capodichino and Corso Secondigliano, a good ~mile walk from the airport).\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_KoNF_a56w57d)    Avoiding Pickpockets\n\nNaples in general, and especially the Central Train Station at Piazza Garibaldi, is notorious for pickpockets. A **particularly bad place is getting on & off the Circumvesuviana train**. However, if you take the following precautions, you can travel in safety (also realize that it is extremely rare for pickpockets to actually hurt anyone—you are much more likely to get hurt/killed in the US):\n\n- **Be a \"hard target\"** \\- if you blend in and look like you know what you're doing, you're less likely to be targeted than if you obviously look like a lost foreigner\n- **Don't have anything to steal** \\- minimize how much cash/credit cards you have on you\n- **Hide what you have** \\- put your money, etc. in a money belt, interior pocket, etc. so it's hard to get\n- **Get on the front** \\- pickpockets tend to avoid the first car of the train, since the driver/conductor is usually there\n- **Avoid commotions/crowds** \\- any commotion is likely a distraction so you don't notice the pickpockets\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_XC7OejAEw57h)    Tickets\n\nConveniently, all the various transit agencies have collaborated to allow you to buy one ticket, the UnicoCampania TIC (\"Ticket Integrato Campania\"), to use across several different transit networks. Most _tabaccherie_ (tobacco shops) and newstands sell them. For example, you can buy a TIC that takes you from the Cumana (run by [Ente Autonomo Volturno](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0BjDifdRI5cXpseLBBCVzQ) (E.A.V.), formerly SEPSA) to the metro line 2 (run by [Trenitalia](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aQR4S7llrhRoSa0ET3bME) or RFI, Rete Ferroviaria Italiana) to the Circumvesuviana (also run by [E.A.V.](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0XYFAbYV279svLnLN6T0qf), formerly its own agency) to a local Amalfi Coast bus (run by [SITA Sud](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.sitasudtrasporti.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3GQB0-Kgms_PUaT8YaOpip), also see [unofficial English site](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.ondaverde.it%2Fsitai.htm&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1KVQMy9oxqKXBIXUw-YZsX)). Here are the major tickets:\n\n- **\"Integrato\" TIC Napoli**\\- This is good for any public transit in and near the city of Naples, including the entire Metropolitana system. You can either buy an hourly ticket (good for 90 minutes, €1.50) or an all-day pass ( _giornaliero_, €4.50).\n- **UnicoCampania TIC NA-x**\\- This is for travel to/from Naples and somewhere outside the city. For example, to travel between urban Naples and...\n  - Pozzuoli, Herculaneum, Gricignano, Quarto = NA-2 (€2.70 for 120 min)\n  - Pompeii, Monte di Procida, Villa Literno = NA-3 (€3.50 for 140 min)\n  - Caserta, Capua = NA-4 (€4.30 for 160 min)\n  - Sorrento = NA-5 (€4.90 for 180 min)\n- **UnicoCampania TIC AC-x** \\- This is for travel between two points that doesn't include Naples. For example, TIC AC-4 gets you between Salerno and Positano. Find the exact fare [here](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.tic-campania.net%2FTIC%2Fcomponent%2Fcontent%2Farticle%3Fid%3D7&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw11wQ68QmM-etJwVn02toAP).\n- **\"Aziendale\" (Transport Operator) Tickets** \\- these are issued by one transport company and are only good on that company's services. Usually cheaper than the TIC tickets for a specific ride/itinerary (as long as you aren't changing operators), but more of a hassle. [This page](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1344%26Itemid%3D320&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1A_NjD94buzEh1i_XthmF-) shows what they look like for ANM; [this PDF](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2Fallegati%2FEAV%2520Brochure%2520Ferro%25202015.pdf%23overlay-context%3Dit%2Fcontent%2Fnuovi-biglietti-e-abbonamenti-aziendali-2015&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1gl3ONH-oKQ5HrTx0uU35P) shows the ones for EAV. (Prices below are as of 2021:)\n  - for example, if you want to take a single Naples Metropolitana Line 1 ride within the city of Naples, you can pay €1.10 for a single-way ( _corsa singola_) ticket from ANM (confusingly called \"ANM-U-NA-1\") or \"tipo A\", or €3.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - ...but if you want to ride the Metropolitana Line 2 (which is run by Trenitalia/RFI) or the Cumana (which is run by EAV) within the city of Naples, you pay €1.30 for a single-way ticket ( _corsa singola_\"ANM-U-NA-2\") or \"tipo B\", or €3.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - ...compared to the €1.60 UnicoCampania TIC Napoli which lets you ride any form of transport within the city of Naples for 90 minutes (including transfers), or €4.50 for an all-day ( _giornaliero_) card\n  - Similarly, an \"Aziendale\" ticket from Trenitalia/RFI on the metropolitana line 2 between Pozzuoli and Napoli costs €2.20 (good for a single trip) compared to the €2.70 TIC NA-2 (which good for 120 minutes, including transers)...unfortunately there does not seem to be a daily pass for NA-x and AC-x passes.\n- [**UnicoAlibus**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.anm.it%2Findex.php%3Foption%3Dcom_content%26task%3Dview%26id%3D1314&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3eDUmJ1LJn8661we1UncDO) \\- €5 one-way ticket to/from the Capodichino airport, Piazza Garibaldi (train station), and Piazza Municipio (ferry terminal near Piazza Plebiscito), that is also valid for other connections within the Naples urban area (for up to 90 minutes)\n- Ticket Prices\n  - You can [look up which zone (NA-x or AC-x) cities/towns are from each other on the UnicoCampania site](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.unicocampania.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2fwgW0CRvLuzFEmyNYsXNJ)\n  - [This sheet](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.eavsrl.it%2Fweb%2Fsites%2Fdefault%2Ffiles%2FSchema%2520Tariffario%2520Integrato%2528Allegato%25202%2529_0.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3s_yU6VLx6fAtSb2TGWrop) shows all the prices.\n  - You can also download the UnicoCampania app ( [iOS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fapps.apple.com%2Fit%2Fapp%2Funico-campania-app%2Fid1504055273&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw06uUwDC8AlK1fYA85oJQy-), [Android](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=net.pluservice.unicoc))...but be forewarned that (as of 2021) they have really bad reviews (they are sloooow...but they do let you look up costs/ticket amounts)\n- [**ArteCard**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1h1sXs-lTTgxCDPTCtQEOv) \\- While the [ArteCard 365](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see) is great for residents, there are a few tourist versions that offer free transport as well as entry into museums/archaeological sites:\n  - [\"Campania 3 day\"](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-3-days%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0OO0hb1EJ5IxkvKafiLsH9), costs €32 (as of 2021) which includes free transit anywhere in Campania for 3 days, plus free entry into two sites with 50% discounts on most other sites\n    - [\"Young\" (18-25) version](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-3-days-young%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3nO9gJhrvzaO35kp2V75ea) is €25 and has the same benefits\n  - [\"Campania 7 day\"](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.campaniartecard.it%2Fcampania-7-days%2F%3Flang%3Den&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2ATeKtMAqpm-S_DwQROWYb) costs €34 (as of 2021) and includes 5 sites for free, but does _not_ include free transit (anymore)\n  - ArteCard passes _don't_ cover the Campania Express, Alibus, Sita Sud (Sorrento/Positano/Amalfi buses), or Trenitalia trains (except Metropolitana Line 2)\n- \\[confusingly the Campania Region started with \"UnicoCampania\", rebranded as \"TIC - Ticket Integrato Campania\" in 2015, and then in 2017 re-rebranded to \"UnicoCampania - TIC\" (!)\\]\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_HzUAG2k2w57u)    Ferries\n\nFerries connect Naples (and other places, like Pozzuoli) with the various islands (Procida, Ischia, Capri) and with the coast (Sorrento, Amalfi, etc.). There are also some longer-haul ferries that run to the Pontine Islands, Aeolian Islands, Sicily, Tunisia, etc.\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_zjUhyxoww57v)    Ferry Terminals\n\nThe following are the main ferry terminals; [Napoli Unplugged's Maritime Transportation Page](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2FNaples-Maritme-Transportation.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3iJhv1BCNS8Num8t7D-XQ-) also shows all the area ports' locations on Google Maps:\n\n- Napoli\n  - **Mergellina** is an smaller terminal near the Mergellina metro stop at 40°49.63'N 14°13.35'E\n  - Near the Castel Nuovo (with free shuttle bus connecting the two every ~15 min):\n    - **Molo Beverello** (40°50.28'N 14°15.3'E)\n    - Calata **Porta di Massa**(near Molo Immacolatella Vecchia, 40°50.57'N 14°15.56'E)\n- **Pozzuoli** \\- ferries to Ischia & Procida, plus a couple to Capri. Metro del Mare runs to Sorrento and Amalfi Coast from spring to fall. 40°49.44'N 14°7.09'E accessible by Cumana or Metro Line 2\n- **Procida** \\- one port, many ferries going to/from Ischia stop here\n- Ischia - **Ischia Porto** (closest to Naples/Castello Aragonese, 40°44.74'N 13° 56.32'E), **Casamicciola** (north coast, near spas, 40°44.96'N 13° 54.33'E), **Forio** (largest town, on the west coast)\n- **Capri** \\- Marina Grande\n- **Sorrento** \\- Marina Piccolo (is actually larger than the Marina Grande, which cannot accomodate ferries)\n- **Positano**, **Amalfi**, **Salerno** \\- during the summer, ferries link these Amalfi Coast towns with each other and Naples\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_o0FLLKFTw57z)    Schedule Summaries\n\n- **Recommended: [Traghetti Ischia](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.traghetti-ischia.info%2Fen%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2vc69Cko_ZKp19Lol6MU8d)**shows Ischia ferries but also pretty much everything in the area except for some Amalfi Coast ferries\n  - [Capri.com](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.capri.com%2Fen%2Fferry-schedule&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1a1Ks_tvkcdFs9ZBUfqvbO) also has a pretty nice / easy to read schedule lookup tool\n  - [NaplesBayFerry.com](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.naplesbayferry.com%2Fen&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0iXssgPsyn4FStO_9Z7tS-) is also pretty good, and includes a helpful map\n  - [Capri Tourism](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.capritourism.com%2Fen%2Fship-timetable&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1zVozch0EbkSuTwnmoMK1N) also is pretty comprehensive but despite its dropdown menu it's still missing most of the Amalfi ferries; it does have a nice PDF of the Capri ferries\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_c7P1y6mew571)    Ferry Companies\n\n- [**SNAV**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.snav.it%2Fen%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0bTXyi1JsUd38lCWc1UMC0) \\- ferries from Naples Molo Beverello to [Capri, Ischia Casamicciola, and Procida](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/capri-procida-ischia); also goes from Naples Mergellina to [Palermo (Sicily)](https://www.wikinapoli.com/italy/sicily), Pontine Islands ( [Ventotene](https://www.wikinapoli.com/sports/scuba/ventotene) and Ponza), and Aeolian Islands (such as Stromboli), 081-428-5555 \\[warning: their catamarans are known by some as the \"Hurlinator\"!\\]\n- [**NLG**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.navlib.it%2Fen%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw28ikva_r24cwqkvpd1CKtC) (Navigazione Libera del Golfo) - ferries from Naples Molo Beverello to Capri and Sorrento, and on weekends to Amalfi/Positano, as well as ferries within the [Amalfi Coast (among Sorrento, Amalfi, Positano, and Salerno)](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi), 081-552-0763, 081-552-7209\n- [**Alilauro**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.alilauro.it%2Fen&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0-Bk_PpEf876YCS8ub3dCl) \\- ferries from Naples Molo Beverello to Ischia, 081-497-2238, [reservation@alilauro.it](mailto:reservation@alilauro.it)\n- [**MedMar**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.medmargroup.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0hip9lWtPvIEbftjnKqCgK) \\- ferries from Pozzuoli to Ischia (Porto & Casamicciola) and Procida, 081-333-4411\n- [**Caremar**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fshop.caremar.it%2Fen%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3W07J2mYKj42TEKVit9apR) \\- ferries from Pozzuoli to Procida, Ischia (Porto & Casamicciola) and from Naples Molo Beverello and Calata Porta Massa to Procida, Ischia, and Capri, as well as between Sorrento and Capri, 081-189-66690, [callcenter@caremar.it](mailto:callcenter@caremar.it)\n- [**Gestour (Gestur)**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.minicrocieregestur.com%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1TOAvvNMLAKXlquCNi9jib)\\- ferries from Pozzuoli to [Procida](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.minicrocieregestur.com%2Fprocida-pozzuoli%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2trStbTt2TNkZEKaHYzeK5), [Ischia](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.minicrocieregestur.com%2Fpozzuoli-ischia-porto-pozzuoli%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw06-vP8t79k8UUWJivddMcI), and (some years in July/Aug) [Capri](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.minicrocieregestur.com%2Fcapri%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2yrHWolqj3QQHyCGc1kN33), 081-853-1405, [info@gestour.it](mailto:info@gestour.it)\n- [**Alicost**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.alicost.it%2Fen&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2J8sgXsOPcamrDllUV7w4F) \\- ferries within the [Amalfi Coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/amalfi) (including to/from Capri and Ischia, and to/from the [Cilento coast](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/southern-campania/cilento)), plus a ferry from Salerno to the Aeolian Islands (such as Stromboli), 089-871-483, 081-497-2238, [booking@alicost.com](mailto:booking@alicost.com)\n- [**Tirrenia**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.tirrenia.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0qBlokrpaA8Ef3-ZB-e5_-) \\- daily ferry to Cagliari (Sardinia), 027-602-8132, [helpdesk@tirrenia.it](mailto:helpdesk@tirrenia.it)\n- \\[ **Metro del Mare** \\- \\*was\\* a low-cost series of ferries that ran in the summer from Bacoli, Pozzuoli, and Naples to the Vesuvius suburbs, Sorrento, and Amalfi Coast...as recently as 2018 Alilauro ran the [\"Vie del Mare\" program](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.alilauro.it%2Fnews%2Fle-vie-del-mare-71&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1mjbWpZZoklwE5BuCOAfwc) that was similar\\]\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_855xO8PHw579)    Trains\n\nTrains connect you easily and conveniently with the rest of Italy and Europe. Depending on how many people are traveling, trains can be cheaper than driving, and in many cases (such as going to a crowded city like Rome or Florence) cause less hassle. For a great introduction to Italian trains, see [The Man in Seat 61's Guide to Train Travel in Italy](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.seat61.com%2Ftrain-travel-in-italy.htm&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1rUmRZkhUzoeH-NmTAsnHN).\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_k7Ha06Gqw57_)    Stations\n\nThe following are the main train stations:\n\n- **Napoli Centrale (Garibaldi)** \\- this is the main train station, with the most traffic, next to Piazza Garibaldi downtown. Be forewarned that there are a number of pickpockets in the area.\n- **Napoli Campi Flegrei**\\- like Mergellina, this is both a Metro and a train stop; a handful of trains going north to Rome or south to other destinations stop here.\n- **Pozzuoli Solfatara (or Villa Literno)**\\- while Pozzuoli is mostly a Metro Line 2 stop, occasionally trains run from here to Villa Literno (and even Formia), from which you can catch a train to Rome, bypassing downtown Naples. If you live in the Campi Flegrei, you can go from Pozzuoli or Villa Literno to Rome fairly cheaply/easily.\n  - **Is it worth it?** Since it takes 0.5-1 hours to get to Napoli Centrale from Pozzuoli on Metro Line 2, if you're starting from the [Campi Flegrei](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/campi-flegrei) it can sometimes be both cheaper _and_ faster to take a train to Rome directly from Pozzuoli (changing trains in Villa Literno or Formia). For example, a Regionale train from Pozzuoli (changing in Villa Literno or Formia) takes about 2.5 - 3 hours and cost €12.65, while even the fastest trains from Napoli Centrale are 1.2 hours and can cost at least 3-4 times as much—if it takes 1 hour to get to Napoli Centrale (which is typical), that means your total travel time is almost the same!\n  - **What's the schedule?** Go to the [Trenitalia website](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aQR4S7llrhRoSa0ET3bME) and search for trips between Pozzuoli Solfatara and Roma (be sure to select \"Regional trains\"); the ones that show 2 Regionale trains will be the ones that bypass downtown Naples.\n  - **How do I buy tickets?** To buy a ticket, stop by the bar outside of the Pozzuoli station and ask for a _biglietto anadata a Roma_ (one way ticket to Rome). If the connecting train in Villa Literno/Formia is an Intercity, stop by the ticket booth to buy an upgrade, or you can pay for the upgrade plus an additional fee on the train.\n- **Napoli Afragola** \\- this relatively new (2017) station services Frecciarossa trains bound between Salerno and Milano Centrale, departing roughly once an hour. If you live near the Support Site this is probably the most convenient/quick way to get to cities in the north.\n- **Caserta** \\- this station to the [north of Naples](https://www.wikinapoli.com/to-see/northern-campania) serves a handful (~3/day) of Frecciargento trains leaving for Rome/north and Salerno/south\n- **Aversa**\\- this is the closest train station to the Support Site, although it does not service high-speed trains\n  - Want to go to Rome from here? The best bet is the relatively slow (but also pretty cheap) ~2.5 hour Regionale train, since traveling to Napoli Centrale takes a while (it's similar to the Pozzuoli discussion, above). If speed matters more than money, you might try the Afragola station (see above).\n  - See CTP, above, for information about the bus between the Support Site and Aversa train station.\n  - EAV (see above) also runs a metro (the Arcobaleno or \"Rainbow\" line) between Aversa and Piscinola (where you can catch Metropolitana line 1 to Naples).\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_RMV1DFbbw58G)    Types of Trains\n\nThere are four major types of trains, in descending order of speed and cost\n\n- **Frecce (\"Arrow\")**\\- high-speed trains that travel between major cities\n  - Other names: \"Frecciarossa\" (\"Red Arrow\"), \"Frecciaargento\" (\"Silver Arrow\", slightly slower than the red ones), \"Alta Velocità\" (Highest Speed), or \"Fast\"\n  - Frecciabianca (\"White Arrow\") are the slowest high-speed trains (formerly known as \"EuroStar\")...as of 2021 [they're fairly rare](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.italiarail.com%2Ffrecciabianca&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw12-fauEOrnz4a4SWVc9Bfz) (mostly for travel between Milan and other cities in the North)\n  - Time from Naples to Rome is just over 1 hour\n  - Price varies by when you buy; if you buy well in advance, you can get substantial discounts\n- [**Italo**](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.italotreno.it%2Fen&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw19zeCIDhOKJuyeZPwy27kC) \\- this is a private train company that runs Frecce-like trains along the same tracks as Trenitalia...it's almost the same price/speed, although with some plusses (free wifi) and minuses (Rome's station is Tiburtina, which is less convenient for most people than Termini). See [this comprehensive review by The Man in Seat 61](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.seat61.com%2Ftrains-and-routes%2Fitalo.htm&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3DJpGwH9cagVe1-luBYhcz)\n- **Intercity** \\- while they are slower than Frecce trains, IC trains have more comfortable 2nd class cabins, and arguably have more personality. Since they are older, they are likelier to be dirtier, however. Intercity \"Plus\" trains are somewhat newer, and inexplicably cost less.\n- **Regionale** (\"Regional\") - these stop in every cattle town along the way (\"Regionale Veloce\" skip a few stops). Some are modern commuter trains; some look like they were left over from Mussolini's regime. Cheap but slow. Most Regionale do not have reserved seating, and you have to \"validate\" your ticket before boarding.\n\nAt night, many trains (such as ICN, \"Intercity Notte\") offer beds, with between 2 and 6 to a compartment. This can often be a convenient way to travel long distances, since you don't have to pay for a hotel room that night, and you show up first thing in the morning. On some trains (such as Intercity 2nd class), if there is room in a compartment, you can also push together two facing seats to make a horizontal bed.\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_Z4psilLlw58M)    Airports/Airlines\n\nThree main airports are nearby that you should consider for air travel; also see the [Europe page's **list of** **discount airlines**](https://www.wikinapoli.com/europe):\n\n- **Naples Capodichino (NAP)** \\- obviously the closest and most convenient, although it does not have as many flights as Rome's airports\n- **Roma Fiumicino** (\"Leonardo da Vinci\", **FCO**) \\- Rome's major airport. Obviously this would involve getting to Rome first, but sometimes it is cheaper/easier since Rome has more flights.\n- **Roma Ciampino** ( **CIA**) \\- a formerly military-only airport that now caters to low-cost airlines such as easyJet and RyanAir\n  - as of 2019 there's a Trenitalia-sponsored bus that connects between Roma Termini train station and Ciampino train station called [Ciampino AirLink](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen%2Fservices%2Fciampino-airlink.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1MMC_JXfbeeA5xc-O_ZxVp), thus making rail connections much easier\n  - There is **cheap parking for military personne** l near the military base (41°48.6'N 12°34.9'E, see [map](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport/ciampino-parking-lot)). If driving, do not follow signs for Ciampino Centro (with a target), only Ciampino Airport (with a picture of an airplane). Some Americans also like [parkeu.com](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.parkeu.com%2Fairport%2Froma-ciampino%2Fparking%2Frome-ciampino-airport-parking.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3-IiIK9wpqdhHuVKvVaJ8P) which charges ~€30 for 7 days.\n  - there are various other transport options (see [Wikipedia's information on transport here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org%2Fwiki%2FCiampino%25E2%2580%2593G._B._Pastine_International_Airport%23Ground_transportation&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0-BSHfuDd6DFcHRJH4Mvfv))\n  - [Do NOT take a taxi from Ciampino, as Amanda Ruggeri at Revealed Rome explains](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Frevealedrome.com%2F2013%2F08%2Fciampino-airport-taxi%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0dVzd2OI0HZSBseI3dS5VW) ( [An American in Rome echos this and gives a bunch of alternatives](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fanamericaninrome.com%2Fwp%2F2018%2F02%2Fciampino-airport-to-rome%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3pcs3tCPPm9zu9a7SUzj14), although the article was written before the AirLink launched.)\n\nSee [this site for information on transport to and from both FCO and CIA](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.toandfromtheairport.com%2Frome.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3fqOT1NkNoIr2GQSYkDrNF).\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_1osui1txw58P)    Taxis/Uber\n\nUnless you are at a place like Piazza Garibaldi, it is generally hard to find/flag down a taxi. Thus, it is usually advisable to call/use an app.\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_A8UO157r-qfV)    Apps\n\n- [**Uber**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.uber.com%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0NcemYm2Xwi8qD2fMs_2fT) \\- same app you probably already have; Uber finally launched in Naples in [fall 2020](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.uber.com%2Fit%2Fblog%2Fuber-taxi-ora-disponibile-napoli%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0nhOWFe84HHMcS4qf-x7NC)\n- [**FREE NOW (fka MyTaxi)**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Ffree-now.com%2Fit%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0NrgwlFt9cy54tBvZOqQBG) \\- [iOS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fapps.apple.com%2Fus%2Fapp%2Ffree-now-mytaxi%2Fid357852748&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2D6GkFeYv1Y32ngrlA-q2S) and [Android](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=taxi.android.client), taxi app that works in many places in Italy (and Europe)...note that the meter starts when the driver _accepts_ the trip, so if you notice a driver accepting from very far away (and suspect there are closer cabs) you might want to cancel & try again.\n- (otherh apps: inTaxi? it Taxi? GOXGO?)\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_vK5eIc6u-bxa)    Phone Numbers/Companies\n\n- [Consortaxi](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.consortaxi.com%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1Yx5iv9eh-Nw-si02jnkuj) 081-22-22, 081-20-20-20\n- [Taxinapoli](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.taxinapoli.it%2Fen%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3o6p2WWCXWzmi4a2x5t0Kj) 081-88-88, they use the [GOXGO app](http://www.google.com/url?q=http%3A%2F%2Fgoxgo.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1aUFrkl3vFF1NfAWH6c_7m)\n- [Radio Taxi Partenope](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.radiotaxipartenope.it%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw32iyxtRNCcKk_OMSmzrdfC) 081-01-01, they use the [itTaxi app](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.ittaxi.it%2Fapp&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0Sq2nyDZW5LDVyw1a0IJS9)\n\n### [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_OfMk1ociw58T)    Prices\n\n- Official taxis have [**pre-determined fixed rates**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2018%2F03%2FTariffe_taxi_2017.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3Y2-WZAByDEsCKJQgh3M9p) (copy [here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fvisitnaples.com%2Fwp-content%2Fuploads%2F2019%2F02%2FTariffe_taxi.pdf&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw0NzYTNmp0y5gs57PtTyzQc)) set by the [city government](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.comune.napoli.it%2Fflex%2Fcm%2Fpages%2FServeBLOB.php%2FL%2FIT%2FIDPagina%2F1193&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2-rzl9UodF8-1i8AIFdy9w) (more on [Napoli Unplugged](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.napoliunplugged.com%2Fnaples-taxi-services&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2pRIjXDU47A7XPQvvGAJyg))\n  - Consult the fare card (which should be displayed) and discuss the fare with the driver before you leave to make sure you don't get ripped off.\n  - [The airport also has a useful page](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.aeroportodinapoli.it%2Fen_GB%2Fon-arrival%2Fby-taxi&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw3vAmtfm-GnGKmWg6Jgqxbv) describing how to register a complaint\n- For U.S. Military, the Naples Chief Petty Officers' Association runs a **\"Tipsy Taxi\"** service to prevent drunk driving (basically the chief's mess will spot you up to €150 for a taxi, which you need to repay the next day); call the NSA CDO at 335-640-6597 or NSA Quarterdeck at 081-568-5547\n\n## [Copy heading link](https://www.wikinapoli.com/transport\\#h.p_1T0g1EMyw58U)    Strikes\n\nStrikes are, unfortunately, common in Italian public transit, trains, and airports. However, even during a strike, or _sciopero_, there are still trains and busses that run...in fact, there are certain trains that are \"guaranteed\" even in the case of a strike (see a [Trenitalia page on the subject](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fen%2Fpurchase%2Finfo_contacts%2Fin_case_of_strike.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2V27LxNN3btMwEvc5oBbPN), which links to [a page in Italian that lists guaranteed trains](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.trenitalia.com%2Fit%2Finformazioni%2Ftreni_garantiti_incasodisciopero.html&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw1BsnU7PLu_G8-kaQqKL_MM)).\n\nTo learn when strikes may be scheduled, consult the following resources:\n\n- [**Commissione Garanzia Sciopero**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.cgsse.it%2Fcalendario-scioperi%2F&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw27R51oDfl602ErcVWW3F47) \\- the official calendar of all strikes, you can search by region / date to learn more, in Italian\n- [**SummerinItaly's Strike Page**](https://www.google.com/url?q=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.summerinitaly.com%2Ftraveltips%2Ftransport-strikes-in-italy&sa=D&sntz=1&usg=AOvVaw2R0Z9bQ7k84ktI0hA9Xmki) \\- easy to read, in English, but not very comprehensive\n\nGoogle Sites\n\nReport abuse",

        "title": "Train travel in Italy 2026: Trains, tickets & rail passes",
        "description": "### _Times, fares & how to buy tickets_\nThe best way to visit the cities of Italy is by train, trains link almost every town & city of any size, there are even [trains to \\\nSicily](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trains-to-sicily.htm)! This page explains how Italian trains work, whether to use Trenitalia or Italo, whether a rail pass is worth buying, and how best to buy tickets.\n\n![small bullet train](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/nice-to-italy-by-train.htm)\n\n![small bullet point](https://www.seat61.com/images/template/bullet-small.jpg)[TGV high-speed \\\ntrains from Paris to Italy](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/paris-to-milan-by-train.htm)\n\n![small bullet point](https://www.seat61.com/images/template/bullet-small.jpg)[Travel insurance, mobile data, \\\nVPN & other tips](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#travel-insurance-and-other-tips)\n\n## How to check train schedules & fares\n- **Check train times & fares**\n**within Italy** [**using any of the**\\\n**websites shown here**](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Buy_Italian_train_tickets_online) **.**\n- Some major cities have more\nthan one main station, [see which \\\nstation to use in which city](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Which_station_in_which_city).  If you're not sure, most websites allow\nyou to select the plain city name, or _any station_.\n- Most Italian trains are run by state-owned\nnational operator _Trenitalia_, but privately-owned\n[high-speed _Italo_ trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm)\ncompete with Trenitalia on the high-speed lines linking Turin, Milan, Venice, Bologna, Florence, Rome, Naples\n& Salerno.  If you use Trenitalia.com or\nItaliarail.com you'll only see Trenitalia's trains, but if you use\n[Thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) or\n[Raileurope.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm)\nyou'll see both operators' trains and can compare prices.  This is\nworth remembering!\n[More about \\\nItalo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm).\n[Which is better, Trenitalia or Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm#Italo_versus_Trenitalias_Frecciarossa)?\n- Some tourist destinations have no station but\ncan be reached by bus, such as San Marino, Positano, Praiano & Amalfi.\nTo reach the islands of Ischia, Capri & Sardinia you'll need a ferry.\n[See how to reach \\\nmajor tourist \\\ndestinations not on the rail network](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Direct_trains_to_Sicily_).\n- A few tourist destinations are\nserved by a local railway run by a transit authority and cannot be found using websites\nthat only show Trenitalia trains.  For example, Sorrento, Pompei and Herculaneum are linked to Naples by the Circumvesuviana Railway\nrun by EAV,\n[see the information here](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#How_to_reach_Herculaneum,_Pompeii_&_Sorrento).\n\n## Travel tips\n### Which station in which city?\n- **Naples**\n\n[Napoli Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm) is the main station in Naples city centre, co-located with the Circumvesuviana station for trains to Pompeii & Sorrento and 30 minutes walk from the ferry terminal for Capri & Ischia.\n\n_Do not book to Napoli Afragola by mistake_ as I've known a number of people do, Naples Afragola is a 'parkway' station for high-speed trains outside the city in the middle of nowhere, aimed at motorists from surrounding towns & villages.\n\n### How to reach  Herculaneum, Pompeii & Sorrento\n- **Naples to Herculaneum,**\n**Pompeii & Sorrento by Campania Express**\n\n### How to reach Capri\n- Buy a mainline ticket from Venice, Florence, Rome or\nwherever to Naples Centrale at\n[[www.italiarail.com](http://www.italiarail.com)](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [[www.trenitalia.com](http://www.trenitalia.com)](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n- The island of\nCapri is just off Sorrento.  You have two options:  You can take a\ndirect ferry from Naples to Capri, journey time around 45 minutes, fare €25, or you can\ntake the Circumvesuviana Railway to Sorrento (55-65 minutes) then a shorter\nferry crossing to Capri (around 25 minutes, fare around €20).\n- If you choose the ferry from\nNaples, take a taxi (5-10 minutes) or walk (25 minutes) from\n[Naples \\\nCentrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm) to Naples Beverello ferry quay.  Fast ferries link Naples Berevello with Capri every hour or two between 07:00 &\n18:00 taking 45 minutes, see [[www.snav.it](http://www.snav.it)](https://www.snav.it/)\nor [Omio.com](https://omio.sjv.io/c/146261/409973/7385) for times\n& fares.  The ferry fare is around €25 plus a euro or two per item of large\nluggage.  You don't need to pre-book, just turn up, buy a ticket\nand hop on.\n[Map of Naples showing station & ferry terminals](https://maps.google.co.uk/maps/ms?msid=201915664237202451070.0004e2172a5588963a13d&msa=0&ll=40.844853,14.272699&spn=0.065903,0.132093).\n- If you choose to take the local\nCircumvesuviana Railway to Sorrento, there are many ferries to Capri, no\npre-booking necessary.  Just be aware that it's a longish steep walk from\nSorrento Circumvesuviana station down the hill to the ferry terminal.\n- Ferries from both Naples &\nSorrento arrive at Capri's busy Marina Grande, there's a funicular railway up\nthe steep hillside to Capri town itself. Bring plenty of money to Capri, even a\nsmall beer costs over €7!\n\n### How to reach Amalfi, Positano,  Praiano\n- **Option 4, a private transfer**\n**from Naples to Praiano, Positano or Amalfi hotels**",

point](https://www.seat61.com/images/template/bullet-small.jpg)  <br>Booking opens up to 4 months ahead, but it varies, <br>[as explained \\<br>here](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-booking-horizons.htm).<br>![small bullet point](https://www.seat61.com/images/template/bullet-small.jpg)**Airports:**  You can buy tickets from Rome Fiumicino, Milan Malpensa or <br>Pisa airports to anywhere in Italy.<br>![small bullet point](https://www.seat61.com/images/template/bullet-small.jpg)**Pompeii & Sorrento** are not on the mainline Booking.com](https://www.seat61.com/images2/bookingdotcom-banner-300x250.jpg)](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247). |\n|  |  |\n\n### _Times, fares & how to buy tickets_\n\nThe best way to
