*\n\n## How to check train schedules & fares\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Trains in Italy:  A Frecciarossa train at Rome Termini](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-ETR500-boarding.jpg)<br>**No check-in, no hassle**. Simply walk <br>from the city centre into the station, glance at the <br>departures board, walk to your train and hop on, any time up to departure.  <br>Above, a [Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) <br>has arrived at [Rome Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm) |\n|  | ![Florence SMN station](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-florence-smn.jpg)<br>**Concourse at** [**Florence SMN**](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm), walking distance from <br>the famous Duomo & Ponte Vecchio. |\n\n- **Check train times & fares**\n**within Italy** [**using any of the**\\\\\n**websites shown here**](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Buy_Italian_train_tickets_online) **.**\n\n- Some major cities have more\nthan one main station, [see which \\\\\nstation to use in which city](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Which_station_in_which_city).  If you're not sure, most websites allow\nyou to select the plain city name, or _any station_.\n\n- Most Italian trains are run by state-owned\nnational operator _Trenitalia_, but privately-owned\n[high-speed _Italo_ trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm)\ncompete with Trenitalia on the high-speed lines linking Turin, Milan, Venice, Bologna, Florence, Rome, Naples\n& Salerno.  If you use Trenitalia.com or\nItaliarail.com you'll only see Trenitalia's trains, but if you use\n[Thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) or\n[Raileurope.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm)\nyou'll see both operators' trains and can compare prices.  This is\nworth remembering!\n[More about \\\\\nItalo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm).\n[Which is better, Trenitalia or Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm#Italo_versus_Trenitalias_Frecciarossa)?\n\n- Some tourist destinations have no station but\ncan be reached by bus, such as San Marino, Positano, Praiano & Amalfi.\nTo reach the islands of Ischia, Capri & Sardinia you'll need a ferry.\n[See how to reach \\\\\nmajor tourist \\\\\ndestinations not on the rail network](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Direct_trains_to_Sicily_).\n\n- A few tourist destinations are\nserved by a local railway run by a transit authority and cannot be found using websites\nthat only show Trenitalia trains.  For example, Sorrento, Pompei and Herculaneum are linked to Naples by the Circumvesuviana Railway\nrun by EAV,\n[see the information here](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#How_to_reach_Herculaneum,_Pompeii_&_Sorrento).\n\n\n## Maps  of the Italian rail network\n\n- [Map of the Italian railway network](https://www.acprail.com/maps/italy-train-map/)\n\n- [Schematic rail map](https://www.viaggiatreno.it/) (not all routes shown) with\nreal-time train info.\n\n- [For other European train maps see the \\\\\nadvice here](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-travel.htm#maps).\n\n\n## How  to buy tickets\n\n### Do you  need to buy in advance?\n\n- **Regional trains, no**\n\nFor example, Pisa to Florence,\nFlorence to Lucca or Siena, Milan to Como or Tirano, Venice to Trieste.\n\nThere's no need to buy in advance\nand no cost advantage in doing so as the price is fixed, buying online or in an\napp just saves time at the ticket office.  There are no assigned seats, you\nsit where you like.  In most cases tickets are sold in unlimited numbers so\nthe train can't sell out, although regional trains on a few routes now have\nlimited numbers.\n\nSince August 2023, online tickets\nfor regional trains work in a fiddly but flexible way,\n[learn more about regional (R) & regional express \\\\\n(RV) trains and how regional tickets work](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#regionale).\n\n\n- **Long-distance trains,**\n**yes**\n\nFor example, Venice to Florence\nor Rome, Rome to Naples or Turin, Milan to Venice.\n\nAll seats on\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm),\n[Frecciargento](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciargento.htm),\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca) & [InterCity](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#intercity-trains)\ntrains are reserved, so they can in theory sell out.  However, as there are\nso many trains each with hundreds of seats\nthere are almost always places available on most trains even just before\ndeparture. So you can buy at the station on the day if you want. The issue is price.  Trenitalia ditched the old\nfare-per-kilometre approach\nto pricing in 2009 and adopted airline-style dynamic pricing for advance-purchase fares whilst increasing\nthe fully-flexible _Base_ price.  So on the day of travel you'd pay\nthe _Base_ fare, Rome-Florence €55, Rome-Venice\n€99, but if you book in advance you can\nbuy a cheap _Economy_ or _Super-Economy_ fare from as little as €19.90\nRome-Florence or €29.90 Rome-Venice, assuming you're OK with limited or no refunds or changes\nto travel plans.  It's your call!\n\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  |  |\n\n### When does booking open?\n\n- **Booking opens up to 4 months ahead,**\n**but this varies**\n\nIt\ncan shrink to as little as 30 days for dates immediately after Europe-wide timetable changes on the 2nd Saturday in\nJune and the second Saturday in December.\n\n\n- **If some trains are shown, but others**\n**are missing...**\n\nTrenitalia loads trains in\nblocks, usually high-speed trains first and regional, InterCity & sleeper trains\nlater.  I've seen high-speed trains loaded, but not regional trains.  I've seen regional trains loaded, but not high-speed trains.\nIntercity trains to Sicily and ICN sleeper trains usually get loaded last, after\nother trains.  The Milan-Sicily night train usually gets loaded last of\nall!\n\nSo if\nyou don't see all the trains you _expect_ to see, _don't_\nassume that the missing trains have all been mysteriously cancelled, assume they haven't\nbeen loaded yet.  Wait!  Remember that the Italians themselves buy\ntrain tickets just hours, days or weeks ahead, not 6 months!\n\n\n### Types of fare\n\n- **Base fare**\n\nThis\nis the fully-flexible fare for Trenitalia's high-speed Frecce & intercity trains, it's what you'd pay at the station on the day.\nRefundable, only valid on the train booked but can be changed as many times as\nyou like before departure\nor (just the once) up to an hour after departure.\n\nThe Base fare was originally one fixed price for a\ngiven journey, but since 2017 the Base fare for Frecciarossa & Frecciargento trains varies slightly by day of the week or the popularity of\neach particular train.\n\n\n- **Economy & Super-Economy**\n\nThese are cheaper\nadvance-purchase fares for Trenitalia's high-speed & intercity trains, only\nvalid on the train booked, no refunds, limited availability.  Economy\ntickets can be changed before departure, paying any difference in fare.\nSuper-Economy tickets cannot be changed, use it or lose it.\n\nThese are the fares you want\nfor cheap travel if you are certain what time train you want.  The price\nvaries like air fares, cheaper in advance and on less popular dates, more\nexpensive close to departure and on busy days or times of day.\n\n- **Speciale Frecce**\n\nAn ultra-cheap advance purchase\nfare for Trenitalia's high-speed & intercity trains, buy at least 14 days\nahead.  No refunds, no changes.\n\n- **Insieme**\n\nA small group fare for 3-5\npeople.  Limited availability, price varies.  No refunds, no changes,\nuse it or lose it.\n\n\n- **Senior & FrecciaYoung**\n\nThese can only be bought if you have\nTrenitalia's **Cartafreccia** railcard which you probably\nhaven't, so ignore these.  When you select them it immediately asks for\nyour Cartafreccia card number, so you can't buy them by mistake!\n\n\n- **Ordinaria**\n\nThis is the normal fare for\nregional trains.  It's fixed-price, you can buy a ticket at that price even\non the day.  Since 2023, online regional tickets are only good for the specific train you've selected, but you can change the date or time of departure free of\ncharge up to 23:59 the day before departure, and you can change the time of\ndeparture until 23:59 on the day of travel.\n\nOrdinaria tickets are available in unlimited numbers\non most regional trains, although there are now some regional trains where the\nticket numbers sold are limited, [see more about how regional (R) & regional express \\\\\n(RV) trains \\\\\nare ticketed](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#regionale).\n\n\n- **Smart & Smart2**\n\n\nThese are cheap\nadvance-purchase fares for international journeys.\nNo refunds or changes, specific train only, limited availability, must be bought\nat least a week before departure.\n\n\n- **Child fares & child age limits**\n\nTrenitalia changed its child age limits\nin 2012.  On all trains, children under 4 go free with no ticket required,\njust bring them along.  On\nregional trains, the old age limit still applies, children under 12 travel at\nthe child rate.  But now, on national trains children under 15 can travel\nat the child rate.  National trains means Frecciarossa, Frecciargento,\nFrecciabianca, InterCity, InterCity Notte (ICN) sleeper trains.\n[See the train travel with children page](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-travel-with-children.htm).\n\n\n### How to buy tickets\n\n- **Option 1, buy at** **[Italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)** _\\- no booking fee!_\n\nYou can buy Trenitalia tickets at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U) in €, £, $,\nCa$ or Au$ - [to buy in US$ \\\\\nclick here](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61).\n\nItaliaRail are an established and\nreliable US-based agency who link to Trenitalia's ticketing system.\n\nItaliarail books the same trains as Trenitalia's own website, but in plain English with\nno quirky translations.  You can use familiar English\nplace-names such as Venice or Florence.  However, they don't sell tickets\nfor\n[Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm).\n\nItaliarail shows a whole day's\ntrains in the search results and can book up to 20 people at a time, Trenitalia's own website only shows a\nfew hours-worth of trains at a time and can only\nbook up to 5 people at a time.\n\nItaliarail has implemented its\nown fare types, different from Trenitalia:\n\n**_Basic_** is the\ncheapest fare, no refunds, no changes.  It's the same price\nas whatever Trenitalia's cheapest fare is for that train, whether Super-Economy,\nEconomy or Base, but it's always non-changeable & non-refundable when bought\nfrom Italiarail.\n\n**_Viva_** is the\nflexible fare,\n100% refundable and 100% changeable, often costing little more than the _Basic_\nfare.  A _Viva_ fare can be a better deal than Trenitalia's own fares as\nonly Trenitalia's most\nexpensive _Base_ fare is refundable (and even then not 100%).  _Viva_\nfares include free seat\nselection from a seat map, Trenitalia charge €3 for this.  _Viva_\nfares also include\naccess to [ItaliaRail's lounge at Roma Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm#italiarail-lounge).\n\n**_Primo_** offers\nexactly the same features as _Viva_, but adds entry to major attractions in Rome, Florence &\nVenice such as the Colosseum, Pantheon or a gondola ride and some key\ntravel-related discounts.  Only buy the _Primo_ fare if those add-ons\nmake it worthwhile.\n\nOn a\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm),\nItaliarail refers to standard, premium, business & executive classes as _standard, premium, 1st & VIP_.\n\n\nHigh-speed & intercity trains are ticketless, you print your\nbooking reference or can show it on your phone.  For regional trains you print your own\nticket, show them in an app or in a few cases collect it from\n[the self-service \\\\\nmachines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines) at a Trenitalia station.\n\n- **Option 2, buy at** [**Thetrainline.com**](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm)\n\nThetrainline also connects to\nTrenitalia's system to sell tickets in plain English at the same prices as\nTrenitalia, in €, £, $, Ca$ or Au$, overseas credit cards no problem, small\nbooking fee.\n\nFor an extra €4,\nyou can choose your seats from a seat map on Trenitalia high-speed & intercity\ntrains.\n\n[Thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) has two key\nadvantages:\n\n\nFirst, it sells tickets for [Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm)\nas well as Trenitalia, so you can compare times & prices\nfor both operators.\n\n\nSecond, it also links to the French, Spanish, Swiss, German, Austrian & Benelux\nnational ticketing systems so you can book train tickets across much of western\nEurope together all in one place.\n\n\nHigh-speed & intercity trains are ticketless, you simply print your booking reference or show it on your phone.\nFor regional trains you print your own ticket, can show them in an app or in some cases collect them from\n[the self-service \\\\\nmachines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines) at a Trenitalia station.  _[Who are \\_\\\n_Thetrainline.com?](https://www.seat61.com/websites/who-are-trainline.htm)_\n\n\n- **Option 3, buy at** [**Raileurope.com**](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm)\n\nYou can also buy Trenitalia &\nItalo tickets at\n[www.raileurope.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm),\nalso in plain English with prices in €, £ or $, small booking fee.  It also connects to the French, German,\nAustrian, Spanish, British ticketing systems (but not the Swiss) so\ncan sell train tickets for much of western Europe all in one place.  Again,\nhigh-speed & intercity trains are ticketless so you simply print out your\nbooking reference or show it on your phone, for regional trains you usually print your own\nticket or collect tickets from\n[the self-service \\\\\nmachines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines) at any main Trenitalia station.  _[Who are \\_\\\n_Raileurope.com?](https://www.seat61.com/websites/who-are-raileurope.htm)_\n\n\n- **Option 4, buy at Trenitalia.com**\n\nYou can of course buy Italian train tickets direct from Italian Railways at\nwww.trenitalia.com, English button at the top,\nonly in €.\nIt's pretty easy to use but you'll need to use\nItalian-language place names (for example, Firenze or Venezia) and it has a few quirky translations & processes\nespecially when booking sleepers or international trains so\n[see the \\\\\nstep-by-step guide](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#how-to-use-trenitalia.com).\n\nHigh-speed & intercity trains are\nticketless _,_ you print your booking reference or show it on your phone, other tickets can be\nprinted or collected at any main Italian station from\n[the self-service machines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines).\nIt also offers seat selection for €3 on high-speed & intercity trains.\n\nObviously, they don't sell\ntickets for their competitor\n[Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm), or for other\nEuropean trains outside Italy.\n\n\n### Buying tickets at the station\n\n- It's easy to buy tickets at the\nstation on the day of travel, even if you don't speak\nItalian.  You can buy at the staffed ticket office counters, but you may\nhave to queue. It's usually much quicker to use one of the many _Self service_ machines\ninstalled at all main stations, these have a touch screen with an English\nlanguage facility,\n[see an illustrated \\\\\nguide to using the ticket machines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines).\n\nThe machines sell both\nregional and long-distance tickets including seat reservations for\nlong-distance trains (but unfortunately not Interrail passholder\nreservations), for any date you like in the next 90 days.  They\ntake Visa & MasterCard, but your card needs to have a PIN code.\n\n_Tip:_  You can also\nbuy or change tickets or make passholder reservations for Trenitalia trains\nleaving in the next half hour or so at any of the little mobile _Last Minute_\ndesks _(servizi last minute)_ on the concourse.  Much quicker than\nqueuing at the ticket office!\n\n\n- Trains seldom sell out, finding\ntickets even on the day of travel isn't a problem unless you hit a major holiday\nperiod.  If one train is full, the next will have seats.  Just\nremember that high-speed & Intercity trains are cheaper booked in advance, just\nlike flights.  If you buy on the day, you will have to pay the\n['Base' fare](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#types-of-fare), in other words the top tier flexible\nprice.\n\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Self-service ticket machines](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-ticket-machines.jpg) |  | ![Last Minute desks ](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-last-minute-desk2.jpg) |\n| [Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm) & Trenitalia ticket machines.<br>[How to use them](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines). |  | _Last Minute_ desks at<br>[Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm). _Photo [DiscoverByRail.com](https://discoverbyrail.com/)_ |\n\n### How to buy international  tickets\n\n- **The easy way**\n\n\nThe easiest option is to use either [www.thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm)\nor\n[www.raileurope.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm) as they\ncan book most international journeys to/from Italy.\n\nBoth sites connect to the\nTrenitalia, SNCF (French), SBB (Swiss), ÖBB (Austrian) and DB (German) ticketing\nsystems so they can book most routes to/from Italy including any Italian\ndomestic connecting trains.\n\nThey're very easy to use, you can\nbook in €, £ or $, international credit cards are no problem.  There's a small booking\nfee.\n\n[Thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm)\nallows you to select seats from a seat map on Trenitalia's high-speed &\nIntercity trains, and on\n[French \\\\\nTGVs](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/paris-to-milan-by-train.htm) in 1st class.\n\nFor more specific information on\ninternational trains from Italy to other European cities and how to book them,\nclick on your starting city:\n\n[Rome](https://www.seat61.com/international-trains/trains-from-Rome.htm) [Naples](https://www.seat61.com/international-trains/trains-from-Naples.htm) [Florence](https://www.seat61.com/international-trains/trains-from-Florence.htm) [Venice](https://www.seat61.com/international-trains/trains-from-Venice.htm) [Milan](https://www.seat61.com/international-trains/trains-from-Milan.htm)\n\n\n- **The advanced way**\n\nAlternatively, you can book with\nthe relevant operator, usually with no booking fee, but you need to know which operator runs which route.\nYou'll often need to book any connecting\ntrains separately, as most operators can't book each other's trains.\n\n**To/from Switzerland**\n\nThe direct\n[EuroCity trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/switzerland-to-italy-by-train.htm) between Milan and\nBrig, Lausanne, Geneva, Lugano, Luzern, Bern, Basel & Zurich can\nbe booked at either\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\nBoth sites can book from anywhere in Italy to any Swiss station\nthat is _directly_ served by the\n[EuroCity trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/switzerland-to-italy-by-train.htm)\nfrom Milan.  But they can't book onward tickets within\nSwitzerland such as Brig to Zermatt, Arth-Goldau to Luzern or Spiez to\nInterlaken, so buy those separately from Swiss Railways at\n[www.sbb.ch](https://www.sbb.ch/).\n\n**To/from Paris**\n\nThe\n[Frecciarossa trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/paris-to-milan-by-train.htm#Whats_the_Frecciarossa_like) between Milan/Turin & Lyon/Paris can be booked at either\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).  Both sites\ncan book from anywhere in Italy to Lyon or Paris.  However, they can't book\nonward French trains to other French cities (or to London or Brussels) so you'll\nneed to book those separately at [www.sncf-connect.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/sncf.htm).\n\nThe\n[French TGV trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/paris-to-milan-by-train.htm#Whats_it_like_on_board_the_TGV) between Milan, Turin & Paris can be booked at the French\nRailways website [www.sncf-connect.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/sncf.htm)\nwith no booking fee.  It allows you to choose a seat from a seat map in 1st\nclass, too.  However, it cannot book connecting Trenitalia trains within\nItaly, so you'll need to book those separately at either\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\n**To Nice, Cannes, Monaco or**\n**Marseille**\n\nFirst book from anywhere in Italy\nto Ventimiglia (on the French border where Trenitalia's trains terminate) at\neither [www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).  Trenitalia\ncan't sell an onward French ticket, so buy\nyour onward ticket from Ventimiglia to any French destination at the French\nRailways website [www.sncf-connect.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/sncf.htm).\nThere's more about the Italy-Nice route on the\n[Italy to \\\\\nNice page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/nice-to-italy-by-train.htm).\n\n**To Innsbruck, Munich & Germany**\n**by daytime trains**\n\nThe\n[railjet trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/munich-to-venice-by-railjet-train.htm) between Bologna/Venice/Verona and Innsbruck or Munich can be\nbooked at the German Railways website\n[int.bahn.de](https://www.awin1.com/awclick.php?gid=425395&mid=14964&awinaffid=92204&linkid=2476039&clickref=)\nwith no booking fee, this can book tickets from the EuroCity train's starting\nstations to anywhere in Germany, but it cannot book connecting Trenitalia trains\nwithin Italy (at least not in their main system with through fares), so book\nthose separately at either\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\n**To Vienna by daytime trains**\n\nThe\n[railjet trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/vienna-to-venice-by-train.htm) between Venice and Vienna can be booked at the Austrian\nRailways website [www.oebb.at](https://www.seat61.com/websites/oebb.htm)\nwith no booking fee, this can book tickets from Venice to anywhere in Austria,\nbut it cannot book connecting Trenitalia trains within Italy, so book those\nseparately at either\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\n**To**\n**Munich & Vienna by Nightjet sleeper train**\n\nThe\n[Nightjet sleeper trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/nightjet.htm)\nbetween Rome, Florence, Genoa, Milan, Venice and Munich or Vienna can be booked\nat the Austrian Railways website [www.oebb.at](https://www.seat61.com/websites/oebb.htm), but this can't\nbook connecting trains within Italy, so book those separately at\neither [www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n## CartaFRECCIA loyalty scheme\n\n- CartaFRECCIA is\nTrenitalia's loyalty scheme, free to join.\n\n- **For most visitors,**\n**there's not much point in applying** as you probably won't clock up enough\npoints to make it worth it.  Basically, €1 spent = 1 point. 300 points =\n€10 off a train journey.  So only worth considering if do _lots_ of\ntrain travel, perhaps on multiple trips to Italy.\n\n- **However, if you are**\n**under 30 or over 60**, becoming a CartaFRECCIA member allows you to buy\nthe cheap _FrecciaYOUNG_ & _FrecciaSENIOR_ fares for\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm)\n& [Frecciargento](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciargento.htm) trains and the _Young_ & _Senior_ fares for\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca),\n[Intercity](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm) and\n[Intercity Notte](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-notte.htm) trains.\n\nBut here's a reality check:\nIf you're booking a month or more in advance, a normal advance-purchase\nSuper-Economy fare _without_ any youth or senior discount is often cheaper than the FRECCIAyoung/FRECCIAsenior\nfare.  So again, it may not be worth applying.\n\nOnly if travelling in the\nnext few weeks when normal advance-fares\nhave risen in price do the CartaFRECCIA youth/senior fares save\nmoney.  But the CartaFRECCIA youth/senior fares must be bought at least\n5 or 6 days in advance, so aren't any good for very short notice flexible\ntravelling.\n\nSo before applying for a CartaFRECCIA card, do some dummy bookings\nand compare the senior/youth prices with the normal Economy & Super-Economy\nfares.  Is it worth it?\n\nCartaFRECCIA points and\nyouth/senior fares do not apply to regional trains, only Frecce & Intercity.\n\n\n- **How to get a**\n**CartaFRECCIA card**\n\nGo to\n[www.trenitalia.com/it/cartafreccia.html](https://www.trenitalia.com/it/cartafreccia.html) (please\n[let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if that link changes).\n\nAt the time I write this it's\nin Italian with no English version, so use Google's Chrome browser, right-click\nand select _Translate to English_ or use Google Translate.  Click the\nred _Inscrivit a CartaFreccia_ button.\n\nFill in the form to sign up.  The normal process\nrequires an Italian address, as a foreigner you should tick the _I do not have_\n_a tax code_ box and use the _My residence is abroad_ toggle.\n\n- **Using a CartaFRECCIA**\n**card**\n\nYou can use a CartaFRECCIA\ncard when booking at [raileurope.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/raileurope.htm),\n[thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) or\n[trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/),\nbut not [italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U).\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n## Should you  buy a railpass?\n\n- If you just want to make one,\ntwo or perhaps three journeys from A\nto B, book point-to-point tickets in advance online, it's the cheapest & easiest option.  But\nfor a more\nextensive itinerary, consider a railpass.  A railpass allows you to stay\nflexible.\n\n\n### Interrail passes\n\n- **Global & one-country passes**\n\nYou can buy an\n[Interrail pass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm)\ngiving unlimited travel on Trenitalia trains all over Italy.  An\nInterrail **global pass**\ncovers most of Europe including Italy, a cheaper Interrail **one-country** pass\nonly covers Italy.\n\nInterrail passes cover all Trenitalia trains, high-speed, intercity,\nIntercity Notte and\nregional.  They don't cover\n[Italo trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm), or some\nsmall railways such as the Circumvesuviana Railway Naples-Sorrento.\n\n- **Reservation fees**\n\nInterrail passholders must\nreserve seats to travel on\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm),\n[Frecciargento](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciargento.htm) &\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca) trains, this costs €13 in addition to the cost of the pass.\nThe reservation fee for\n[Intercity trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm) is €3.\nFor [Intercity Notte sleeper trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-notte.htm),\n[see the \\\\\nreservation fees here](https://www.seat61.com/interrail-and-eurail-reservations.htm#Italy).\n\nYou can make passholder reservations\nonline [as \\\\\nexplained in the Italy section of the Interrail reservations page](https://www.seat61.com/interrail-and-eurail-reservations.htm#Italy).  You can also\nmake them at stations, but at  the staffed counters only, not the self-service machines.  Passholder\nreservations cannot be made at Trenitalia.com.\n\n- **Is a rail pass worth it?**\n\nPeople struggle with this,\nperhaps because they think point-to-point fares have one fixed price that can\nbe compared with a pass.  But point-to-point fares vary like air fares so\nyou have to check actual fares for your specific trains & dates.\nAnd it's not just about price, it's about how you value the flexibility of a\npass versus cheap but inflexible no-refunds no-changes tickets.  Here's how\nto make a decision:\n\nFirst go to\n[www.thetrainline.com/passes](https://prf.hn/click/camref:1011lIEk/[p_id:1011l6288]/destination:https/www.thetrainline.com/passes) and check the current price for a single-country\n[Interrail pass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm) for\nItaly for however many days you want.  Divide the cost by the number of days.\nThat's the cost-per-day.  Now add in the €13 reservation fee for a\nhigh-speed train.\n\nAt the time I write this, a 4-day\nItaly pass works out as €49 per day + €13 = €62 assuming you only make one\njourney per day.\n\nHere are some point-to-point\nfares for comparison:\n\nRome to Florence:  _Cheapest possible price,_ no refunds, no changes, €19.90.  Flexible\n'Base' fare €50.\n\nFlorence to Venice:  _Cheapest possible price_, no refunds, no changes, €19.90.  Flexible\n'Base' fare €57.\n\nNaples to Florence:  _Cheapest possible price_, no refunds, no changes, €29.90.  Flexible\n'Base' fare €76.\n\nRome to Venice:  _Cheapest_\n_possible price_, no refunds, no changes, €29.90.  Flexible 'Base' fare\n€92.\n\nDo you notice anything?\nIt's not a trick question!\n\nAs you can see, point-to-point\nfares _can_ be cheaper than a \\[pass + reservation fee\\], but (a) you get\nzero flexibility with the cheapest point-to-point prices, specified train only\nwith no changes allowed, no refunds and (b) I'm quoting the cheapest possible\nprice here, point-to-point fares vary like air fares and for your date or train\nusually cost _significantly more_ than the _cheapest possible_ price.\n\n\nIf you need flexibility, a \\[pass\\\n\\+ reservation fee\\] costs a little more than the full-flex _Base_ fare for\nshort hops such as Rome-Florence, but saves money on longer trips such as\nRome-Venice or Naples-Florence.\n\nI'm assuming one trip per day\nhere.  Passes give you unlimited trips and mileage, with complete\nflexibility.  As soon as you want to make multiple trips the same day (Rome\nto Florence and back, for example, or Rome to Florence then a side trip to Pisa\nand back to Florence), a pass makes sense.\n\nAlso, children under 12 qualify\nfor a free\n[Interrail pass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm) (but\nstill have to pay for reservations), so if\nyou have kids, that can swing it towards a pass.\n\n\n- [More about \\\\\nInterrail passes, with prices](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm).\n[More \\\\\nabout what Interrail passes cover](https://www.seat61.com/interrail-and-eurail-reservations.htm#Italy).\n\n\n### The Trenitalia Pass\n\n- Trenitalia also sell their\nown railpass called the Trenitalia Pass.  It can be bought by anyone resident outside Italy.\n\n- **How is it different from an Interrail pass?**\n\nUnlike an\n[Interrail \\\\\npass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm), it\nonly covers Trenitalia high-speed, Intercity & Intercity Notte sleeper trains.\nIt doesn't\ncover regional trains.\n\nUnlike an\n[Interrail \\\\\npass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm), it doesn't give you unlimited travel, you buy a specific number of journeys\n(3, 4, 7 or 10 trips) to be made within a set period of days, where a journey = one ride on one train.  If you\nwent Rome to Florence in the morning and back in the evening, that's 2 trips\non a Trenitalia Pass, but would be covered by just one day on an Interrail\npass.\n\nBut on the plus side, unlike with\nan [Interrail pass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm) there are no extra fees to pay for reservations, it's all included.\n\n- **Is a Trenitalia Pass**\n**cheaper than an Interrail pass?**\n\nA Trenitalia Pass for a given\nnumber of trips is significantly cheaper than a _global_ Interrail pass covering the same number of days.\n\nA Trenitalia pass is about\nthe same price as a _one-country_ Interrail pass for Italy\ncovering the same number of days, but as you don't need to pay €10 for every\nreservation, the Trenitalia pass works out cheaper.\n\nThat assumes you plan to use only\n_one_ train per day.  A 4-journey Trenitalia Pass gives 4\nindividual train rides, a 4-day Interrail pass gives unlimited rides, as many as you\ncan cram in over 4 days _._  If you're going to use multiple trains\nper day, an Interrail pass is a better deal.\n\n- **Is a Trenitalia Pass**\n**cheaper than point to point tickets?**\n\nA Trenitalia Pass saves money over\nthe fully-flexible _Base_ fare even for a series of short hops such as\nRome-Florence, Florence-Venice, Venice-Milan, especially if you are under 28\nso qualify for the youth pass.  But if you can book a few months in\nadvance and don't need any flexibility, a no-refunds no-changes\nadvance-purchase Super-Economy fare can still be cheaper than a Trenitalia Pass, check prices before\nbuying a pass.\n\n\n- **Trenitalia passes come in 4 sizes:**\n\n3 journeys within 7\nconsecutive days;\n\n4 journeys within 7\nconsecutive days;\n\n7 journeys within 15\nconsecutive days;\n\n10 journeys within 30\nconsecutive days.\n\n\n- **Trenitalia passes come in**\n**2 classes:**\n\n**Easy** \\- good for 2nd class,\nstandard class on\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm), seats or couchettes on [Intercity \\\\\nNotte trains](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#overnight-trains).\n\n**Comfort** \\- good for 1st class,\nbusiness class on\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm),\nseats, couchettes or double or triple sleepers on\n[Intercity Notte trains](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#overnight-trains) trains.\n\nExecutive for executive\nclass has been discontinued.\n\n- **Trenitalia passes come in**\n**3 passenger types:**\n\nAdult - up to 2 children under 12 can be added to an adult pass for free (but infants under 4 go\nfree anyway, of course)\n\nYouth - anyone under 28 on\nday of purchase.\n\nSenior - anyone over 60 on\nday of purchase.\n\nFor more details and\nall the small print, see [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) and look for\n_Trenitalia Pass_.\n\n\n- **How to buy a Trenitalia pass**\n\nYou don't need to specify dates\nwhen buying a Trenitalia pass, you can book your first train for travel on any\ndate up to 11 months afterwards.\n\nGo to the\n[www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) home page and\nswitch it to English top right.\n\nClick _Advanced search_\nabove the journey planner.\nClick _Carnet_.  Then look for & click _Trenitalia pass_.\n\nAfter buying the pass, you're emailed your pass number.  The pass is entirely electronic.\n\n_Tip:_  I recommend registering for an account at Trenitalia.com before buying a pass.  Log\ninto your account before buying.\n\n\n- **How to book trains with a**\n**Trenitalia Pass**\n\nSeat reservations are needed on\neach train, but they are free and can be made online at Trenitalia.com or at\nstations.\n\nGo to the\n[www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) home page and switch\nit to English top right.\n\nClick _Advanced search_\nabove the journey planner.\nClick _Carnet_.  Then click _Trenitalia pass._\nThen click _Book_.\n\nEnter you pass number, first &\nlast name of the passholder, hit _Confirm_ and book a train with your pass.\n\nThe pass validity period starts\nticking from the date of the first train you book.  So don't book your _second_ train first then try to book another train for the previous day, that\nwon't work.  You must book your trains in the order you'll take them!\n\nReservations have the same change\nconditions as a _Base_ fare.  So you can cancel or change a\nreservation any time up to 60 minutes after the departure of the train.\nIf you don't do that, 60 minutes after departure that journey is considered\n'used up' even if you missed the train.\n\nWhen travelling, the pass can be\nshown on your phone or printed out.\n\n[Feedback](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) from buying & booking trains\nusing a Trenitalia pass would be appreciated, as without buying a pass I cannot\ntest the process myself.\n\n\n### Italia In Tour, for regional trains\n\n- **Italia In Tour passes**\nare available for 3 or 5 consecutive days unlimited travel on **Trenitalia**\n**regional trains all over Italy**.\n\nGood for any Trenitalia\nregional, regional express & metropolitan train.  But _not_\nvalid (1) between La Spezia &\nLevanto through Cinque Terre, (2) within the territory of Lombardy, (3), the\nprovinces of Bolzano and from Trento to Bassano del Grappa.  It can't be\nused on other operators' trains such as Trenord, so not valid\nbetween Milan & Tirano, for example.\n\nIt'd be ideal\nfor a series of day trips from Florence to Siena, Luca, Pisa on successive\ndays.  Or you could use it to work your way right across Italy\non regional trains.  With this pass you can just hop on and off Trenitalia regional trains, as you like.\n\nAt the time I write this, it\ncosts €35 for 3 days and €59 for 5 days, check latest prices online.\n\nYou must buy before midnight the\nday before it starts, you cannot buy on the day.\n\n\n- To see prices and buy a pass,\nsee\n[www.trenitalia.com/en/offers/italia-in-tour.html](https://www.trenitalia.com/en/offers/italia-in-tour.html) (please\n[let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if that link stops\nworking).\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n## Tours of Italy by train\n\n- of\nsuggested tours which can be varied or customised to your own requirements.\nAnd as you're booking a package, they'll take care of you if anything happens to\none part of the itinerary such as a strike or delay.  They have offices\nin the UK, USA & Australia.\n\n![UK flag](https://www.seat61.com/images/Flag-UK.jpg)\nIn the UK call 0207 864 [Byway.travel](https://www.byway.travel/?utm_source=seat61&utm_medium=affiliate)) is a new UK-based\neco-holiday firm with a\n[5-star \\\\\nTrustPilot rating](https://uk.trustpilot.com/review/byway.travel).  If you're nervous about booking train travel\nyourself, they'll book a trip for you as a package, including\novernight hotels.  Byway includes package\nprotection, Covid refund guarantee, free disruption & re-planning and\non-demand WhatsApp support while you're away.\n\nBrowse their pre-configured packages\nto Italy by train at\n[www.byway.travel/.../italy-by-train](https://www.byway.travel/destination/italy/italy-by-train?utm_source=seat61&utm_medium=affiliate).\n\nOr ask them to build a trip to your requirements,\ncall
