1461** and **quote seat 61** when booking.  From\noutside the UK call **+44 20 3778 1461**.  Lines open 09:00-17:30\nMonday-Friday.  Their website is\n[www.tailormaderail.com/destinations/italy](https://www.tailormaderail.com/destinations/italy/).\n\n\n### Expert individual trip planning  & advice\n\n- [DiscoverByRail.com](https://discoverbyrail.com/train-travel-architect-service/)\noffers an expert 'travel architect' service to plan trains around Europe, with\nsuggestions for routes, trains, hotels to your own specification.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## What  are Italian trains like?\n\n### Frecciarossa: _[See \\_ _the Frecciarossa guide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm)_\n\nTrenitalia's top high-speed trains are the _Frecciarossas (red arrows)_,\nmostly operated either by the original 300 km/h (186 mph)\n[Frecciarossa 500](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-500) trains or the latest\n[Frecciarossa 1000](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-1000)\ntrains\nintroduced in 2015.  Some are operated by 250 km/h (155 mph)\n[Frecciarossa 700](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-700) &\n[Frecciarossa 600](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-600)\ntrains.\n\nFrecciarossas have 3 or 4 classes of accommodation, a cafe-bar, power\nsockets at all seas & free WiFi, [s](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) [ee the Frecciarossa \\\\\npage for more information](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm).\n\nTickets for all Trenitalia's long-distance trains include a seat\nreservation and are only valid on the specific date & train you've\nbooked.\n\n**Principal Frecciarossa routes:**\nTurin-Milan-Bologna-Florence-Rome-Naples-Salerno ( [Frecciarossa 1000](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-1000)\n& [500](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-500));\nVenice-Florence-Rome-Naples ( [Frecciarossa 1000](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-1000)\n& [500](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-500));  Turin-Milan-Verona-Venice\n(usually\n[Frecciarossa 700](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-700));  Milan-Ancona-Bari-Brindisi-Lecce ( [Frecciarossa 500](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm#frecciarossa-500)).  [See seat \\\\\nmaps](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-seat-maps.htm#Italy).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Frecciarossa 1000 at Roma Termini](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciarossa1000-train.jpg) |\n| Frecciarossa 1000 at Rome <br>Termini. |\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Frecciarossa 500 at Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciarossa500-train.jpg) |\n| Frecciarossa 500 at<br>[Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm). |\n\n### Frecciargento:  _[See the Frecciargento \\_ _guide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciargento.htm)_\n\nNext down the pecking order are Trenitalia's 250km/h (155mph) _Frecciargento_\n(silver arrow)\ntilting trains, although these are now rare as most are being rebranded as Frecciarossa.  Frecciargento\nservices are operated by _pendolino_\ntilting trains,\nair-conditioned with cafe-bar, power sockets at all seats & free WiFi.  The trains reach 250 km/h on the high-speed lines\nand use their tilt to cut journey times through curves when running on classic\nlines.\n\nTickets for all Trenitalia's long-distance trains include a seat\nreservation and are only valid on the specific date & train you've\nbooked.\n\n**Principal remaining Frecciargento routes:**\nRome-Bari, plus the odd Genoa-La\nSpezia-Pisa-Rome trains.\n[See seat maps](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-seat-maps.htm#Italy).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Italian ETR600 train at Venice](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-frecciargento.jpg) |\n| An ETR600 Frecciargento. |\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| **[![Frecciargento train 2nd class seats](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciargento-train-2nd.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciargento-train-2nd-large.jpg)** |  | **[![Frecciargento train 1st class seats](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciargento-train-1st.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/frecciargento-train-1st-large.jpg)** |\n| 2nd classseats. |  | 1st classseats. |\n\n### Frecciabianca\n\nOne step down from Frecciarossa\nand Frecciargento are the _Frecciabianca_ (white arrow) services, now\ngetting very rare. The remaining Frecciabianca services on the Rome-Pisa-La\nSpezia-Genoa route are operated by older ETR460 tilting trains bumped off\nFrecciargento service.  They have 1st & 2nd class, power sockets at seats &\nfree WiFi.\nTickets include a seat\nreservation and are only valid on the specific date & train you've\nbooked.\n\n**Principal remaining Frecciabianca route:**\nMilan-Genoa-La Spezia-Pisa-Rome.\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-frecciabianca.jpg) |\n\n### Intercity  trains:  _[See \\_ _the IC guide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm)_\n\nNext in the\npecking order are the Intercity trains, fast trains\nhauled by locomotives at up to 160 km/h (100 mph), sometimes 200 km/h (125 mph).\nMost Intercity cars are\nopen-plan with a centre aisle, a few are classic side-corridor-and-compartment\ncars with 6-seat compartments, though you don't always get both sorts in both\nclasses on a given train.\nSome Intercity trains have a cafe counter, some just vending machines selling\ndrinks and snacks.  You're free to bring your own food & drink, even a\nbottle of wine if you like.\n[See \\\\\nthe Trenitalia Intercity page for more information](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm).\n\nTickets for all Trenitalia's long-distance trains include a seat\nreservation and are only valid on the specific date & train you've\nbooked.\n\n**Principal Intercity routes:**\nRome-Naples-Sicily;  Rome-Livorno-Pisa-Cinque\nTerre-La Spezia-Genoa-Milan;  Milan-Genoa-Savona-Ventimiglia (for\nNice).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Italian Intercity train](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-intercity-train-milan3.jpg) |\n| An InterCity train at Milan <br>Centrale. |\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| [![InterCity train, 2nd class](https://www.seat61.com/images/Trenitalia-intercity-2nd.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/Trenitalia-intercity-2nd-large.jpg) |  | [![InterCity train, 1st class](https://www.seat61.com/images/Trenitalia-intercity-1st.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/Trenitalia-intercity-1st-large.jpg) |\n| 2nd class on an Intercity train.  _[Larger photo](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-intercity-2nd-large.jpg)._ |  | 1st class on an Intercity train.  _[Larger photo](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-intercity-1st-large.jpg)_. |\n\n### Intercity Notte sleeper trains: _[See \\_ _the ICN guide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-notte.htm)_\n\nComfortable Intercity Notte (ICN) overnight trains\nlink Milan, Bologna, Rome, Naples with Messina, Palermo, Catania & Siracuse on\nSicily, [see the \\\\\nTrains to Sicily page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trains-to-sicily.htm).\n\nIntercity Notte overnight trains\nalso link Trieste/Venice with Rome, Turin/Milan with Naples, and Turin/Milan\nwith Bari, Brindisi & Lecce,\n[see \\\\\nthe Intercity Notte page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-notte.htm).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Sleeping-car on an Intercity Notte](https://www.seat61.com/images/intercity-notte2.jpg) |\n| Intercity Notte sleeper train at <br>Messina on Sicily. |\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| [![Intercity Notte comfort couchette](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-icn-couchette.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/icn-comfort-night-large.jpg) |  | [![Intercity Notte 2-bed sleeper](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-icn-sleeper.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/icn-double-sleeper-large.jpg) |\n| 4-berth Comfort couchettes. |  | 2-bed sleeper. |\n\n### Regionale & Regionale Veloce  (R, RV)\n\nRegional trains come in many\ndifferent shapes & sizes, they operate all over Italy including Florence-Pisa,\nFlorence-Siena, Florence-Lucca, Venice-Trieste, Venice-Verona, Rome-Civitavecchia.  On\nregional trains there are no assigned seats, you sit where you like.\nLuggage goes on the racks or simply on the floor.  There's no catering, so\nbring your own food & drink.  Many are 2nd class only.\n\nThere's\nlittle point in paying for regional trains in advance as there's just one cheap fixed-price _Ordinaria_\nfare which can be bought on the day.  Buy a ticket from the\nticket office or self-service\nmachines or buy online or in the Trenitalia app.\n[Interrail](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm) passholders\ncan just hop on, nothing more to do or pay.\n\n**How online tickets for**\n**regional trains work:**\n\nIn August 2023, Trenitalia made a\nsimple thing unnecessarily complicated.  For tickets bought online, the old\nsystem of having to stamp your ticket in a validator is gone, as is the system\nof tickets only being good for a 4-hour time slot.\n\nRegional tickets bought online are now only good for\nthe specific train you select, but you can change this free of charge as follows:\n\nUntil 23:59 on the day before\ndeparture, you can change the date and/or\ndeparture time free of charge using the Trenitalia app or\nwebsite, as many times as you like.  Tickets are 80% refundable.\n\nFrom 00:00 on the day of travel,\ntickets become non-refundable, but you can change the\ndeparture time\nas many times as you like free of charge until 23:59 or the last train,\nwhichever is sooner.\n\nHowever, at the departure time of\nwhatever train is currently selected, the ticket is considered 'used' and no\nfurther changes can be made.  Obviously,\nthis means that if you decide to take a later train you must change your\ndeparture time in the app _before_ your original train leaves!\n\nOnline regional tickets bought\nfrom a retailer such as\n[www.thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) or\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nalso work in this way, you'll get a link to make changes to the\ndate & time.  You show the QR code on your phone or can print the\nconfirmation email with the codes on.\n\n**Can regional trains sell out?**\n\nTickets are usually available in unlimited numbers\nso regional trains can't sell out, for example Milan-Tirano, Florence-Siena or\nFlorence-Pisa.  However, Trenitalia now have _some_ regional routes such as Venice-Trieste where the number of tickets sold for each train is\nlimited so they can in theory sell out and occasionally do - even though specific seats aren't\nassigned.  This devious practice started during the pandemic and has\ncontinued.\n\nTo check, find the train on [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) and click the 'i' symbol for details.  If it says _non-prenotabile_,\nyou're fine, tickets are unlimited and can't sell out.  If it says _prenotabile_ ticket numbers are limited, so bear that in mind.  If you\nuse the Trenitalia app, you can see how many places are left on each train,\nusually many, which should reassure you.\n\n**Buying regional tickets at the station**\n\nYou can buy regional tickets at\nthe station on the day from a Trenitalia ticket machine or ticket office.\nThe ticket may be for a specific departure.  You must validate it by\nsticking the end of the ticket, blank side up, into one of the green validator\nmachines at the entrance to (or on) the platform, see the validator photo below.  _Convalidare sul retro_\non the front means _Validate other side!_\n\n**Using a contactless card:**\n**_Tap & Tap_**\n\nOn selected regional routes you\ndon't even need to buy a ticket, you can just tap any contactless bank card\nagainst a green validator machine at your starting station (see the photo below), it beeps and you can\nboard the train.  When you reach your destination, tap the same card on\nanother green validator machine and the full adult single fare will be deducted\nfrom your bank account.  You can only buy one adult ticket for one person\nthis way, only on specified routes, only for regional (R or RV) trains, only\nregular _Ordinaria_ price.  You cannot buy tickets for more than one\nperson, or child tickets, or first class.\n\nRoutes with Tap & Tap include\nFlorence-Pisa-Livorno and Venice Santa Lucia-Venice Mestre-Padua-Verona, for a list see\n[www.trenitalia.com/en/purchase/tap-and-tap.html](https://www.trenitalia.com/en/purchase/tap-and-tap.html) (please\n[let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if that link changes).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| [![Trenitalia ticket validator](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-validate-tickets.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-validate-tickets-large.jpg) |\n\n**Ticket validator/Tap & Tap**\n**point**:  If you buy from the ticket office or a ticket machine, you must\nvalidate a regional (R or RV train) ticket by sticking it blank side up into the\nblack slot.  This is also the validator for tap & tap:  Hold your\ncontactless bank card against the contactless reader in the middle.  _[Larger photo](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-validate-tickets-large.jpg)_.\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| **[![2nd class seats on a regional train from Florence to Pisa](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-rock-regional-train.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-rock-regional-train-large.jpg)** |  | **[![2nd class seats on a regional train from Florence to Pisa](https://www.seat61.com/images2/italy-rock-regional-train2.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-regional-trains2-large.jpg)** |\n\nA modern double-deck 'Rock' regional train\nat [Verona Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/verona-porta-nuova.htm),\noperating a Verona-Venice _Regionale Veloce_ service.\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| **![Regional train from Florence to Pisa](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-regional-trains.jpg)** |  | **[![2nd class seats on a regional train from Florence to Pisa](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-regional-trains2.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-regional-trains2-large.jpg)** |\n\nAn older regional train of the sort that runs from Florence to Pisa, Livorno\n& Siena.\n\n### _Italo_ high-speed trains:  _[See \\_ _the Italo \\_ _guide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm)_\n\nPrivate\noperator NTV (Nuovo Trasporto Viaggiatori) started operating its _Italo_\nhigh-speed trains on the\nMilan-Bologna-Florence-Rome-Naples route in 2012, and now competes with\nTrenitalia on the Turin-Milan-Verona-Venice and Venice-Florence-Rome-Naples\nroutes as well.  Competition between Trenitalia and Italo has driven up\nquality, increased capacity and driven down fares.  It's well worth\nconsidering Italo for a journey between the main Italian cities.\n\n[See the Seat61 \\\\\nItalo information page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm) for more information,\nan illustrated guide and the\n[Italo video \\\\\nguide](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm#Italo_-_video_guide).\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ![Italo AGV train at Venice Santa Lucia](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italo-agv-train.jpg) |\n\nAn Italo AGV train at\n[Rome Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm).\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## Travel tips\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Trenitalia ticket machines](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-ticket-machines.jpg)<br>**Multi-lingual self-service ticket machines** are found at all main Italian <br>stations.  Click the UK flag on the touch screen for English.  <br>They can sell tickets for both regional & mainline trains, plus some international trains.  They take cash & credit cards.  Easier than going to the ticket office!<br>_**[How to use these machines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#ticket-machines)**_ |\n|  | ![Luggage between the seat backs on an Italian train](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-luggage.jpg)<br>Medium-sized suitcases & backpacks fit between the seat backs on a Frecciarossa.  <br>There are also racks above your seat, and at the car ends. |\n\n- **1st or 2nd**\n**class?**\n\n2nd class (or standard class\non [Frecciarossas](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#trains)) is\nabsolutely fine.  It's very comfortable and there's plenty of luggage space,\nthere's\nno need to pay for 1st class (or business class on\n[Frecciarossas](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#trains)) if you are on a\nbudget.  There are very few peasants & chickens in 2nd class on European\ntrains these days...\n\nOn the other hand, 1st (or business) class is nicer, with wider, plusher seats and a quieter environment with more laptops tapping\nand fewer noisy kids.  And with long-distance trains dynamically\npriced, it often costs only a little more to go 1st class if you book ahead.\n\n\n1st class seats are generally arranged 2+1 across the car width rather than 2+2, so you\nget solo seats (ideal for single travellers) and face-to-face tables for two on\none side of the aisle, ideal for couples as you then face each\nother and both get a window seat that is also an aisle seat, the best of both\nworlds.\n\nOn\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) trains you also get a complimentary\nglass of prosecco and a small snack box in business class.\n\n- **Luggage arrangements:** [See the luggage section](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#luggage) _._\n\n\n- **Do you need to validate your**\n**ticket?**\n\nTrenitalia's high-speed and intercity trains are ticketless,\nso no, as there's nothing to validate!\n\nSince August 2023,\nregional tickets bought online are only good for a specific departure, you can change\nthis as\nmany times as you like before the departure of the selected train,\n[see more about \\\\\nhow regional tickets work](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#regionale).  Again, there's nothing to validate.\n\n\nHowever, tickets for regional trains (R, RV etc.) bought from a ticket office or\nticket machine need to be validated before you board by sticking the end of the\nticket, blank side up, into one of the [green validator machines](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#green-validator)\nat the entrance to (or on) the platform.  This prints the time & date on\nthe ticket to show that it's used.\n\n\n- **Language problems**\n\nFirst-time visitors often think this will be a problem, but it hardly ever is.\nAt stations, finding your platform & train using the various departure screens\nis no different from finding your gate & plane at an airport.  Signs are\noften in English as well as Italian, or\npictograms are used.  On high-speed trains, announcements are usually\nrepeated in English.\n\nAt ticket offices clerks have a\npretty shrewd idea you want a train ticket not a packet of washing powder, in\nfact clerks in popular tourist cities will be used to dealing with Brits,\nAmericans & Australians and will usually know the relevant words in English\nabout one-ways & round trips, first or second class.  The ticket machines\nat every main station have a touch-screen with an English language facility.\n\nThe one thing that _does_\nhelp is knowing Italian place names:  Rome = Roma, Florence\n= Firenze, Venice = Venezia, Naples = Napoli, Milan = Milano, Turin = Torino,\nGenoa = Genova.\n\n\n- **Food & drink on trains in**\n**Italy**\n\nMost high-speed trains have a\ncafe-bar, although most Intercity trains (and Italo trains) only have vending\nmachines.  Feel\nfree to bring your own food and drink with you, even a bottle of wine if you like, no rules against that on the rails!\n\n\n- **Bicycles**\n\nYou can take\na bike with you on suburban, Regional & InterRegional trains if you buy a bike ticket costing\nabout €4.  You can also pay to reserve a bike space on most\n[Intercity trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm).  However, on high-speed trains such as Frecciarossas you need to\nput your\nbike in a zip-up bike bag, front wheel & pedals removed and handlebars turned,\n[see the bikes by train page](https://www.seat61.com/bike-by-train.htm).\n\n\n- **Dogs & pets**\n\nGo to\n[www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) and search\nunder _Services_ for _Transport of pets_.  You can take\npets on many\ntrains, but the rules vary slightly by type of train.  Very small dogs,\ncats & other pets in containers no bigger than 70cm x 30cm x 50cm are carried\nfree on almost all trains & classes.  Larger dogs on a lead & muzzled are\nallowed on almost all trains but must have a ticket bought for them at 50% of\nthe adult 2nd class fare (whatever class the owner is using) and they are not\npermitted in Executive or Premium classes on Frecciarossa trains, or in catering\ncars.  You can take a dog in sleepers or couchettes only if your party\noccupies the whole compartment.  Guide dogs are always free.\n\n\n- **First class lounges at Italian**\n**stations**\n\nExecutive class passengers & holders of Trenitalia's frequent traveller card can use the _FrecciaClub_ lounges at\nstations in major cities.  You can also enter with a ticket for a business\nclass _Salottino_ seat (but not a regular business class seat).\n\nClub class passengers on\n_[Italo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/italo.htm)_, can use\nthe Club Italo lounge at major city stations.  Prima class\npassengers can also use the lounge if they pay a €12 add-on when booking -\nthough this may not be offered at busy times or with the cheapest tickets.\n\n\n- **A train planner app for your**\n**phone**\n\n_Railplanner_ is a free\noffline train timetable app that you can download onto your phone to\ncheck train times, station departures & train calling points on the move\n_without the need to_\n_be on WiFi or to use mobile data_.  It's blisteringly quick and\ncovers not just Italy but most of Europe.  It highlights the\nreservation-required high-speed & InterCity trains in red and the no-reservation-required\nregional trains in green.  It's created with Interrail\npassholders in mind, but is useful for anyone. Download for iPhone or Android\nat\n[www.interrail.com/en/plan-your-trip/rail-planner-app](https://www.interrail.com/en/plan-your-trip/rail-planner-app) \\- please\n[let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if the link stops\nworking.\n\n\n- **Are the trains running on time?**\n\nYou can check real-time arrivals and departures at any Trenitalia station or the\nrunning of any train by train number at [www.viaggiatreno.it](https://www.viaggiatreno.it/).  Most regional trains run more or less on time,\nand so do most high-speed long-distance trains, with perhaps a 10 or 20\nminute delay here and there.  However, make allowances for a typical 30 to\n90 minute delay when catching the overnight sleeper trains to/from Sicily, for\nexample.\n\n\n### Choosing your seat\n\n- [Trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/),\n[Italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\n& [Thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm) allow you to\nchoose your seats from a seat map on\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm),\n[Frecciargento](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciargento.htm),\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca) and\n[Intercity trains](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trenitalia-intercity-trains.htm)\nwithin Italy.\n\n- It's pretty self-explanatory,\nbut as I'm often asked, yes, the grey bars are tables, and no, you can't\ntell which way seats face.  Indeed, most Naples-Rome-Venice and Naples-Rome-Milan\ntrains change direction at both\n[Rome Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm) and\n[Florence SMN \\\\\nstation](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm), both of which are dead-end terminus stations.\n\n- For a couple in 1st class I recommend a face-to-face table for two.\n\n\n\n![Choosing a seat on an Italian train](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-seat-plan.jpg)\n\n\n### Luggage on trains in Italy\n\n- Luggage is no\nreal problem on Italian trains, and it makes no real difference whether you go\n1st or 2nd class, there's always room for bags.\nYou don't check your bags in and there is no baggage car.  You simply take\nwhatever you like into the train with you, and stick your bags on the racks\nabove your head or on the big luggage racks at the end of each car or between the seat backs.\nOn regional trains, it just goes on the floor next to you if there aren't any\nracks.\n\n- There are no baggage fees or\nweight limits to worry about, for most practical purposes if you can carry it\nyou can bring it.  It's so simple, it hardly needs explaining, yet overseas\nvisitors chase their tails worrying about it.\nDon't over-think it, but don't travel with more than you really need.  Anything up to backpack-sized fits on the racks above your head, larger\nitems such as bulky suitcases go on the racks at the end of the car, in the\nseating area, or\nbetween the seat backs.\n\n- Security is not a major problem,\nyour bags full of clothing are no more likely to be stolen than airline checked\nbaggage.  There's no need to chain your bag to the rack, any more than\nyou'd chain you bags to the luggage bin on a plane.  Although I like to use a rack which I can see from my seat and I\nalways keep cameras, passports and so on in my daypack at my seat.\nBut I thought that was obvious?\n\n- Porters are pretty much a thing of\nthe past along with butlers, valets and ladies' maids.  However,\n[Milan \\\\\nCentrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm), [Milan Porta Garibaldi](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-porta-garibaldi.htm),\n[Venice Santa Lucia](https://www.seat61.com/stations/venice-santa-lucia.htm),\n[Rome Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm),\n[Florence SMN](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm),\n[Turin Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#Porta_Nuova)\nare all termini with level access to and between all trains.  You can just pull your\nbag on its wheels from street or taxi rank across the station concourse right up\nto the train\ndoor, lift it two steps up into the train, and wheel it to a convenient rack or space\nbetween the seats next to your seat.  If you're elderly or pregnant, another passenger will almost always help you get your bags the two\nshort steps into the train.\n\n\n- **Luggage storage at stations**\n\nAll main\nItalian stations including\n[Turin Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#Porta_Nuova),\n[Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm),\n[Verona Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/verona-porta-nuova.htm),\n[Venice Santa Lucia](https://www.seat61.com/stations/venice-santa-lucia.htm),\n[Florence SMN](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm),\n[Rome Stazione Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm) &\n[Naples Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm) have left-luggage facilities,\neither lockers or a staffed facility.\n[Information on \\\\\nleft-luggage prices & opening times](https://www.seat61.com/luggage-lockers-at-stations.htm).\n\n\n### Which station in which city?\n\n- **Rome**\n\n[Roma Termini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm) is the main\nstation in Rome, in the city centre walking distance from all the sights,\n[see the Roma Termini station \\\\\nguide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/rome-termini.htm).\n\nRoma Ostiense and Roma\nTiburtina are on the outskirts of the city, you'll need a local train or taxi (around €15)\ninto the city centre.  The Vatican has its own suburban\nstation, Roma San Pietro, but it’s easy to reach St Peter’s from the Stazione\nTermini by bus or taxi.\n\n- **Naples**\n\n[Napoli Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm) is\nthe main station in Naples city centre, co-located with the Circumvesuviana\nstation for trains to Pompeii & Sorrento and 30 minutes walk from the ferry\nterminal for Capri & Ischia.\n\n_Do not book to Napoli Afragola by mistake_ as I've known a number of\npeople do, Naples Afragola is a 'parkway' station for high-speed trains outside\nthe city in the middle of nowhere, aimed at motorists from surrounding towns &\nvillages.\n\n- **Venice**\n\n[Venezia Santa Lucia](https://www.seat61.com/stations/venice-santa-lucia.htm)\nis the main station in Venice, in the city of Venice itself on the banks of the\nGrand Canal, 15-25 minutes walk from the Rialto Bridge & St Mark's Square.\n[See the Venice Santa Lucia \\\\\nstation guide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/venice-santa-lucia.htm).\n\n[Venezia Mestre](https://www.seat61.com/stations/venice-santa-lucia.htm#venice-mestre) is on the mainland in an industrial area\noutside Venice itself, always book to Venice Santa Lucia unless you have a hotel\nin Mestre.\n\n- **Florence**\n\n[Firenze Santa Maria Novella](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm)\nis the main station in Florence, often abbreviated to SMN, in the city centre\neasy walking distance from all the sights.\n[See the Florence SMN \\\\\nstation guide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/florence-santa-maria-novella.htm).  A few trains use Campo Marte or Rifredi stations\noutside the city centre, linked to SMN by frequent local trains.\n\n- **Milan**\n\n[Milano Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm) is the main station\nin Milan, a magnificent terminus in the city centre, served\nby most mainline & international trains.  It's a major Milan attraction in\nits own right, see if you can\n[spot Mussolini](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm#Can_you_spot_Mussolini).\n[See the Milan Centrale \\\\\nstation guide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm).\n\n[Milan Porta Garibaldi](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-porta-garibaldi.htm) is\nrather less magnificent, but still\ncentral, used by the\nFrench Railways\n[TGVs to Paris](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/paris-to-milan-by-train.htm)\nand by some Trenitalia trains.  It's a 25 minute walk, 5 minute metro ride\nor 8 minute taxi ride from Centrale.\n\nSome Malpensa airport trains arrive\nat Milan Cadorna, a small local terminus also located in the city centre,\nother Malpensa airport trains run to\n[Milan Porta Garibaldi](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-porta-garibaldi.htm) and\n[Milan \\\\\nCentrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm).  Milan Lambrate is much less central, and\n[Milan Rogoredo](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-rogoredo.htm) is 5 km\nfrom the city centre.\n\n- **Turin**\n\n[Torino Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#Porta_Nuova)\nis the main station, a large and historic terminus,\n[see \\\\\nthe Turin stations guide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#Porta_Nuova).\n\nHowever,\ntrains to & from Paris use Turin's other main station,\n[Turin Porta Susa](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#turin-porta-susa), and most\ntrains to or from Rome, Milan or Venice call at\n[Porta Susa](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#turin-porta-susa)\nbefore or after\n[Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/turin.htm#Porta_Nuova).\nBoth stations are in Turin's city centre, walking distance from all the sights.\n\n- **Siena**\n\nIn Siena, the station is\nat the foot of the hill and used to be a steep trek up to the old town.\nHowever, there's now a series of modern escalators and moving walkways that\nferry you almost painlessly to the top of the hill, from where it's just a\nminute or two's walk to the Porta Camollia at the entrance to the old town.\nFrom the Porta Camollia it's a pleasant 15 minute stroll to the famous Piazza del\nCampo.\n\n- **Leaning Tower of Pisa**\n\n[Map of Pisa showing tower & railway stations](https://www.google.com/maps/d/edit?mid=zfgnXI1FPqHg.kqsBCMRWkr_A).  Many people do\nPisa as a day trip from Florence, using the frequent local\ntrains.  Pisa Centrale is 2km from the Tower, a 30 minute walk, but\nif you take a train to Pisa S. Rossore station it's only a 5-10 minute walk\nto the Tower.  Some trains from Florence go direct to Pisa S. Rossore, 1\nstop beyond Pisa Centrale, others require a change at Pisa Centrale.  Check\ntrain times using [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) or\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U).\n\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Train going onto the ferry to Sicily](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ferry-sicily2.jpg) |\n|  | **Sicily Express!**  <br>The sleeper train from Milan to Palermo is shunted off the ferry at <br>Messina.  Yes, the trains to Sicily really are direct, they are put <br>on a ferry to cross the straits from Villa San Giovanni to Messina. _Photo courtesy of David Smith._<br>_[Watch the \\_<br>_video here](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9grLtdPWYhg)._ |\n|  | ![Naples-Pompeii-Sorrento circumvesuviana train](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-sorrento-train.jpg) |\n|  | **A**<br>**Naples-Pompeii-Sorrento train** on the Circumvesuviana Railway, every 30 <br>minutes for just a few euros. |\n\n### How to travel to Sicily\n\n- The train is a wonderful way to\nreach Sicily, an experience in itself.\n\n- There are two daytime\nInterCity trains from Rome & Naples to Palermo, Catania, Siracuse and\ntime-effective overnight sleeper trains from Milan, Rome & Naples direct to\nPalermo, Catania & Siracuse.\n[See the \\\\\ntimetable & information on the Trains to Sicily page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trains-to-sicily.htm).\n\n- If you use the daytime trains,\nbring a picnic and bottle of wine (as\nthere's no catering car) and enjoy the\nride, much of it along the Italian coast just a stone's throw from the sea\ntowards the toe of Italy.\n\n- All these trains are shunted onto a ferry at\nVilla san Giovanni for the short crossing of the Straits of Messina to\nSicily.  It's the last remaining place in Europe where passenger trains go onto a\nferry, a unique experience,\n[watch the \\\\\nvideo](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trains-to-sicily.htm#Watch_the_video).   Once the train is secured in the\nship's hold, steps are placed next to the train\ndoors, and you can either remain on board the train or get off and walk\nupstairs to the deck to take some sea air, returning to the train as the ferry\ndocks on the other side.  Highly recommended!  You can book all of these\ntrains to Sicily\n[as shown here](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/trains-to-sicily.htm#How_to_buy_tickets).\n\n- _Or use an overnight_\n_ferry from Naples_:  You can sail from Naples to Palermo by\ncomfortable overnight ferry, with a  or shared cabin with en suite shower & toilet,\nand there are restaurants and bars for an enjoyable evening on board.\nFerries typically sail every day at around 20:00 and\narrive around 06:30 in both directions.  See\n[www.tirrenia.it](https://www.tirrenia.it/) &\n[www.snav.it](https://www.snav.it/)\nfor times, dates, fares & online booking.\n\n\n### How to reach  Herculaneum, Pompeii & Sorrento\n\n- **Naples to Herculaneum, Pompeii**\n**& Sorrento by Circumvesuviana train**\n\nThe railway from Naples to Herculaneum, Pompeii &\nSorrento isn't run by Trenitalia, it's the privately-run Circumvesuviana\nRailway, [www.eavsrl.it](https://www.eavsrl.it/).  That's why you can't find trains to Sorrento on\nTrenitalia.com or Italiarail.com.\n[Map of Naples showing stations](https://maps.google.co.uk/maps/ms?msid=201915664237202451070.0004e2172a5588963a13d&msa=0&ll=40.844853,14.272699&spn=0.065903,0.132093).\n\nSimply buy a mainline ticket from Venice, Florence, Rome or\nwherever to Naples Centrale at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).  On arrival at Naples Centrale, follow the signs to\n_Circumvesuviana_, these will take you downstairs to the Circumvesuviana\nstation.\n\nThe Circumvesuviana station has\nits own ticket office, either buy a ticket to\nErcolano, Pompeii Scavi or Sorrento there or use the ticket gate that allows you\nto touch in with a contactless bank card.  Go through the automatic ticket gates onto\nthe platform and hop on the next train.\n\nTrains run to Herculaneum\n(Ercolano), Pompeii and Sorrento every 30 minutes throughout the day, no\nreservation is necessary or possible.  Outside the weekday rush hours the trains are not crowded, there are plenty of\nseats and it's very easy to use.  Luggage goes on the racks or just on the\nfloor, no problem.\n[This handy \\\\\nvideo shows you what to expect](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FU9U07u-UZM).\n\nNaples to Pompeii costs around €3.20 one-way,\njourney around 40 minutes.\n\nNaples to Sorrento costs\naround €4.50 one-way, journey 55-65 minutes.\n\n- **Naples to Herculaneum,**\n**Pompeii & Sorrento by Campania Express**\n\nIn addition to the regular Circumvesuviana trains, there are up to 4 daily\nair-conditioned all-reserved _Campania Express_\ntrains from\n[Naples Garibaldi \\\\\nstation](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm#circumvesuviana) to Ercolano, Pompeii Scavi & Sorrento, aimed at tourists.\nThese use exactly the same stations on the same line as the regular\nCircumvesuviana trains, but if you're\nhappy paying a higher fare and restricting yourself to these 4 departures,\nyou'll get a reserved seat, extra luggage space & air-con.  The fare is €15.  Check times\n& book\ntickets at\n[seat61.transport-ticket.com](https://seat61.transport-ticket.com/result?marketingCarrierCode=PEXR&retailerPartnerNumber=455015&locale=en&departureTime=06:00&%20&seatProduct=false&currency=EUR&departureStation=ITRRONGB&arrivalStation=ITSORSBA) (powered by Distribusion, which is who you're\nbuying from).\n\n_Update 2026:  The_\n_Campania Express will not run from 1 October 2026 until further notice, as EAV's_\n_new CEO apparently wants to concentrate on improving the Circumvesuviana_\n_Railway's normal service._\n\n\n- **Naples to Sorrento by ferry**\n\nYou can also travel from Naples Beverello ferry terminal to Sorrento by fast\nferry with around 5 departures a day, journey time 45 minutes, fare around €13,\nbags €2.10, see [www.alilauro.it](https://www.alilauro.it/).  You can buy online or just buy at the ferry terminal\non the day.\n\n\n- **To the top of Vesuvius**\n\nTo visit the summit of Vesuvius,\ntwo morning buses run from Naples or a more regular bus service runs from\nPompeii, both run by EAVBUS, see [www.eavbus.it](https://www.eavbus.it/) (Italian only) or (more usefully)\n[www.unicocampania.it](https://www.unicocampania.it/),\nclick English top right and look for 'Vesuvio Fares' under 'Tourist Info'.\n\n\n- **Day trip to Pompeii?** [It's easy to arrange \\\\\na trip to Pompeii yourself by train, see the \\\\\nguide here](https://www.seat61.com/places-of-interest/day-trip-to-pompeii.htm).\n\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Ferry from Naples to Capri](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-ferry-to-capri.jpg) |\n|  | The SNAV <br>fast ferry from Capri to Naples. |\n\n### How to reach Capri\n\n- Buy a mainline ticket from Venice, Florence, Rome or\nwherever to Naples Centrale at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).\n\n- The island of\nCapri is just off Sorrento.  You have two options:  You can take a\ndirect ferry from Naples to Capri, journey time around 45 minutes, fare €25, or you can\ntake the Circumvesuviana Railway to Sorrento (55-65 minutes) then a shorter\nferry crossing to Capri (around 25 minutes, fare around €20).\n\n- If you choose the ferry from\nNaples, take a taxi (5-10 minutes) or walk (25 minutes) from\n[Naples \\\\\nCentrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/naples-centrale.htm) to Naples Beverello ferry quay.  Fast ferries link Naples Berevello with Capri every hour or two between 07:00 &\n18:00 taking 45 minutes, see [www.snav.it](https://www.snav.it/)\nor [Omio.com](https://omio.sjv.io/c/146261/409973/7385) for times\n& fares.  The ferry fare is around €25 plus a euro or two per item of large\nluggage.  You don't need to pre-book, just turn up, buy a ticket\nand hop on.\n[Map of Naples showing station & ferry terminals](https://maps.google.co.uk/maps/ms?msid=201915664237202451070.0004e2172a5588963a13d&msa=0&ll=40.844853,14.272699&spn=0.065903,0.132093).\n\n- If you choose to take the local\nCircumvesuviana Railway to Sorrento, there are many ferries to Capri, no\npre-booking necessary.  Just be aware that it's a longish steep walk from\nSorrento Circumvesuviana station down the hill to the ferry terminal.\n\n- Ferries from both Naples &\nSorrento arrive at Capri's busy Marina Grande, there's a funicular railway up\nthe steep hillside to Capri town itself. Bring plenty of money to Capri, even a\nsmall beer costs over €7!\n\n\n### How to reach Ischia\n\n- Ferries link Naples with Porto\nIschia, see [www.caremar.it](https://www.caremar.it/) (sailings every hour or two, crossing\ntime 45 minutes fast ferry or 90 mins conventional ferry) or\n[www.alilauro.it](https://www.alilauro.it/).\n\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Bus to Amalfi](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-sita-bus-amalfi.jpg) |\n|  | SITA bus on the <br>narrow coast road between Amalfi and Sorrento. |\n\n### How to reach Amalfi, Positano,  Praiano\n\n- **Option 1, by bus from**\n**Salerno**\n\nThere's no railway to these towns on the famous Amalfi Coast, but buses run\nfrom Salerno to Amalfi.  So buy a train ticket for one of the many\nhigh-speed trains from Milan, Florence and Rome direct to Salerno, then hop on a bus to Amalfi.  This Salerno\nbus option is the quickest way to Amalfi.\n\nBuses link\nSalerno & Amalfi every hour or better between 06:00 &\n22:30 on Mondays-Saturdays, slightly less frequently on\nSundays, journey time 1h15, fare €2.80 one-way, buy a ticket at the\ntobacconists shop inside Salerno station.\n\nThe\nbuses are operated by SITA, to check bus times see\n[www.sitasudtrasporti.it](https://www.sitasudtrasporti.it/)\n(in Italian only, click _Orari_ then 'Campania').\n\nTo check fares,\nuse public transport site\n[www.unicocampania.it](https://www.unicocampania.it/),\nclick English top right then _Fares & Tickets_.\n\n\n- **Option 2, by ferry from Salerno**\n\nAlternatively, you can take a ferry from Salerno to Amalfi or\nPositano.  See\n[www.coopsantandrea.com](https://www.coopsantandrea.com/) for a timetable of small coastal\nferries from Salerno (Concordia dock, 800m from Salerno railway\nstation) to Amalfi and Positano.  At the time of\nwriting, they had departures from Salerno at 08:40, 09:40,\n10:40, 11:40, 14:10 and 15:30, journey time to Amalfi just\n35 minutes, but check their website for\ncurrent timings.  This is a good option in summer when the narrow coast\nroad is clogged with traffic.\n\n**Option 3, by bus from**\n**Sorrento**\n\nYou can also buy a train ticket to Naples, hop on the\nCircumvesuviana Railway to Sorrento\n[as shown above](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#How_to_reach_Herculaneum,_Pompeii_&_Sorrento) then\ntake a bus to Positano, Praiano & Amalfi.  For buses linking Sorrento with Positano,\nPraiano & Amalfi, see\n[www.sitasudtrasporti.it](https://www.sitasudtrasporti.it/) (in Italian only, click _Orari_ then _Campania_).  Sorrento-Amalfi takes 1 hour\n40\nminutes, buses run hourly or at certain times half-hourly 06:30 to 22:00, fare around\n€3.  The journey along the coast road is dramatic,\nthe bus hugging the cliff and it rear end swinging out\nprecariously at every hairpin bend!\n\n- **Option 4, a private transfer**\n**from Naples to Praiano, Positano or Amalfi hotels**\n\nIf cost\nis no object, a private car transfer from Naples Centrale railway station to\nPositano or Praiano costs around €95 one-way for up to 3 people, or around €110\nto Amalfi.\nTry [www.amalfishuttle.com](https://www.amalfishuttle.com/) or\n[www.positanoshuttle.com](https://www.positanoshuttle.com/) (same people - click 'transfer' at the top).  I\nhave not had any reports about them yet, so [feedback](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) would be appreciated.  They will also do transfers from Sorrento\nCircumvesuviana station, which reduces the cost.\n\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ![Bus to San Marino](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-bus-san-marino.jpg) |\n|  | The bus <br>from Rimini <br>to San Marino. |\n\n### How to reach Elba\n\n- Travel by train to Piombino Marittima.  Moby Lines ( [www.mobylines.com](https://www.mobylines.com/))\nsail every hour or so from Piombino to Portoferraio on Elba,\ncrossing time 1 hour, foot passengers €7 one-way.\n\n\n### How to reach Lake Como\n\n- **Como San Giovanni** is the mainline station\nfor Como, it's on the Zurich-Milan\nmain line and linked to [Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm) by frequent regional train.\n[Check times as above](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#how-to-check-schedules-and-fares).\n\n- **Lecco, Varenna & Bellanoare** on the eastern side of Lake Como, there is a station at Lecco & Varenna-Esino (for the ferry to Bellagio) and Bellano on\nthe Trenord local line linking\n[Milan Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/milan-centrale.htm) with Tirano\n(near the Swiss border, for the fabulous narrow-gauge\n[Bernina route](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/bernina-express.htm)\nto Chur & Zurich).  [Check times \\\\\nas above](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#how-to-check-schedules-and-fares)\n\n\n### How to reach Lake Garda\n\n- There are two main stations for Lake Garda, **Desenzano del Garda-Sirmione** or **Peschiera del Garda**,\nboth on the Milan-Verona-Venice main line.  As well as\nregional trains, some Milan-Verona-Venice\n[Frecciarossas](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) call at Peschiera or Desenzano.\n\n- Alternatively, take a train to\n[Verona Porta Nuova](https://www.seat61.com/stations/verona-porta-nuova.htm).\nBuses run frequently times an hour from outside the station to the _eastern_\nshores of Lake Garda including Lazise, Bardonlino & Garda, see the bus company\nwebsite [www.atv.verona.it](https://www.atv.verona.it/).\n\n- You can also travel to Brescia,\nthen take frequent buses to the _western_ shore of Lake Garda, see the bus\ncompany website [brescia.arriva.it](https://brescia.arriva.it/).\n\n\n### How to reach San Marino\n\n- San Marino has no rail\nstation, so first take a train to Rimini.\n\nBuses to San Marino leave from outside Rimini railway station\nevery hour or so between 08:10 & 19:25 in winter, between 06:45 & 20:30 in\nsummer, less frequently on winter Sundays, journey time 50 minutes.   Run by Bonelli Bus, the buses are\nair-conditioned with WiFi.\n\nThe fare is €7 one-way.  Buses often leave full, so pre-book if you can.\n\nTo reach the bus stop, walk out\nof Rimini station main exit and turn right, walk 170m, then cross to the far\nside of the road,\n[see walking \\\\\nmap](https://maps.app.goo.gl/sVArjntsc9bz9sLE9).\n\nIf you still need to buy a ticket\nwhen you get to Rimini, walk out of the station, cross the road and turn left.\nTwo doors beyond Burger King there is a tobacconist shop which sells bus\ntickets.\n\n- Buy tickets from Bonelli Bus at\n[www.bonellibus.it/en/shuttle-rimini-san-marino-2](https://www.bonellibus.it/en/shuttle-rimini-san-marino-2/) (please\n[let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if that\nlink changes).\n\n\n### How to reach Sardinia\n\n- **Ferries to Sardinia**\n\nFerries sail from Civitavecchia,\nLivorno, Naples & Palermo to various ports on Sardinia including Olbia & Golfo\nAranci in the north and Cagliariin the south.  Operators include Tirrenia,\nGrimaldi Lines, Corsica-Sardinia Ferries,\n[use Direct Ferries to check and book all ferry routes & operators](https://www.directferries.co.uk/?dfpid=394&affid=3&rurl=).\n\nThe shortest ferry crossing is\nCivitavecchia (just north of Rome) to Olbia where the daily daytime ferry takes\n5h30, and an overnight ferry with cabins takes 7h.  A daily ferry with\ncabins links Naples with Caligari overnight.\n\n\n- **Trains on**\n**Sardinia**\n\nRegional trains run by a division of\nTrenitalia link the major centres and ports:  Golfo Aranci, Olbia,\nSassari, Porto Torres, Oristano, Cagliari.  You can check train times\nat [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/)\nalthough as trains are classed as regional you may as well just buy tickets\nat the station on the day.\n\nIn addition to the main\nTrenitalia routes, tourist services called the _Little Green Trains_\nrun on two or three very rural routes, see\n[www.treninoverde.com](https://www.treninoverde.com/)\n(in Italian only, use Google translate) or\n[sardiniagreentrain.com](https://sardiniagreentrain.com/)\n(in English).  These trains\nare run by transport authority ARST, a company formed by merging the urban\ntransport authority with Sardinian Railways.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## Airport  train connections\n\n### Milan Malpensa airport\n\n- There are regional trains from\nMilan Malpensa airport to Milan Centrale every 30 minutes, journey time 52\nminutes, fare around €12, no reservation necessary or possible, just buy a\nticket at the station and hop on the next train.\n\nChange at Milan Centrale for high-speed\n[Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) &\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca) trains to Venice, Florence,\nRome, Naples or anywhere\nelse in Italy.  You can check times & fares at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/).  Reservation is required\nfor Italian long-distance & high-speed trains, make sure you read the tips\nbelow.\n\n\n### Rome Fiumicino airport\n\n- Leonardo Express trains run from\nRome Fiumicino airport to Rome Termini in the city centre every 30 minutes,\njourney time 32 minutes, fare around €14, no reservation necessary or possible,\njust buy a ticket at the station and hop on the next train.\n\nChange at Rome Termini for\nhigh-speed [Frecciarossa](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/frecciarossa.htm) &\n[Frecciargento](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Frecciargento_) trains to Venice, Florence, Naples\nor anywhere else in Italy - indeed, there's even a very occasional direct\nhigh-speed train from Rome Fiumicino Airport to Florence & Venice.\nYou can check times & fares at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) \\-\nReservation is required for Italian long-distance & high-speed trains, make sure\nyou read the tips below.\n\n\n### Pisa airport\n\n- A\npeople-mover (driverless train) shuttles passengers from Pisa airport to Pisa Centrale in the city\ncentre in just 8 minutes.  Change at Pisa Centrale for hourly regional trains to Florence, and\nregular regional, [Intercity](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#intercity-trains) or\n[Frecciabianca](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#frecciabianca) trains to Rome, La Spezia and Monterosso in Cinque Terre.  You\ncan check times & fares at [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/)\nselecting _Pisa fermata Aeroporto_ for Pisa airport station - but read the\ntips below.\n\n\n### Bologna airport\n\n- A people mover links\n[Bologna Centrale](https://www.seat61.com/stations/bologna-centrale.htm)\nwith Bologna airport, for more info\n[see the Bologna Centrale station guide](https://www.seat61.com/stations/bologna-centrale.htm#people-mover-to-bologna-airport).\n\n\n### _Tips for_ _buying plane-to-train tickets_\n\n- The regional trains from\nMalpensa into Milan, from Fiumicino into Rome or from Pisa to Florence are no problem - you can\nturn up, buy a ticket at the station & hop on the next train.  No reservation\nis possible and no pre-booking necessary.  They cannot sell out.\n\n- But if you intend to catch an\nonward high-speed train from Milan or Rome to (let's say) Florence, Naples or Venice, remember that Italian\nhigh-speed trains require reservation and tickets are _only valid on the_\n_specific train you book_.\n\n- Cheap advance-purchase economy or super-economy tickets\nbecome worthless if your flight is late and you miss your train.  Even\na flexible 'base' ticket becomes worthless one hour after departure if you\ncan't get to a Trenitalia ticket counter to change the reservation to a\nlater train.\n\n- So when arriving by air at\nMalpensa or Fiumicino and going to Florence, Naples, Venice and the like,\nyou must choose one of two strategies:\n\n_Option 1, prioritise your_\n_budget:_\nThe money-saving option\nis to buy a cheap Super-Economy fare and commit to a specific train in\nadvance at [www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nor [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/),\nbut allowing _a hefty 3-4 hours_ between flight arrival and train, to allow\nfor any flight delays or long lines at immigration.\n\n_Option 2, prioritise your_\n_time:_\nThe time-saving option\nis to just buy a ticket at the _base_ price when you get to the airport\nstation.  Then you can buy a ticket for the first train leaving after\nyou have landed, collected your bags and are ready to go.  I know this\ngoes against the grain for overseas visitors desperate to reserve every\nStarbucks cappuccino 6 months ahead, but unless it's Christmas Eve or the\nlike, you'll always find places\navailable even right before departure as there are so many seats on so many\ntrains, this shouldn't be a concern.  Or\n\nYou can check what the _base_\nfare is by running an enquiry at [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/),\nthat's what you'd pay at the station on the day.\n\nAlternatively, book in advance at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nand select the Viva fare, this is 100% refundable and changeable before\ndeparture time, and can be cheaper than Trenitalia's Base fare.\n\n_A compromise option?_\nYou may find it cheaper to book cheap advance-purchase tickets on _two_\ntrains - the train you should easily make if your flight is on time and a\nback-up train an hour or two later - than to pay the base fare for one train\nas in the time-saving option above.  Or book one train that you should\nnormally easily be able to make, then just accept that if the plane is\nsignificantly late, you'll have to buy another ticket at the base fare.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n|  | ### Troubleshooting:  The usual mistakes  with Trenitalia.com<br>**1\\. No trains appear**<br>**in the search results** or only 1 or 2 trains at odd times of day. <br>**Reason**:  Data <br>isn't fully loaded yet for that date, either because you're looking at a <br>date more than a few months ahead, or at a date after the twice-annual <br>timetable change, on the 2nd Sunday in June and the 2nd Sunday in <br>December, when they are always late loading the data and the 4 months <br>often slips to 60 days or even less.<br>**Solution**:  <br>Wait till bookings open!<br>**2\\. My train is missing from the search results** <br>even though other trains are shown. <br>**Reason**:  Trenitalia <br>loads trains in blocks, and data may be incomplete.  Some trains - such as <br>Intercity Notte sleepers or some EuroCity trains that go to/from Switzerland - <br>may be loaded last.<br>**3\\. Problem:  It says my**<br>**arrival and/or departure station is invalid.** On<br>Trenitalia.com you need to use Italian place names, such as 'Roma Termini' for Rome, 'Milano <br>Centrale' for <br>Milan, 'Venezia S. Lucia' for Venice, 'Firenze' for Florence.<br>**4\\. Problem:  I**<br>**want a sleeper and it says 'Double seat compartment'.** This is <br>just a poor translation, this does in fact mean a 2-bed sleeper!<br>**5\\. Problem:  Pompeii or Sorrento not shown**.  That's <br>because you need to travel to Naples Centrale with Trenitalia, then <br>switch to a local private railway, the Circumvesuviana,<br>[www.eavsrl.it](https://www.eavsrl.it/). |\n\n## How to  use Trenitalia.com\n\n- The Italian\nRailways website [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/) is well worth getting to know.  It can sell:\n\nDomestic Italian train tickets for any train journey within Italy, including\ncouchettes & sleepers on overnight trains, at cheap prices with no booking fee.\n\nInternational train tickets for most direct international trains\nstarting in Italy heading for Paris, Switzerland, Austria, Germany and Slovenia,\nincluding couchettes & sleepers on overnight trains, again with cheap tickets if you pre-book.\n_Remember that ticket collection for trains to Austria & Germany is only possible at stations in Italy!_\n\n- **Trenitalia have an app** so you can\ncan manage tickets on your phone.\n\nSome overseas visitors find it\n_Not available in your region._ There's an easy workaround for this by\nusing _Settings_ to change your Apple ID region, just Google _How to_\n_change iPhone app store region._\n\n- **Or use Italiarail.com**\n**instead.**  You\nmay find\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\neasier to use than trenitalia.com for sleeper trains, international trains and\npassholder reservations.  ItaliaRail is a US-based agency who connects\ndirectly to the Trenitalia ticketing system to sell Trenitalia tickets, but in\nplain English using English-language place names.  They charge no booking\nfee.\n\n\n### How to use [www.trenitalia.com](https://www.trenitalia.com/)\n\n- **Go to** [**www.trenitalia.com**](https://www.trenitalia.com/)\nand change it to English at top right.\n\n_Tip:_  I prefer using Trenitalia's advanced search page,\n[www.lefrecce.it/Channels.Website.WEB](https://www.lefrecce.it/Channels.Website.WEB).  This is the one to bookmark!\nIt offers more features than their home page journey planner and the input\nfields don't suddenly shift downwards while you're trying to type like they do\nwhen you first open the home page.\n\n\n- **Booking usually opens 4**\n**months before departure**, though this can vary.\nLooking too far ahead is the most common mistake made by overseas visitors\nplanning a big trip many months ahead.  Europeans tend to book train travel\nonly days or weeks ahead, not months, but of course the further ahead you can\nbook the cheaper it can be.\n\n- **Booking may open only 1-2**\n**months ahead** for travel immediately after the two annual timetable changes, on the\n2nd Sunday in June and 2nd Sunday in December.  Trenitalia is always late\nloading the data for a new timetable period, especially for regional, InterCity\nand international trains.  For example, I'm often asked in early May why\nTrenitalia seems to have cancelled all Venice-Trieste trains in late June.\nOf course they haven't, they've simply not got around\nto loading all the data for dates after the mid-June timetable change so no\ntrains are shown!\n\n- **You need to use**\n**Italian-language place names**.  _Firenze_ for Florence, _Roma_\nfor Rome, _Milano_ for Milan, _Venezia_ for Venice, _Torino_\nfor Turin, _Napoli_ for Naples.\n\n- _**Tutti le Stazione**_\nmeans all the stations in that city, so simply select this if\nyou don't know which station is best.  But for the record you want\n_Venezia Santa Lucia_ for central Venice, _Firenze SMN_ for central\nFlorence, _Roma Termini_ is best for central Rome and _Centrale_ (obviously) in most other cities.\nRemember that Sorrento isn't on the mainline network, you book to/from Naples.\n\n- **Warning: Time zone glitch when**\n**setting search time**\n\nWhen you set your desired departure time in the\nsearch parameters, there's a glitch, it sets this in your device's own local\ntime zone.  I'm in the UK and Italy\nis 1 hour ahead, so if I set desired departure time to 07:00, I see trains\nfrom 08:00 onwards.\n\nOnly a minor annoyance for me, as\ntrain times in the search results are correctly shown in local Italian time.\nBut if you were in California and set departure time to 17:00, Italy is 9 hours\nahead and Trenitalia.com would interpret this as 02:00 Italian time and would\nshow early trains the following morning.  I have known people then scroll\ndown to a train leaving at their desired 17:00 time and book it, failing to\nrealise that they are booking a train on the day _after_ the day they want.\nBe aware of this and _always, always, always_ double and triple check the\ndate before you confirm and pay.  Trenitalia will not let you change a\nnon-changeable ticket if you screw up, even though they set you up!\n\n\n- **You can book up to 7 adults at**\n**a time**.  Up to 5 adults on the main page, click _Advanced search_\nand you can select up to 7.  More than this, and you'll either need to split the booking and\nmake creative use of the choose exact seat feature so you all sit together - or book at\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nwhich can book up to 20 people at a time.  Indeed, for any small group it's\nworth checking\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\nas it can be cheaper because of the way it handles multiple passengers.\n\n- **Infants under 4** go free\non all trains, no ticket required, just bring them along.\n\n- **Children** means anyone\nunder 14 on _national_ trains (= Frecciarossa, Frecciargento, Frecciabianca,\nInterCity, ICN), but only under-12s qualify for child fares on _regional_\ntrains.  If you are taking a 12 or 13-year-old on a part-Frecciarossa,\npart-regional journey, split the booking.\n\n- **Choosing your fare**\n\n**Base**\nis the fully-flexible fare, it's what you'd pay at the station on the day.\nRefundable, only valid on the train booked but can be changed before departure,\nor at the station up to an\nhour after departure.  The base fare was originally one fixed-price for a\ngiven journey, but as from late 2017 the base fare for Frecciarossa &\nFrecciargento trains can vary slightly by day of the week or the popularity of\neach particular train.\n\n**Economy & Super-Economy**\nare Trenitalia's cheap advance-purchase fares, only valid on the train booked,\nlimited refunds & changes, limited availability, these are the fares you want\nfor cheap travel, if you are certain what time train you want.\n\n**Cartafreccia Special**\nfares can only be bought if you have a _Cartafreccia_ card, which you probably\nhaven't.\n\n**Ordinaria** is the regular\nfare for regional trains.  It's fixed-price, you can buy at that price even\non the day.  In principle this fare is good for any train, but you now need\nto select a specific departure and check in online for it before boarding,\n[see more about how regional tickets now work](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#regionale).\n\n**Smart & Smart2** are cheap\nadvance-purchase fares for international journeys, feel free to select those.\nNo refunds or changes, specific train only, limited availability, must be bought\nat least a week before departure.\n\n\n- **It doesn't translate**\n**sleeper & couchette types into English**\n\n_Vagone Letto Relax - Tripla Cabina Intera_ = 3-berth sleeper with\nwashbasin, books the whole compartment ( _intera_ = whole).\n\n_Vagone Letto Relax - Doppia Cabina Intera_ = 2-berth sleeper with\nwashbasin, books the whole compartment.\n\n_Vagone Letto Relax Uso Singolo - Cabina Intera_ = Single-berth sleeper\nwith washbasin, books the whole compartment.\n\n_Superior Doppia - Cabina_\n_Intera_ = 2-berth sleeper with shower & toilet, 2-berth compartment, books\nthe whole compartment.\n\n_Superior Uso Singolo - Cabina_\n_Intera_ = Single-berth sleeper with shower & toilet, books the whole\ncompartment.\n\n_Cuccetta Comfort 4 Posti - Promiscuo_ = bunk in shared 4-berth Comfort couchettes, normal mixed sex\ncompartment.\n\n_Cuccetta Comfort 4 Posti - Donna_ = bunk in shared 4-berth Comfort couchettes, in special ladies-only compartment.\n\n_Cuccetta Comfort Tripla - Uso_\n_exclusivo_ = whole couchette compartment booked for sole use by 3 people.\n\n_Cuccetta Comfort Quadrupla -_\n_Cabina intera_ = whole couchette compartment, booked for sole use by 4\npeople.\n\nSo if you want to book 2 people together in a\n2-bed sleeper you should select _Doppia Cabina Intera_ for both\npassengers.\n\nOn some international routes they\nuse slightly different sleeper terminology:\n\n_Cabina A3_ = 3-berth.\n_Cabina A2_ = 2-berth.  _Gran classe_ = deluxe with shower &\ntoilet.\n\nBut again, _Uomo_ means\nmale, _donna_ means female, _intera_ means you want to book a whole\ncompartment.\n\nYou'll see your berth numbers\nbefore payment stage, but before you panic that you're not together in the same\ncompartment, remember\nberth numbers are not consecutive, so for example 21 & 25 are indeed\ntogether in the same compartment,\nwith 22 & 26 next door,\n[see how sleeper & couchette \\\\\nberths \\\\\nare numbered on the sleeper & \\\\\ncouchettes page](https://www.seat61.com/sleepers.htm#sleeper-berth-numbering-system).  [See the \\\\\ndescription of Italian couchette and sleeper types above](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#overnight-trains) for more\ninformation.\n\n- **I recommend using the _Choose seat_ s toggle.**  You'll see a _Choose seats_ toggle at\nthe bottom of the page when selecting a fare for Frecciarossa, Frecciargento, Frecciabianca\nor InterCity trains.  You'll then be able to pick specific seats from a\nnumbered seat map.  It should be obvious, but the grey rectangles separating\nsome seats are the tables between seats which face each other,\n[see the screenshot here](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#Choosing_your_seat).  You cannot\ntell which seats face forwards or backwards (indeed, some trains change direction en route,\nfor example Rome-Venice trains usually reverse at Florence) but in any case if there are\ntwo or more of you I recommend seats facing each other across a table as the\nmost natural and convivial way to travel, and you both then get a window seat.\n\n- **Select _Continue without logging_**\n**_in_** to buy without registering.  You can register if you like - if\nyou don't have an Italian\naddress, tick the _I don't have a tax code_ box and select your country (UK\n= _Regno Unito_, Unites States = _Stati Uniti_).  You can still retrieve your booking later if you need to,\neven if you don't register.\n\n\n- Some people report an error\nmessage about incorrectly entered data if they fill in more than names in the\n'Passengers' section.  The error went away if they filled in just the names\nof the passengers, leaving email and phone number fields in the passengers\nsection blank, but completing the name, email and contact number field in the\n'Contact-Buyer-Required' section.  I cannot replicate this error, so\n[feedback would be appreciated](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm)!\n\n- **How are tickets delivered?**\nOn most Italian domestic trains (but not international ones other than to/from\nSwitzerland) you can select the ticketless option, which means you can simply\nprint out your booking reference or show it on your phone.  Easy!\nAlternatively, the 'self service' option means that tickets can easily be picked\nup from the self-service machines at all main Italian stations, you simply enter\nyour name and booking reference and out come the tickets.  Or they can be\npicked up from the ticket office at any main Italian station.\n\n- **Don't buy international tickets starting in**\n**Germany or Austria using Trenitalia.com!!!!**  It's OK to book\nSwitzerland-Italy trains and Paris or Lyon to Italy trains as these are ticketless.  But most other international tickets booked\nat Trenitalia.com such as tickets from Germany or Austria to Italy need to be collected at an _Italian_ station or posted to\nan _Italian_ address, they _cannot_ be collected at any station\noutside Italy!!!  So even though Trenitalia will happily sell you a ticket\nfrom Vienna to Venice, you can't pick up the tickets in Vienna because (do I\nreally have to spell it out?) Vienna is in Austria and has no Trenitalia staff\nor ticket machines.  And no, they won't accept the booking printout as a\nticket on the train, and no, Austrian Railways staff cannot access a Trenitalia\nbooking and cannot help you!  So you will have wasted you money and will\nneed to buy another ticket.  So don't use trenitalia.com for international\njourneys heading into Italy, unless it's one from Switzerland, or you'll be\nvisiting Italy beforehand, or have an Italian address to which tickets can be\nsent.\n\n- **You can book up to 4 journeys as one transaction**.  Even credit\ncards that work can fail to work if used for a second transaction, so book all\nyour trips together (up to a maximum of 4 journeys), by adding each ticket to\nyour basket and paying for them all together.\n\n- **Time limit:**\nYou must complete your booking(s) within a rather\nannoying time limit, about 15 minutes, otherwise the reservations are\ndropped and you have to start again.  Especially annoying if you are\ntrying to book 4 journeys!\n\n- **Passenger**\n**names:**  Don't worry, unlike airlines, Trenitalia\ndoesn't need passenger names for anyone except the person booking, unless it\nspecifically asks for every passengers' name.  So if it didn't ask, and\nthere's only one name on the booking, that's not a problem.\n\n- **Don't worry**\n**if your seat numbers aren't consecutive!**  The\nsystem does normally book all of the passengers on\none booking in seats next to each other!  For example,\nseats 62 & 68 in a 2nd class car on a Frecciarossa are in fact side by side! And in a 2-bed\nsleeper, berths 21 & 25 are in the same compartment, with 22\n& 26 in the compartment next door!  For seat numbering\nplans for Italian trains,\n[see the \\\\\ntrain seat maps page](https://www.seat61.com/european-train-seat-maps.htm#Italy).\n\n- **Payment problems?**  If your card doesn't\nwork, it may be your\nbank blocking the transaction as possible fraud\nbecause it's a 'foreign' transaction.  Call your bank and ask\nthem to unblock Trenitalia.com for you.  In fact, it can be a good idea to call\nyou bank before using Trenitalia.com.  If Trenitalia.com really won't accept any of your credit cards, buy your Italian\ntrain tickets from\n[www.italiarail.com](https://www.italiarail.com/?aff=S61U)\ninstead.\n\n- **Other problems?**  If\nyou get stuck, you can contact Trenitalia's web team at [areaclienti@trenitalia.it](mailto:areaclienti@trenitalia.it) to unfreeze frozen accounts and so\nforth.  They can handle emails in English.  Or you can\ncall Trenitalia telesales on +11 39 06 6847 5475.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## How to use Trenitalia ticket machines\n\nIt's easy to buy tickets at the\nstation using the self-service\nticket machines at all main Italian stations, as long as you have a credit card\nwith a PIN (4-digit personal identification number).  They have an English\nlanguage facility, and it's faster and easier than using the ticket office.\nThe machines are pretty self-explanatory, but to give you confidence and so you\nknow what to expect, here's how to use them.\n\nIncidentally, you'll sometimes\nfind annoying types who hang around offering to 'help' foreigners, just be\nhealthily suspicious of them and refuse all help, you don't need it as the\nmachines are self-explanatory and you certainly don't need anyone near your\nwallet or pockets while you are distracted.  Italian stations are perfectly\nsafe (I've spent literally hours hanging round them out of professional\ninterest, without any problem whatsoever), but it pays to be streetwise!\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Trains in Italy:  How to use the self-service ticket machines](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine1.jpg) |  | ![Trains in Italy:  Switch the self-service ticket machines to English](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine0.jpg) |\n| There are self-service machines at all main <br>Italian stations, and it's quicker & easier than <br>using the ticket office.  **Touch the UK flag on**<br>**the bottom of the touch-screen for English.** |\n\n### To buy a ticket\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Trains in Italy:  Touch 'buy your ticket' to buy a ticket ](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine2.jpg) |  | ![Choose a destination](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine3.jpg) |\n| **To buy a ticket**, touch 'BUY YOUR TICKET'.  You can also collect <br>pre-booked tickets or change an existing booking, if the ticket type allows <br>changes. |  | _Select a destination_.  By default, the origin is the station you're at, but <br>you can modify this to buy a ticket for any route in Italy. |\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Choose a train from the list](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine4.jpg) |  | ![Choose a class and price](https://www.seat61.com/images/Italy-train-ticket-machine5.jpg) |\n| _Choose a departure from the timetable list_.The system <br>defaults to the immediate departures at the current time, but you can change <br>this to book for later today, tomorrow, whenever. |  | _Choose a class & price, and_<br>_buy_.  Touch _Promo_ to see if there are cheaper fares, but for <br>immediate departure it'll only be the standard _Base_ fare (for mainline <br>trains) or _Ordinaria_ for regional trains.  You probably haven't got <br>a _Cartafreccia_ card, so ignore these fares. |\n\n_Children under 4 go free_\nwith no ticket needed, _children under 12_ can use a child rate ticket on\nregional trains, _children under 14_ can use a child ticket on\nFrecciarossa, Frecciargento, Frecciabianca, InterCity & InterCity Notte mainline\ntrains.\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-italy.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## Guidebooks\n\nPaying for a guidebook may seem an unnecessary\nexpense, but it's only a fraction of what you spend on the whole trip.  If\nyou have a decent guidebook, you see so much more and know so much more about\nwhat you're looking at.  I think the Lonely Planets or Rough Guides are the\nbest ones out there for the independent traveller.\n\n### **[![Amazon logo](https://www.seat61.com/images2/Amazonlogo.gif)](https://www.amazon.co.uk/exec/obidos/redirect-home?tag=themaninseats-21&site=amazon)** Click the images
